# Skotiq sweep — findings

Generated by `tools/production/findings_report.dart` from `reports/coverage/swept`.

- **431** distinct findings across **17** areas (431 raw, 0 duplicates merged)
- **248** independently reproduced by an adversarial verifier; **26** were refuted and are excluded
- **70** units recorded as unreached, with reasons


## P0

### Stack & Share byte-swaps every pixel when 'Apply calibration' is on (the default), so it reports 'Reference frame has only 0 stars' and cannot stack anything

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With 8 accepted 300s L subs whose FITS provably contain 40 well-separated stars each (peak 40614 ADU over a 440 ADU background, sigma 22 ADU -> SNR ~1800), pressing Start in Stack & Share fails instantly with 'Failed to initialize stacker: Reference frame has only 0 stars, need at least 5 for alignment'. Turning OFF the 'Apply calibration' switch and pressing Start again on the SAME frames initialises fine -- the log reads 'Live stacker initialized: 512x512 reference with 34 stars'. The two paths differ only in how the pixels reach the stacker: calibration-off uses the native file loader (stacking_start_from_file), calibration-on loads the frame into Dart via apiReadFitsLinearData and hands back a u16 buffer (stacking_start_from_data). The bridge's image_data_to_linear_f64 decodes the ImageData buffer with u16::from_be_bytes, but fits.rs wrote that buffer with to_le_bytes (as does every other reader in the imaging crate -- processing.rs, calibration.rs, registration.rs, stretch.rs, star_reduction.rs, background_extraction.rs and lib.rs all use from_le_bytes). Every pixel therefore comes back byte-swapped: a 440 ADU background becomes 0xB801 = 47105 and a 40614 ADU star becomes 0xA69E = 42654, so the background spans ~42000-52000 with ~10000 ADU of fake noise and the stars are buried. Hence zero detections. 'Apply calibration' is ON by default, so out of the box Stack & Share fails 100% of the time on 16-bit FITS -- the format every camera writes.

**Expected:** apiReadFitsLinearData must decode ImageData with the same endianness fits.rs encoded it with (little-endian). Stack & Share with the default options must stack the session's frames.

**Repro:** 1. Have a session with 8 accepted 16-bit (BITPIX=16, BZERO=32768) light FITS. 2. Imaging > Stack > Stack & Share. 3. Leave all options at their defaults and press Start -> 'Reference frame has only 0 stars'. 4. Toggle 'Apply calibration' OFF and press Start -> stacker initialises with 34 stars (it then fails later for an unrelated reason, see the residual finding). 5. Verify the frames really have stars: python3 peak-finder over audit_L_017..024.fits reports 40 local maxima each, top fluxes ~37000-40000 over a 440 median with sigma 22.

**Where:** `native/skotiq_native/bridge/src/api/imaging.rs:1537-1560 image_data_to_linear_f64 (from_be_bytes for U16/U32/F32/F64) vs native/skotiq_native/imaging/src/fits.rs:322-365 (to_le_bytes). Reached from packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/stack_and_share_service.dart:724 _loadRawU16 -> stacking_engine_seam.dart:172 apiReadFitsLinearData.`

**Proposed fix:** Change the four decodes in image_data_to_linear_f64 to from_le_bytes, and add a round-trip unit test (write a known FITS, read it back through api_read_fits_linear_data, assert the pixel values match). The same bug silently corrupts two science surfaces: default_science_backend.dart:856 (the Dart fallback for per-frame science quality metrics, which then writes science_frame_quality_metrics / science_tile_metrics) and default_science_backend.dart:890-892 (computeLineRatios reads Ha/OIII/SII this way, so every BPT line ratio is computed on byte-swapped pixels), plus stretch_pipeline_service.dart:34.

### FITS DATE-OBS records the END of the exposure, not the start - off by exactly EXPTIME

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** 30 s exposure: native log 'Starting 30s exposure' at 14:04:37.85, 'Exposure complete' at 14:05:09.05; the FITS says DATE-OBS = '2026-08-01T14:05:09.000Z'. 5 s exposure: start 14:07:56.16, complete 14:08:01.93, DATE-OBS = '2026-08-01T14:08:01.000Z'. In both cases DATE-OBS equals the completion timestamp, i.e. it is late by exactly the exposure duration. The Dart side takes `capturedImage.timestamp` (the download/completion time returned by the bridge) and hands it straight to FitsWriteHeader.captureTimestamp, even though the same function already holds `exposureStartedAt`.

**Expected:** DATE-OBS is the start of the observation by FITS convention (and is what every photometry/occultation/astrometry tool assumes). It should have been 14:04:37 and 14:07:56 respectively.

**Repro:** 1. Imaging, set exposure 30, Snapshot. 2. `grep 'Starting\|Exposure complete' /tmp/ns-audit/imaging-guiding/app.log`. 3. Dump DATE-OBS from the written FITS. 4. Repeat with exposure 5 - the error scales with the duration, proving it is end-of-exposure and not clock skew.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/imaging_service.dart:326 (captureTimestamp = parseUtcTimestamp(capturedImage.timestamp)) -> imaging_service/persistence.dart:51 (captureTimestamp: timestamp) -> native/skotiq_native/bridge/src/api/imaging.rs:3993 (header.set_string("DATE-OBS", &header_data.capture_timestamp))`

**Proposed fix:** Pass `exposureStartedAt` (already in scope at imaging_service.dart) as the FITS captureTimestamp and keep the download time only for captured_images.captured_at. Add DATE-END or EXPEND if the completion time is wanted. Note the sequencer path has the same bug at api/imaging.rs:3383 where capture_timestamp is `chrono::Utc::now()` at header-build time.

### Plan Tonight's own #1 pick cannot be turned into a sequence: "no usable imaging window" for every non-circumpolar target

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With a valid site (40.0N, -75.0W) and an active equipment profile, the Tonight screen's card says "M92 — Peak alt: 84° — Hours up: 5.3 h — Excellent peak altitude (84°) ... 5.3 hours above minimum altitude". Clicking the single primary CTA "Image this target tonight" fails with the red error: 'Target "M92" has no usable imaging window between 2026-08-01T22:01:44.882 and 2026-08-02T04:11:18.515 at min altitude 30°.' The identical failure occurs from Plan Tonight → Recommendation → "Review in Sequencer" for M92 (score 77, the app's top pick) and for M31 (score 63). A circumpolar target (NGC6015, dec +62.3) succeeds and loads 261 frames / 3h59m into the sequencer, which bounds the bug: any target with a finite rise/set at the site is blocked. At lat 40 that is essentially the whole imaging sky (M31, M42, M27, M13, the Veil...).

**Expected:** M92 is above 30° for 308 of the 370 minutes inside that exact window (astropy, 1-min grid, lat 40 lon -75, 2026-08-01). The builder should produce a sequence, or the card must not claim 5.3 usable hours. A screen whose entire purpose is pick -> sequence must not fail on its own default pick.

**Repro:** 1. Settings > Location: lat 40, lon -75, elev 100. 2. Create an equipment profile (sim camera + sim mount, 600mm/120mm/3.76um). 3. Dashboard > "Image tonight" > click "Image this target tonight". 4. Also: Plan Tonight > Recommendation > "Review in Sequencer" on the hero card (M92) and on a searched M31. 5. Search NGC6015 and click "Review in Sequencer" -> succeeds.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/smart_night_service/sequence_emitter.dart:134-155 (_usableTargetWindow) and packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/astronomy/astronomy_calculations.dart:1024 (calculateObjectVisibility anchors its search at DateTime(date.year,date.month,date.day,12))`

**Proposed fix:** calculateObjectVisibility searches a noon-to-noon window anchored on the night MIDPOINT date (target_scoring.dart:404-413 passes nightMid, which is after local midnight), so the rise/set pair it returns describes a 24h day that does not bracket tonight's dark window: for M92 the returned rise is the NEXT day's 12:01, for M31 the returned set is the PREVIOUS afternoon's 14:13. _usableTargetWindow then clips [22:01, 04:11] with those and gets an empty interval. Either anchor calculateObjectVisibility on the interval start (windowStart) rather than the midpoint, or make _usableTargetWindow day-aware: if the target's altitude at intervalStart already exceeds minAltitude, do not clip with riseTime; if it exceeds it at intervalEnd, do not clip with setTime. A cheap regression test: assert buildSingleTargetSequence succeeds for a dec=+41 target at lat=40 on any date.

### All 7 dome and cover-calibrator node types are dead: they always fail with 'No dome connected' even when the device is connected AND assigned to the saved profile

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Ran a sequence of [Open Dome, Close Dome, Park Dome, Park Mount] with the Simulated Dome connected. Open Dome failed 6 times in a row with 'Open Dome failed: No dome connected', each retry logged '[RECOVERY] Open Dome promoted device disconnect to recovery', and the whole run aborted 2s in. The Equipment screen at the same moment showed 'DOME  Simulated Dome  Connected' and the header read '7 connected'. I then saved the dome into the active equipment profile (equipment_profiles.dome_id = 'sim_dome_1', verified by sqlite3) and re-ran: identical failure. Root cause at source: runtime_config_operations.dart:84-91 calls backend.sequencerSetDevices(cameraId, mountId, focuserId, filterwheelId, rotatorId) and there is no dome or cover-calibrator parameter anywhere in the contract (roles/sequencer_backend.dart:92-100). The Rust executor exposes set_dome()/set_cover_calibrator() (executor/setup.rs:149,155) but grep shows no caller outside tests, so ctx.dome_id / ctx.cover_calibrator_id are permanently None and instructions.rs:544,551 always return the failure. This kills OpenDome, CloseDome, ParkDome, OpenCover, CloseCover, CalibratorOn and CalibratorOff — 7 of the 37 node types — on every platform, with or without hardware.

**Expected:** With a connected (or profile-assigned) dome, Open Dome should command the dome. If no dome is available the app should say so before the run (preflight raised no equipment error for these nodes at all).

**Repro:** Equipment > Discovery > connect Simulated Dome (and Edit Profile > Add from connected > Save). Sequencer > Builder > add Open Dome + Close Dome > Start > Start Anyway.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/sequence_executor/runtime_config_operations.dart:84; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/backend/roles/sequencer_backend.dart:92; native/skotiq_native/sequencer/src/executor/setup.rs:149`

**Proposed fix:** Add domeId, coverCalibratorId (and weather/safety/switch) to sequencerSetDevices across the backend roles + FFI, populate them from domeStateProvider/coverCalibratorStateProvider the same way rotatorId is, and call executor.set_dome()/set_cover_calibrator(). Until then, add a preflight ERROR when a sequence contains any dome/cover node.

### 'Integrate now' silently threw away 8 of 9 subs and presented the 1-frame result as a finished master, with no error, warning or count anywhere in the UI

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With 9 accepted 300s subs, clicking 'Integrate now' produced a master card reading 'M51 - L / 1 frame - 00:05:00 integration - L' and no other feedback: no toast, no error banner, no 'x of y subs rejected' line. The database shows integrated_master_frames has 1 accepted row and 8 rows with rejection_reason 'registration failed: too few stars to register: reference=0, frame=N, need >= 3', and integrated_masters.stats_json is {"framesIntegrated":1,"framesRejected":8,...}. Two distinct failures compound: (a) the integrator picked sub 1 as the alignment reference, detected 0 stars in it, and then failed every other sub against that empty reference instead of choosing a different reference or aborting; (b) the count of dropped subs exists in the outcome and in the DB but is never rendered for a manual integration.

**Expected:** Two things. (1) A reference frame with 0 detected stars must not be accepted as the reference -- fall back to the next-best frame (frames 4-9 each detected 29-30 stars) or abort with 'could not find a usable reference frame'. (2) Integration must report its own result: 'Integrated 1 of 9 subs - 8 failed to register' with the reason, exactly as the automatic-integration path already does (auto_integration_service.dart:378 builds precisely that sentence). Losing 89% of a night's data must never be silent.

**Repro:** 1. Seed 10 lights for one session with real FITS star fields (star sigma 1.0-2.25 px). 2. Analytics > History > Audit Night A > sparkles. 3. Workbench > 'Reject HFR above' at 3.5 (leaves 9 accepted). 4. Scroll the right column to Integration settings, click 'Integrate now'. 5. Wait ~40 s. 6. Read the Masters card and look for any error surface.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/session_review/session_review_controller.dart:710 integrate() -- the returned outcome is stored in state.lastOutcome but workbench_view.dart:190 uses it only for a preview path and a button label; framesIntegrated/framesRejected are never rendered. Reference selection is in the native post-session integration path.`

**Proposed fix:** In integrate(), after the outcome returns, if outcome.result.framesRejected > 0 raise a warning toast + a persistent banner on the master card carrying the count and the most common rejection_reason; reuse auto_integration_service.dart's existing summary builder. Separately, make reference selection reject a candidate with fewer than the minimum registrable stars and retry with the next candidate before failing the run.

### The entire companion dashboard - and with it the only editor for the 10 push-notification toggles - cannot be opened in a shipped release APK

`defect` · mobile operation - companion dashboard shell, mount/sequencer/log/settings tabs, session picker and session replay, driven disconnected on the ns_test_api35 emulator · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** MobileDashboardScreen (Devices/Mount/Camera/Sequencer/Science/Log/Settings) mounts only when isCompanionUiEnabled is true. That getter is `bool.fromEnvironment('SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI')` OR `Platform.environment['SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI'] == '1'`. The release workflow builds the APK with a bare `flutter build apk --release --split-per-abi` and passes no such dart-define, and Platform.environment on a non-debuggable Android app cannot be set by a user. So in every published build the flag is permanently false and all seven tabs, plus SessionPickerScreen and SessionReplayScreen (whose ONLY constructor call site in the whole repo is mobile_dashboard_screen.dart:168), are unreachable. That is ~6,700 lines of shipped-but-unopenable UI. The sharpest consequence: settings_tab.dart is the only surface anywhere that writes the ten `mobile.notify.*` SharedPreferences keys, and those keys ARE read and enforced at runtime by mobile_event_notifier.dart:162/325 and mobile_sequence_hooks.dart:478. All ten default to true. So a user is permanently opted in to every push category - meridian flip, safety, guiding lost, battery, per-target completed - with no way to mute any of them, and no way to check the setting when a category is not firing.

**Expected:** Either the release build passes --dart-define=SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI=1, or the notification toggles and session replay are re-hosted on a surface the shared SkotiqApp shell actually routes to. A preference that gates whether the phone pages you at 3am must be user-visible; shipping it enforced-but-uneditable is worse than not shipping it.

**Repro:** 1. `python3 tools/ui_audit/drive_android.py start` on the stock installed APK -> Skip Connection -> bottom nav reads Home/Gear/Imaging/Sequence/Guiding/Plan/More (the shared shell). No Devices/Mount/Camera/Sequencer/Science/Log/Settings tabs exist and there is no route to Session Replay. 2. `adb root; adb shell setprop wrap.com.skotiq.mobile SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI=1`; restart -> Skip Connection -> the seven companion tabs now appear. The only difference is a process env var that a release build cannot receive.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/companion_ui_config.dart:7-14; apps/mobile/lib/main.dart:437; apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/mobile_dashboard_screen.dart:24-27,168; .github/workflows/release.yml:163`

**Proposed fix:** Decide which shell ships. If the shared shell is the product, move the ten notification toggles and the Session Replay entry point into it (they have no equivalent there today) and delete the companion subtree rather than shipping it dark. If the companion shell is meant to be available, add --dart-define=SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI=1 behind an in-app developer setting rather than a build flag, so it is switchable on a release device.


## P1

### Flat wizard cannot capture a flat when a filter move is needed - its wheel move neither takes effect nor is confirmed, while the same move works from Equipment

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Every Quick Capture run that needs a wheel move dies with 'filter wheel did not confirm position 0 within 15s - run stopped.' The native call reports success in the same millisecond - app.log: '[API] api_filterwheel_set_position called: device_id=sim_filterwheel_1, position=0' immediately followed by '[API] Filter wheel position set successfully' - but the Dart filterWheelStateProvider the wizard polls (flat_wizard_provider.dart:1372-1383, waiting for !isMoving && currentPosition == position) never reports the new position, so the wizard fails closed after 15 s. Crucially the wheel itself is fine: issuing the SAME position-0 command from the Equipment screen's filter-wheel card moved it and the card updated to 'L / #1' within ~5 s. So the move works everywhere except from the flat wizard, and after the wizard's two attempts the wheel was still sitting on Ha/#5. The only flat runs that ever reached the exposure stage were the ones where the wheel already happened to be at the target and the move was skipped (line 1346). Zero flats were written across four attempts: /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/flats is empty and flat_history/flat_library have 0 rows.

**Expected:** After a successful filterWheelSetPosition the wheel state must be refreshed (event or poll) so the confirmation loop sees the new position. Failing that, the wizard must say the wheel reported success but never confirmed, and offer to proceed - not just stop.

**Repro:** Connect Simulated Camera + Simulated Filter Wheel. Imaging -> Camera tab -> Flat Wizard -> Quick Capture -> Start Capture -> choose a folder -> Continue. Wait 15 s.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/flat_wizard_provider.dart:1370-1394 (the confirmation loop) and whatever should refresh filterWheelStateProvider after filterWheelSetPosition`

**Proposed fix:** Re-read the wheel status from the backend inside the poll loop instead of relying solely on a pushed event, and emit a position-changed event from the filter wheel driver after a successful set.

### A target named M31 with unset 0,0 coordinates passes validation, and the Slew node confirms in green that it 'Will use target: M31'

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Added a Target node and typed 'M31' into Target Name. RA/Dec stayed at their 0.0000/0.0000 defaults - there is no name resolution anywhere in the target editor. The tree card then rendered 'M31 / RA 00h 00m 00s Dec +00d 00' 00"'. The Slew node's Properties panel showed a green check and 'Will use target: M31 / 00h 00m 00.0s +00d 00' 00"'. 'Frame target' opened a dialog titled 'Frame target - M31' showing the sky at RA 0.0000h Dec 0.000 deg (Pisces, not Andromeda) with no way to search or fix the coordinates. Pre-Flight Validation raised 9 issues and not one of them was about the coordinates; it only complains when RA is outside 0-24 or Dec outside +/-90 (target_rules.dart:15-35). The DB stored raHours 0.0, decDegrees 0.0 with targetName 'M31'. Running this points the mount at a random patch of sky all night.

**Expected:** Either resolve the name to coordinates (the app clearly can: the bundled 'DSLR M31' starter carries RA 00h 42m 44s Dec +41d 16' 08"), or refuse: a target at exactly 0,0 that the user never typed should be a validation ERROR, and the Slew node must not display a green confirmation for it.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > palette 'Target' > + . In Properties set Target Name = M31, leave RA/Dec. Read the tree card and the 'Frame target' dialog. Add a Slew to Target node and read its Properties. Press Start and read the whole Pre-Flight list.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/target_rules.dart:15-35; target editor at packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/target_node_properties.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Add a TargetCoordinatesUnset rule (error) for raHours==0 && decDegrees==0 with a hint pointing at the resolver. Add name resolution to the Target properties panel using the same targets DAO / catalog path the Quick-Start Wizard uses, and gate the Slew node's green 'Will use target' confirmation on the target actually having coordinates.

### Per-event routing claims it will deliver to Email/Telegram/MQTT that the same page knows are unconfigured

`defect` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** In Per-event routing I opened Edit on 'Sequence Paused' and selected the Email (SMTP), Telegram and MQTT transport chips. All three highlighted with a tick, with no warning of any kind. After Save the list row reads '> In-app banner + Email (SMTP) + Telegram + MQTT'. Nothing is configured: no SMTP host, no Telegram bot token, no MQTT broker. The very same page's 'Send test' for email answers 'Failed: Email is not configured' -- so the app HAS the knowledge and does not use it in the routing UI. DB confirms the write: notification_routing_matrix -> "sequencePaused":{"transports":["inApp","email","telegram","mqtt"],...}

**Expected:** Unconfigured transports should be disabled (or chip-labelled 'not configured') in the editor, and the summary line should mark them, e.g. '> In-app banner + Email (not configured)'. A user arming alerts before an unattended night is being told three channels will reach them when none will.

**Repro:** Settings (gear) > NOTIFICATIONS & REMOTE > Notifications > scroll to 'Per-event routing' > 'Sequence Paused' > Edit > click Email (SMTP), Telegram, MQTT > Save. Read the row subtitle. Then scroll further down and press 'Send test' under Email (SMTP).

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/notification_routing_settings.dart:166-168`

**Proposed fix:** Give the routing matrix a per-transport 'configured' predicate (SMTP host non-empty, telegram bot token present in keyring, mqtt broker host non-empty, discord/pushover secrets present) and use it in two places: disable/annotate the chip in _CategoryEditorDialog, and render the summary as '<label> (not configured)' in _categoryRow.

### Every Imaging-screen capture loses its filter: FITS has no FILTER card, filename says "NoFilter", DB filter column is empty

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P0, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With the simulated filter wheel connected and reporting slot 2 = R, I took a 30 s light. The file was written as /tmp/Unknown_NoFilter_2026-08-01_0001.fits, its header contains no FILTER keyword at all, and captured_images.filter is the empty string. I then clicked the Ha chip (log: 'api_filterwheel_set_position called: device_id=sim_filterwheel_1, position=4', wheel moved, chip highlighted Ha) and took a second 5 s light - identical result: Unknown_NoFilter_..._0002.fits, no FILTER card, filter='' in the DB. All 11 frames captured in the session have filter=''. Root cause: the only construction site of FilterWheelSelector (imaging_bottom_banner.dart:552-556) omits the `onFilterSelected` callback, and grep shows ZERO callers anywhere pass it, so nothing ever writes the filter name into exposureSettingsProvider. persistence.dart:57 then reads `filter: exposureSettings.filter`, which is always null.

**Expected:** FILTER = 'Ha' in the FITS header, 'Ha' in the filename, and 'Ha' in captured_images.filter - the wheel is connected and the app already knows and displays the active filter name on the same screen.

**Repro:** 1. Onboard with the Sim backend (camera + filter wheel with L R G B Ha OIII SII). 2. Equipment -> Connect All. 3. Imaging -> Capture tab, exposure 30 -> Snapshot. 4. Click the Ha chip in the bottom banner, Snapshot again. 5. `python3` dump of the FITS header and `sqlite3 skotiq.db 'select file_name,filter from captured_images'`.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/imaging_bottom_banner.dart:552 (FilterWheelSelector built with no onFilterSelected); packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/filter_wheel_selector.dart:32,88-91 (callback declared, never supplied); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/imaging_service/persistence.dart:57`

**Proposed fix:** Pass onFilterSelected from the banner and have it set exposureSettingsProvider.filter (and filterPosition). Better: make persistence.dart read the live filterWheelStateProvider name as the source of truth instead of a UI-mirrored copy, so no future call site can drop it.

### Framing "Add to Sequence -> Add target" silently adds nothing: the Sequencer still shows 0 targets

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P0, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On the Framing screen with M92 resolved (RA 17h17m07s, Dec +43 08' 12", 'Target Resolved' green), I clicked Utilities -> Add to Sequence. A dialog opened: 'Insert "M92" as a new target group. You add the imaging instructions under it in the Sequencer.' with 'New empty sequence' pre-selected. I clicked 'Add target'. The dialog closed with no toast, no error and no navigation. Navigating to Sequencer shows 'New Sequence - 0 targets - 1 node - Sequence 0 steps - Drop instructions here'. The database confirms it: sequences=0, sequence_nodes=0, sequence_versions=0.

**Expected:** A Target node named M92 with its RA/Dec appears in the sequence tree (header should read '1 target'), or the flow reports why it could not be created.

**Repro:** 1. Plan Tonight -> Recommendation -> M92 card -> Send to Framing. 2. Scroll the Framing sidebar to Utilities. 3. Add to Sequence -> keep 'New empty sequence' -> Add target. 4. Nav -> Sequencer. 5. `sqlite3 /tmp/ns-audit/imaging-guiding/data/skotiq.db 'select count(*) from sequences; select count(*) from sequence_nodes;'`

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/framing/widgets/add_target_to_sequence_flow.dart (dialog confirms but the resulting node never reaches the sequencer provider or the DB)`

**Proposed fix:** Await and check the insert result, then either navigate to /sequencer with the new target selected or surface the failure. A confirm dialog that returns silently on both success and failure is exactly how this shipped undetected.

### Loop (live view) writes every frame to disk as a full-size FITS and registers it in the database - ~7.5 MB/s

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I pressed Loop with a 5 s exposure and let it run ~25 s, then pressed Stop. In that window the app wrote 8 new 23,394,240-byte FITS files into the capture folder (plus thumbnails) and inserted 8 rows into captured_images. There is no 'save frames' toggle anywhere in the Loop UI. Extrapolated: framing/focusing with Loop for one hour at 5 s subs writes ~27 GB of junk lights named Unknown_NoFilter_*, all of which land in the user's light-frame folder and in the session integration total.

**Expected:** Loop is a live-view/framing mode - NINA, SGP and SharpCap all discard live-view frames unless you explicitly ask to save. At minimum there should be a visible 'Save loop frames' switch, defaulted off.

**Repro:** 1. Imaging, set Dur 5 s. 2. `ls /tmp/*.fits | wc -l` (baseline 3). 3. Press Loop, wait 25 s, press Stop. 4. `ls /tmp/*.fits | wc -l` -> 11; `du -ch /tmp/*.fits` -> 246 MB; `select count(*) from captured_images` -> 11.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/imaging_screen/imaging_screen_actions.dart (loop path calls the same save-and-persist pipeline as Snapshot)`

**Proposed fix:** Route loop frames through the preview path only (no _saveFitsFile / _saveToDatabase), and add an explicit opt-in toggle next to the Loop button if keeping frames is wanted. Also note the collision-avoidance naming produced 0001_001/0002_001/0003_001 before jumping to 0004 - the sequence counter is not monotonic across app state.

### Framing "GoTo & Frame - Ready" with a PARKED mount and a target 5.7 deg below the horizon; Slew to Target is a silent no-op

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Framing wizard step 4 'GoTo & Frame' displayed a green 'Ready' badge and a filled primary 'Slew to Target' button. At that moment the sidebar's own Coordinates card said 'Alt -5.7 deg / Target below horizon' and the mount was Parked (Imaging -> Mount tab: 'Mount Status STOPPED, RA 00h 00m 0.0s, Dec +00 00' 0.0", Status Parked'). I clicked Slew to Target three times, and once through the split-button menu item 'Slew'. Nothing happened: no dialog, no toast, no error banner, zero new lines in app.log, and the mount was still Parked at RA 00h00m/Dec +00 afterwards.

**Expected:** Either the step reports 'Not ready - mount is parked' and disables the button, or clicking it says 'Mount is parked - unpark first' / 'Target is 5.7 deg below the horizon - slew anyway?'. A green Ready plus a primary button that does nothing is the worst of both.

**Repro:** 1. Connect sim mount (it comes up Parked). 2. Plan Tonight -> M92 -> Send to Framing. 3. Scroll the sidebar to step 4. 4. Click 'Slew to Target'. 5. `tail` app.log (no new lines) and check Imaging -> Mount tab (still Parked, RA 00h00m).

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/framing/widgets/framing_sidebar/controls_and_status.dart (step-4 readiness) and packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/slew_dropdown_button.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Derive step-4 readiness from mountState.isParked && !isTracking && target altitude, and make the slew action surface its own failure via a snackbar. Also log the refusal - a completely silent log is why this looks like a dead button rather than a safety interlock.

### Paired devices live in ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db -- outside the app database, outside the configured data dir, and split across two stores

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I launched a completely fresh scratch profile (SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR=SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ns-audit/r-settings-system/data, --fresh, empty skotiq.db). Settings > Remote Access > Manage Pairing immediately listed 12 devices paired 6/15/2026 and 7/20/2026 -- the developer's real, months-old pairings. They are not in skotiq.db; PairingDatabase opens getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/Skotiq/pairing.db, i.e. ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db, which ignores SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR entirely. Two such stores exist on this machine: ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db (12 active rows, mtime Aug 1) and ~/Skotiq/pairing.db (0 rows, mtime Jul 29) -- the same install has written to two different locations depending on how getApplicationDocumentsDirectory resolved.

**Expected:** Long-lived remote credentials belong in the app database (or at minimum under the configured data dir) so that (a) Backup & Restore captures them, (b) moving the data directory moves them, (c) they cannot silently fork into two stores. As shipped, restoring a backup on a new machine silently loses every pairing, and a launch context where XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR differs presents an empty device list with no error.

**Repro:** drive_linux.py start --profile <new> --fresh (scratch data dir) > Settings > Remote Access > Manage Pairing. Observe 12 devices. sqlite3 /tmp/ns-audit/<profile>/data/skotiq.db '.tables' -> no pairing tables. sqlite3 ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db 'select count(*) from paired_devices where is_active=1' -> 12, matching the UI exactly. ls ~/Skotiq/pairing.db -> a second, empty store.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_remote_protocol/lib/src/database/pairing_database.dart:401-410 (_openConnection -> getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/Skotiq/pairing.db)`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the pairing DB through the same directory resolver the main database uses (SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR-aware application-support dir), and migrate any existing Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db on first run. Include it in the backup manifest.

### Dashboard SAFETY card shows a green "Safe" badge while reporting it has no data, at the same moment an app-wide red "Weather Critical" banner is up

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Dashboard right rail: SAFETY panel renders a green check badge reading "Safe", with body rows 'Data: Unavailable', 'As of 10:58:33', an amber inline alert 'No weather data sources available' and the line 'Weather safety is off — conditions are not being checked'. In the same session the app-wide banner reads 'Weather Critical / Weather safety is off — conditions are not being checked', and the Weather screen reports 'Cloud Cover 93% Overcast' with a Critical card 'Heavy cloud cover overhead (93% density) - consider pausing'. So one surface says Safe (green), one says Critical (red), about the same subject at the same time, and the Safe verdict is asserted from admittedly absent data.

**Expected:** A safety verdict must never be green when the monitor is off or the data is unavailable. Show 'Not monitored' / 'Unknown' in a neutral or amber state. And the app-wide banner should not use the word 'Critical' for a configuration state (safety disabled) — that is the same word used for the genuine 93%-cloud alert, which trains the user to ignore both.

**Repro:** 1. Set a location, connect the sim camera+mount profile, do not connect a weather/safety device. 2. Dashboard -> read the SAFETY card. 3. Weather -> scroll to the Critical card and Safety Status card. 4. Compare with the persistent top banner.

**Where:** `Dashboard safety card (rendered on /dashboard right rail); weather banner severity is driven by the disabled state of Settings > Automation & Safety > Weather Safety > 'Enable weather safety' (default off, screenshot planning-90)`

**Proposed fix:** Make the badge tri-state: Safe (monitored and safe) / Unsafe / Not monitored (grey). Never map 'monitoring disabled' or 'no data' onto Safe. Downgrade the persistent banner for the disabled-monitor case from Critical to an informational one-time notice with a 'Turn on weather safety' action, and reserve Critical for an actual unsafe reading.

### Opening Edit Profile and pressing Save silently destroys the reducer and rewrites focal length / image scale by 25%

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P0, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The onboarding wizard let me set telescope FL 550 mm, aperture 100 mm and a 0.80x reducer; it correctly computed and stored effective FL 440.0 mm, f/4.4, image scale 1.76 arcsec/px. Equipment > Edit Profile has NO reducer/barlow field at all -- its Optical Train section only has Telescope/OTA, Focal Length and Aperture, and it displayed 'f/5.5' and 'Scale 1.41"/px at 3.76um'. I changed nothing and clicked 'Save Changes'. The DB went from focal_length=440.0, focal_ratio=4.4 to focal_length=550.0, focal_ratio=5.5. The reducer is now unrecoverable: no field anywhere in Edit Profile accepts it (AT-SPI tree of the whole dialog contains no 'Reducer' or 'Barlow' node), so the only place it can ever be entered is the one-time first-run wizard.

**Expected:** Edit Profile should expose the reducer/barlow factor and preserve it, and the computed f/ratio and image scale should match what the profile actually stores (f/4.4, 1.76 arcsec/px). A no-op open+save must never mutate the profile.

**Repro:** 1. Complete onboarding with telescope library 'Sky-Watcher Esprit 100ED' (550mm/100mm), reducer 0.80, pixel size 3.76um. Review screen shows 'Focal length 550.0 mm x 0.80', 'Image scale 1.76 arcsec/px'. 2. sqlite3 <db> 'select focal_length,focal_ratio from equipment_profiles' -> 440.0 | 4.4. 3. Equipment > Edit Profile > scroll to Optical Train: shows f/5.5 and Scale 1.41"/px. 4. Click 'Save Changes' without editing anything. 5. Re-query: 550.0 | 5.5.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/profile_editor_dialog.dart (Optical Train section)`

**Proposed fix:** Add a Reducer/Barlow factor field to the Edit Profile Optical Train section, seeded from the profile; compute effective focal length = telescope_focal_length * reducer and store that in focal_length exactly as the wizard does. Until then, at minimum make Edit Profile round-trip focal_length rather than recomputing it from telescope_focal_length. A 25% image-scale error breaks plate-solve scale hints, framing FOV and guider px->arcsec calibration.

### INDI 'Test Connection' reports a green 'Connected' against a host that does not exist

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Equipment > Connection > INDI Server Address > Configure > Test Connection returned a green tick and 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' with nothing listening on localhost:7624 (verified: ss shows no listener, /dev/tcp connect returns 'Connection refused', no indiserver process). I then changed the host to 192.0.2.77 (RFC 5737 TEST-NET-1, guaranteed unroutable) and pressed Test again: after a real ~30s attempt it again reported the green 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' The same app's PHD2 test on the very same screen correctly reports 'No response on localhost:4400. Is PHD2 running with "Enable Server" turned on?', so this is not a platform limitation.

**Expected:** 'Test Connection' must report failure when the TCP connect fails, e.g. 'No response on 192.0.2.77:7624'. Reporting success is worse than no test at all: a user with a Raspberry Pi INDI server who typos the address gets a green tick and then spends the night hunting the wrong problem.

**Repro:** 1. Ensure no indiserver is running. 2. Settings > Equipment > Connection > 'INDI Server Address' > Configure. 3. Click 'Test Connection' with localhost:7624 -> green 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' 4. Replace host with 192.0.2.77, click Test Connection -> same green success.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/indi_server_dialog.dart (test handler) and its bridge call`

**Proposed fix:** Treat a failed TCP connect / handshake timeout as failure and surface the socket error, mirroring the PHD2 test's wording. Distinguish 'connected, 0 devices' (server up, no drivers loaded) from 'could not reach host'.

### 'Wait for Time' with no time picked returns Success instantly — a silent no-op with no validation warning

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Added a Wait for Time node and left 'Wait Until' at its default empty state ('Select time...'). Pre-flight raised no issue for it. At runtime the log shows 'Executing child 3/3: Wait for Time' immediately followed by 'Child Wait for Time completed with status: Success' in the same millisecond (17:10:43.766494 -> 17:10:43.766505). No wait happened. Confirmed at source: rules/timing_rules.dart:17-18 does `if (waitUntil == null) continue;` so an unconfigured node produces no ValidationIssue at all.

**Expected:** Either a preflight ERROR ('Wait for Time has no time set') or a runtime failure. The single most common use of this node is 'wait until astronomical dark before imaging'; silently skipping it starts the run in daylight.

**Repro:** New sequence > add 'Wait for Time' > do not touch Wait Until > Start > Start Anyway. Node completes instantly.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/timing_rules.dart:17`

**Proposed fix:** Emit a ValidationSeverity.error when WaitTimeNode.waitUntil == null (and when a Loop's repeatUntil is null under the RepeatUntil condition), and make the runtime fail rather than succeed.

### Run Script shows 'Timeout 300 s' but stores null, so the node refuses to run: 'Script timeout_secs is required in fail-closed mode'

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Added a Run Script node, set only the Script Path to a working /tmp script, left the Timeout field showing 300 (rendered as a real value, not grey placeholder). Run failed at 16:28:21 with 'Run Script failed: Script timeout_secs is required in fail-closed mode' and the script never executed (target file absent). I then typed 60 into the same Timeout field and re-ran: the script executed and the node returned Success. So a Run Script node is unusable out of the box, and the panel states a timeout that is not stored. Three different numbers are shown for the same unset field on one screen: the editor prints `node.timeoutSecs ?? 300` (_misc_properties.dart:52), the Timing card right below it prints `${script.timeoutSecs ?? 30}s` (node_timing_section.dart:588), and the duration estimator uses `?? 60` (sequence_time_estimator.dart:189).

**Expected:** Either the editor writes its displayed default into the node on creation, or the field shows an empty/placeholder state and preflight errors on it. All three surfaces must agree.

**Repro:** New sequence > add Run Script > set Script Path only > Start > Start Anyway. Fails. Type any number into Timeout > re-run. Succeeds.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/node_properties_panel_parts/_misc_properties.dart:52; packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/node_timing_section.dart:588; native/skotiq_native/sequencer/src/instructions.rs:6329`

**Proposed fix:** Give ScriptNode a non-null default timeoutSecs (300) in the constructor, or have the palette factory seed it. Delete the two divergent `?? 30` / `?? 60` fallbacks.

### The Notification example template the UI itself suggests (${frame}) always fails and aborts the entire run, and the reason is thrown away

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Title field's own hint is `e.g. Frame ${frame} of ${target.name} done` (_capture_rich.dart:347). I used `AUDIT NOTIF ${frame}` as the title. At runtime: 'ERROR Notification title `AUDIT NOTIF ${frame}` failed interpolation: Variable `${frame}` cannot be resolved: frame number is only set inside an exposure burst' — the node returned Failure and the whole sequence aborted, so the Park Mount node after it never ran and the mount was left unparked and tracking. A Notification instruction can never be 'inside an exposure burst', so the suggested example is guaranteed to fail. Worse, the Session Report's Errors section said only 'Sequence failed' with no cause, and sequence_runs.stats_json recorded errorMessages:["Sequence failed"] — the specific interpolation error exists only in the log. (By contrast Open Cover / Open Dome / Start Guiding failures DO propagate their real text, so this is a Notification-specific loss.)

**Expected:** Either ${frame} resolves to something sane outside a burst, or the hint text does not advertise a variable this node cannot use. A template typo should not abort the run and strand the mount; and the run record should carry the real error text.

**Repro:** Add Notification, set Title to `AUDIT NOTIF ${frame}`, add Park Mount after it, Start. Run fails at the Notification; Park never runs.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/node_properties_panel_parts/_capture_rich.dart:347; native/skotiq_native/sequencer/src/expressions/resolver.rs:261`

**Proposed fix:** Change the hint to variables valid in this scope; make interpolation failure a warning that sends the raw title rather than a run-aborting error; propagate the instruction's failure message into run stats like every other node does.

### The daylight gate blocks light frames only when a Target node is present — a targetless Take Exposures wrote 3 daylight LIGHT frames

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** At 16:19 (Sun ~55 deg altitude) a sequence with no TargetHeader ran Take Exposures 3 x 2 s and wrote 3 IMAGETYP='Light' FITS files to disk with no gate at all. At 17:02, with the identical camera and sun position but the exposures under a TargetHeader, the runtime refused: 'Daylight gate: refusing light-frame exposure — Sun altitude 55.4 deg is above the maximum -12.0 deg for on-sky light imaging.' Slew is gated the same way. So forgetting to add a Target node silently removes the daylight protection for the exposures themselves.

**Expected:** The daylight gate should key off frame type (light), not off whether a target group happens to exist.

**Repro:** Sequence A: Take Exposures only (no Target) -> captures in daylight. Sequence B: Target > Smart Exposure -> refused by daylight gate.

**Where:** `native/skotiq_native/sequencer/src/instructions.rs (daylight gate in the exposure path)`

### Closing the First Light dialog mid-capture orphans the exposure, and there is no cancel inside the dialog

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** While the dialog showed 'Working on it... / Capturing exposure' with an indeterminate bar, it offered NO cancel or abort - only the title-bar X. Clicking the X closed the dialog but did NOT stop the exposure: navigating to Imaging showed 'Exposing... 999858.8s remaining' with the camera locked. The Imaging viewer's 'Abort capture' X was then clicked three times (tooltip 'Abort capture' confirmed showing, button highlighted) over ~90 seconds and the countdown kept running (999828.7 -> 999816.7 -> 999800.7 -> 999766.7); no abort request appears anywhere in the native log. The camera stayed unusable.

**Expected:** Dismissing a modal that owns a device operation must either abort the operation or refuse to close while it is in flight. And the working state needs a Cancel button of its own - a progress dialog with only an X, where the X does not cancel, is the shape that costs people a night.

**Repro:** Settings > Help & Tutorials > 'Capture your first light' > Start > set exposure to 999999 > Start capture > wait for 'Working on it...' > click the X in the dialog title bar > sidebar Imaging. Observe the running countdown, then click the X at the right of the image toolbar (tooltip 'Abort capture') repeatedly.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/first_light/first_light_flow_dialog.dart (no onDismiss abort, no cancel action in the running state). The dead 'Abort capture' is in the imaging viewer toolbar and does not own the first-light capture path.`

**Proposed fix:** Give the running state a Cancel button that calls the camera abort, and make the dialog's dismiss handler abort as well (or set barrierDismissible/canPop false while a capture is in flight). Separately, route the imaging viewer's abort at the camera rather than at the imaging capture controller so it can stop any in-flight exposure.

### Files & Storage says 'Last backup: Never' while Settings > Advanced > Backup & Restore says 'Last Full Backup: Aug 2, 2026 10:30' — same app, same second, same fact

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The scratch instance ran its startup backup check at 10:27:45 local: three .nsbackup files were written and app_settings got autosave.last_backup_at = 2026-08-02T14:27:45.635595Z. I then opened Settings > Files & Storage at 10:28 and scrolled to Status. At 10:30:02 the card still read 'Last backup / Never' with the trailing status 'Pending'. Clicking 'Back Up Now' immediately corrected it to 'Last backup / 0s ago' and 'In 23h', so the widget can render a real value — it just never picks up the backup the app itself performed. STRONGER REPRO: I then restarted the app (same profile, same DB). With autosave.last_backup_at = 2026-08-02T14:30:25.957100Z persisted and four .nsbackup files on disk, a freshly opened Settings > Files & Storage again reads 'Last backup / Never' and 'Pending'. So the persisted timestamp is never hydrated into the card at all; the only thing that makes it truthful is pressing Back Up Now in the current session, and that truth is lost on the next launch. DIRECT CONTRADICTION BETWEEN TWO SURFACES: in the same session and within seconds of each other, Settings > ADVANCED > Backup & Restore > Auto-Save Status reads 'Last Full Backup: Aug 2, 2026 10:30' and lists the real files under Recent Backups, while Settings > GENERAL > Files & Storage > Status reads 'Last backup / Never' and 'Pending'. Backup & Restore hydrates from the persisted key correctly; Files & Storage does not.

**Expected:** The Status card must reflect the persisted autosave.last_backup_at (and any backup completed in this session). 'Never' is a false statement about the user's data-protection state: the natural reaction is to assume backups are broken, or to conclude they are working when they are not.

**Repro:** Press 'Back Up Now' (card correctly shows '7m ago' / 'In 23h'), then stop and relaunch the app with the same profile and reopen Settings > Files & Storage: the card is back to 'Never' / 'Pending' while sqlite3 app_settings still holds autosave.last_backup_at and the .nsbackup files are still on disk.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/auto_save_service.dart:149-166 (start(lastBackup) hydration) and packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/auto_save_settings.dart (Status card)`

**Proposed fix:** Make Files & Storage read the same source Backup & Restore already reads (autoSaveStatusProvider, which seeds from the persisted autosave.last_backup_at) instead of the raw service status.

### INDI 'Test Connection' reports a green 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' for an address nothing can connect to

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Connection > INDI Server Address > Configure. I set the host to 192.0.2.1 (RFC 5737 TEST-NET-1, guaranteed unroutable) port 7624 and pressed Test Connection: after ~6 s the dialog showed a green check and 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' I repeated with 127.0.0.1 port 9 (discard port, connection refused instantly): same green 'Connected. Found 0 devices.' A raw TCP probe of 192.0.2.1:7624 from the same machine hangs until killed, and nothing listens on 127.0.0.1:9. Nothing was written to app.log for either attempt (grep for 'address discovery failed' / '192.0.2' -> no hits).

**Expected:** Test Connection must distinguish 'reached an INDI server that reports no drivers' from 'could not reach anything'. On failure it should say so, and name the failure (timed out / connection refused / not an INDI server). The whole point of the button is to tell the operator, before dark, whether the address is right.

**Repro:** Settings > Connection > INDI Server Address > Configure > set host 192.0.2.1, port 7624 > Test Connection. Then host 127.0.0.1, port 9 > Test Connection.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/dialogs/indi_server_dialog.dart:106-137; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/backend/ffi_backend/discovery_camera_operations.dart:86-102`

**Proposed fix:** Give the probe a result type that separates transport failure from an empty device list (or let _discoverAddressDevices rethrow for the explicit-address path, keeping the swallow only for background scans). Say 'No INDI server answered at <host>:<port>' on failure and 'Connected, but the server reports no drivers' on a genuine empty list.

### Auto-Save & Backups renders compiled-in defaults instead of the saved settings, and changing any one field silently overwrites the other four

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Before restarting, I set 'Enable sequence auto-save' ON (autosave.sequence_enabled=true) and 'Maximum backups' to 50 (autosave.max_backups=50), both confirmed in sqlite3. After an app restart with the same profile, Settings > Files & Storage > Auto-Save & Backups renders the DEFAULTS: 'Enable sequence auto-save' OFF, 'Maximum backups' 7, 'Last backup' Never — while the database still held sequence_enabled=true, max_backups=50, last_backup_at=2026-08-02T14:30:25Z. I then changed only 'Backup interval' from 24 to 12. sqlite3 immediately after: backup_interval_hours=12 (correct), but autosave.sequence_enabled had flipped true->false and autosave.max_backups 50->7. One edit to an unrelated field destroyed two saved settings, including turning a data-protection feature OFF, with no warning.

**Expected:** The page must render the persisted config, and an edit must be a partial update, not a full rewrite from stale in-memory state. As shipped, a user who configures this page loses it on the next launch and gets it silently reverted the moment they touch anything.

**Repro:** Settings > Files & Storage: turn 'Enable sequence auto-save' ON, set 'Maximum backups' 50. Confirm with sqlite3. Stop and relaunch the app. Reopen the page: switch shows OFF, Maximum backups shows 7. Change 'Backup interval' to 12. sqlite3: sequence_enabled=false, max_backups=7.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/auto_save_settings.dart:178-180 watches `autoSaveServiceProvider` (the raw service) rather than `autoSaveLifecycleProvider`, which is the provider that loads the persisted config (packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/auto_save_service.dart:783-810). _updateConfig at auto_save_settings.dart:68-84 does `service.config.copyWith(...)` and `_persistAutoSaveConfig` (auto_save_service.dart:854-861) writes all five autosave.* keys, so the stale defaults are written back over the saved values.`

**Proposed fix:** Have the page depend on autoSaveLifecycleProvider (await hydration, show a loading state) and make the persist step write only the changed keys.

### One failed 'Connect to Server' attempt drops the app out of Local mode permanently — every screen gets an undismissable red 'Error: not connected to server' banner and there is no way back without a restart

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The app was running standalone: Settings > Connection reported 'Connection Status / Local' with a 'Local Mode' badge. I opened Connect to Server, left the prefilled localhost:8080 (nothing listening) and pressed Connect. It correctly reported 'Connection failed: Connection to http://localhost:8080 did not complete (state=disconnected)'. But after dismissing the dialog the app did NOT return to Local: Settings > Connection now reads 'Server Status / Not connected' and 'Connection Status / Disconnected', and a full-width red banner 'Error: not connected to server' is pinned under the title bar on EVERY screen (verified on Dashboard, Settings and Equipment). The banner has no close button, and the title-bar connection popover just says 'Connection Status / Not connected to a server' with no action. Nothing on the Connection page offers 'use local' or 'disconnect' — the only control is 'Connect' again.

**Expected:** A failed attempt to reach an OPTIONAL remote host must leave the local backend intact, or must offer an obvious 'Continue locally'. Standing up a scary red error bar on a machine that is working perfectly well locally is the cry-wolf pattern: the next time that bar means something, nobody will read it.

**Repro:** Fresh desktop profile, no pairing. Settings > Connection > Connect to Server > Connect (defaults localhost:8080). Dismiss the dialog. Look at the banner on any screen, and reopen Settings > Connection.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart:653-671; connection/backend swap in packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/connection_settings.dart (Connect to Server dialog)`

**Proposed fix:** On a failed connect, roll the backend back to the local one instead of leaving DisconnectedBackend installed; add a 'Work locally' action to both the Connection page and the banner.

### Captured frames are written to the DB with NO gain, offset, sensor temperature, mount pointing, focuser position or target link - although the FITS file it just wrote has all of them

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Run 1 captured 4 lights. The FITS header on disk is complete: GAIN=100, OFFSET=10, CCD-TEMP=15.0, SET-TEMP=15.0, RA=0.0/DEC=0.0, OBJCTRA/OBJCTDEC, FOCUSPOS=25074, FOCTEMP=20.0, SITELAT=30.0, SITELONG=82.0, OBJECT='New Target'. The matching captured_images rows have gain=NULL, offset=NULL, sensor_temp=NULL, cooler_power=NULL, mount_ra=NULL, mount_dec=NULL, mount_altitude=NULL, mount_azimuth=NULL, pier_side=NULL, focuser_position=NULL, focuser_temp=NULL, rotator_angle=NULL and target_id=NULL. Only hfr, star_count, eccentricity, filter, exposure_duration and binning were persisted.

**Expected:** Every value the capture pipeline already knows (it wrote them into the FITS header microseconds earlier) should land in captured_images. Everything downstream reads the DB, not the file: the dark library matches on gain/offset/temp/duration/binning, analytics plots temperature and HFR, project tracking counts frames per target.

**Repro:** Run any sequence with Cool Camera + Take Exposures against the simulators, then: sqlite3 <profile>/data/skotiq.db 'select gain,offset,sensor_temp,mount_ra,focuser_position,target_id from captured_images;' and compare with the FITS header of the same file.

**Where:** `Sequencer run -> captured_images persistence`

**Proposed fix:** Populate captured_images from the same metadata struct used to build the FITS header, in one place, so the two cannot diverge. Add a regression test asserting the DB row and the header agree field for field.

### Light frames are written with NO pointing at all - no RA/DEC/OBJCTRA/OBJECT card and NULL mount_ra/mount_dec - after a successful slew to explicit coordinates

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Run 4 slewed the mount to RA 17.0000h Dec +30.0000 ('Slewing mount sim_mount_1 to RA=17.0000h Dec=30.0000', Slew completed Success at 14:57:13) and then captured 4 lights at 14:58:02-14:58:14. The FITS headers of those frames contain SIMPLE/BITPIX/NAXIS/EXPTIME/DATE-OBS/IMAGETYP/GAIN/OFFSET/CCD-TEMP/SET-TEMP/XBINNING/YBINNING/SITELAT/SITELONG/FOCUSPOS/FOCTEMP/NS-SESID/NS-FIDX/NS-NPLN - and nothing else. There is no RA, DEC, OBJCTRA, OBJCTDEC, OBJECT or FILTER card. captured_images.mount_ra / mount_dec / target_id are NULL for the same four rows. So after the run there is no record anywhere of where the telescope was pointed. (Frames taken under a Target node in run 1 did get RA/DEC cards, so the writer only emits pointing when a Target group exists, not when the mount is simply on target.)

**Expected:** Every light frame carries the mount's actual RA/DEC at exposure start, regardless of whether the coordinates came from a Target group or from a Slew node's custom coordinates. This is basic FITS hygiene: stacking software, plate-solve verification and the app's own project tracking all key off it, and a sequence built around Slew-with-custom-coordinates (the flow the app itself pushes you to when it warns 'No target group found in sequence') silently produces unlabelled data.

**Repro:** Builder: Cool Camera, Unpark, Slew to Target with Use Target Coordinates OFF and RA 17.0 / Dec 30.0, Autofocus, Take Exposures 4x3s, Park, Warm. Run to completion. Then dump the FITS header of any captured frame and select mount_ra,mount_dec from captured_images.

**Where:** `Sequencer run -> FITS writer / captured_images`

**Proposed fix:** Read the mount's current RA/DEC from the device at exposure start and always write RA/DEC (plus OBJCTRA/OBJCTDEC) and persist them; fall back to the target group only for the OBJECT name.

### The four Session charts are computed over three different frame populations; the guiding chart includes frames the app rejected

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** For seeded session 1 (12 light frames, 10 accepted + 2 rejected with rms 4.2", plus 4 darks) the four charts on one card report n=10 (Image quality), n=12 (Guiding performance), n=12 (Focus drift) and n=16 (Sensor temperature). The guiding headline therefore reads median 0.8 / p90 3.9 / range 0.5-4.2 and the 1" warning line is breached, when every accepted frame guided between 0.50" and 0.95". The temperature chart silently mixes in the 4 dark frames.

**Expected:** All four charts on the session card should plot the same, stated population. HfrChart filters isAccepted; the other three do not, and none filters frameType==light. A p90 of 3.9" for a session whose accepted-frame p90 is 0.905" is a wrong number a user would act on (they would go hunting for a guiding fault that does not exist).

**Repro:** Seed session 1 with 10 accepted lights (rms .50-.95), 2 rejected lights (rms 4.2, hfr 9.9, focuser 15100) and 4 darks (sensor_temp only). Restart. Analytics > Session > Reviewing > Night A - M31. Compare the n= counts in the four chart headers.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/session_chart.dart:375 (HfrChart filters isAccepted), :400 TemperatureChart, :425 GuidingRmsChart, :453 FocuserPositionChart (no filter at all)`

**Proposed fix:** Filter one population in _SessionTab.chartGrid (accepted lights) and pass it to all four charts, or give every chart the same `.where((i)=>i.isAccepted && i.frameType==light)` predicate. If rejected frames are wanted, draw them as a distinct marker series and say so in the header.

### Exported FWHM is fabricated from HFR with the wrong factor (2.35x instead of 2x), overstating FWHM by 18%

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** History > Night A - M31 > Export CSV wrote /home/scdouglas/Documents/Skotiq/exports/Night A - M31_2026-08-01T14-40-26.csv with a populated "FWHM (px)" column - 4.70, 5.17, 5.64 - even though captured_images.fwhm is NULL for every one of those rows. The values are HFR * 2.35.

**Expected:** For a Gaussian PSF the half-flux radius is 1.1774*sigma and FWHM = 2.3548*sigma, so FWHM = 2.00 * HFR. Multiplying HFR by 2.3548 treats HFR as sigma and inflates every reported FWHM by 17.7% (HFR 2.00 px -> 4.70" reported vs 4.00" true). Worse, the real `fwhm` column measured by the pipeline is ignored and overwritten by the derived number, and nothing in the CSV says the value is derived.

**Repro:** Analytics > History > click Night A - M31 > click the spreadsheet icon; open the CSV. HFR column x 2.35 == FWHM column exactly.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/session_export_service.dart:69-70 and :171; same factor in session_report_service.dart:301 (meanFwhm), capture_preview_loader.dart:180 (live capture FWHM readout), session_report.dart:36`

**Proposed fix:** Use image.fwhm when present; otherwise derive FWHM = 2.0 * HFR and label the column "FWHM (px, derived from HFR)". Fix the constant in all four call sites - the live capture readout is showing the same inflated number during a run.

### Insights invents a guiding baseline of 0.1 arcsec when there is no history, so every user with <=5 sessions is told their guiding degraded 7x

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Science tab Insights panel showed 'Guiding degradation - Recent guiding RMS is 7.20x the historical baseline. Check balance, flexure, and backlash.' and 'Focus quality drift - Recent median HFR is elevated versus the longer-term baseline.' The five seeded sessions have avgGuidingRms 0.60/0.55/0.70/1.10/0.65 (mean 0.72) and there is NO older session at all - guiding never degraded, and the newest session (0.55) is the best of the five.

**Expected:** With no baseline sessions, no baseline claim. The panel should stay silent (or say 'not enough history') instead of asserting a 7.2x regression and deducting 32 points from the equipment health score.

**Repro:** Seed exactly 5 imaging_sessions with avg_guiding_rms 0.65/1.10/0.70/0.55/0.60 and avg_hfr 2.90/4.20/3.00/2.50/2.20. Restart. Analytics > Science > scroll to Insights.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/equipment_health_service.dart:200 (baseline = skip(5).take(10)), :205-213 (.clamp(0.1,100.0) turns 'no data' into 0.1), :222 and :234 (the two triggers), _mean at :324`

**Proposed fix:** Make the baselines nullable: if the baseline window is empty, skip both insights entirely and do not deduct score. The clamp exists to avoid divide-by-zero; the real fix is to not divide when there is nothing to divide by.

### Stored photometry for a past session is invisible: the light curve is filtered by the CURRENT global target selection, so reviewing any other night shows "has no data yet"

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science with 'Night D - V-Test' selected (120 frames, 480 photometry_measurements rows of which 120 are role='target', object_id='V-TEST', differential_magnitude sweeping -0.30..+0.30) renders 'Differential Photometry has no data yet', Period Analysis 'Need at least 10 photometry points (0 available)', no 'Export to AAVSO' button, and the Science guide ladder stuck on rung 2 'Track a star'.

**Expected:** Reviewing a stored session should plot the photometry that session actually recorded. The DB holds a complete 120-point light curve for it.

**Repro:** Proved by A/B in the running app. sessionLightCurveProvider filters photometry rows to objectId == activePhotometryTargetObjectIdProvider, which is `selection?.target?.objectId ?? 'target_primary'` - a single GLOBAL live selection, not a per-session lookup. I inserted 20 rows into session 5 identical in every way except object_id='target_primary'; after restart the Differential Photometry chart for Night E drew them (dMag axis -0.40..0.40, x 0s..1h35m) and the AAVSO export button appeared, while Night D's 120 real points stayed invisible.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/science_provider/session_products.dart:299-325 (sessionLightCurveProvider) and photometry_selection.dart:203-206 (activePhotometryTargetObjectIdProvider); write side uses selection.target!.objectId at services/science/science_processing_service/private_helpers.dart:169+`

**Proposed fix:** For review mode, derive the object list from the session's own rows (select distinct object_id where role='target' and session_id=?) and offer a picker defaulting to the one with the most points; only fall back to the live selection for the session currently being captured.

### Optical Train Diagnostics grades a session with ZERO data as "A - Excellent" and states "tilt looks controlled" and "center and edge behavior look balanced"

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Diagnostics > select 'Night D - V-Test'. The session has 120 plate-solved frames but zero psf_field_tiles and zero astrometric residual rows. The page renders 'Optical Health: A / Excellent', bars Tilt 0 and Collimation 0, a Tilt card badged 'Within range' with 'Score 0.0: tilt looks controlled for this session', and a Collimation card badged 'Centered' with 'Edge/center residual ratio: 0.0 - Center and edge behavior look balanced for this session'. The two panels immediately to the right of those verdicts read 'No PSF field tile data for this session. (0 tiles)' and 'No astrometric residual data for this session. (0 vectors)', and the Findings card reads 'No diagnostics data'.

**Expected:** With no PSF tiles and no residual vectors the health grade, the tilt score and the collimation verdict are all undefined. They must render as '-' / 'Not measured' with the Findings card's own explanation, not as a green A. As shipped, a user who suspects tilt opens this tab, sees 'A / Excellent / tilt looks controlled' and stops investigating - the app has told them their optical train is healthy on the strength of no measurement at all.

**Repro:** Analytics > Diagnostics > pick any session with no psf_field_tiles rows (a fresh install: every session). Compare the Optical Health grade with the '0 tiles' / '0 vectors' panels beside it.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/optical_train_diagnostics_service.dart (tilt/collimation scores default to 0.0 when the input lists are empty, and 0.0 is the BEST score, so 'no data' grades as perfect); rendered by packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/diagnostics/diagnostic_dump_screen.dart via the Analytics Diagnostics tab`

**Proposed fix:** Make tiltScore/collimationScore nullable. When either input set is empty return null and render 'Not measured - needs N solved frames with PSF tiles', and suppress the letter grade entirely rather than defaulting it to A.

### About page's GitHub and Documentation links are placeholders that 404 / do not resolve

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > About offers three link buttons: GitHub, Documentation, Discord. GitHub opens https://github.com/skotiq-astro, which returns HTTP 404 -- the real project repo is https://github.com/Scdouglas1999/Skotiq (HTTP 200, per the repo README badge). Documentation opens https://skotiq.astro/docs; 'skotiq.astro' does not resolve in DNS at all (.astro is not a delegated TLD). Discord opens https://discord.gg/skotiq, which is not a Skotiq invite. Clicking GitHub in the app produced no error banner -- the launch is reported as successful and the user is dropped on a 404.

**Expected:** The three community links a shipped app puts on its About page must point at the real destinations: github.com/Scdouglas1999/Skotiq, the docs (docs/index.md is what the README points at), and a real Discord invite -- or the button should not exist.

**Repro:** gear icon > Settings > About > click GitHub. Then: curl -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://github.com/skotiq-astro -> 404; getent hosts skotiq.astro -> no answer.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/about_settings.dart:102,109,116 (and the mobile duplicates at :102/:109/:116 vs desktop :128/:135/:142)`

**Proposed fix:** Hoist the three URLs into one const block and set them to the real repo/docs/invite; add a launch-failure path that surfaces the URL (the existing showErrorSnackBar only fires when canLaunchUrl is false, which is not the failure mode here).

### Backups are written to ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups regardless of the configured data directory, so bundles from different databases pile into one undifferentiated 'Recent Backups' list

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I launched a brand-new scratch profile with SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR=SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ns-audit/r-settings-system/data and an empty skotiq.db. Settings > Backup & Restore immediately listed 8 'Recent Backups' dated Aug 1 -- the developer's real bundles -- and reported 'Last Full Backup Aug 2, 2026 10:27'. The backup directory is ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups and ignores the data-dir override entirely, so this sandboxed instance also *wrote* into it: skotiq_autosave_2026-08-02T10-27-37, -27-44, -27-45 and -30-25 are snapshots of my throwaway database (the 10:30 one records profilesCount: 0, taken right after I deleted every profile) sitting in the same list as the real ones. Nothing in the row -- name, size, date, icon -- says which database a bundle came from. A user with two installs, or anyone who has ever moved their data directory, is one click away from restoring a foreign configuration over their live one. As with pairing.db there are also two directories on this machine (~/Documents/Skotiq/backups with 9 bundles and ~/Skotiq/backups with 4), i.e. the same install has written backups to two places.

**Expected:** Backups belong under the configured data directory, and each row should show what it contains (profiles/sequences/targets counts are already in the bundle metadata) and which install/machine wrote it. The bundle already carries appVersion and platform; add a database/install id and show it.

**Repro:** drive_linux.py start --profile <new> --fresh > Settings > Backup & Restore. The list is non-empty on a first-ever launch. ls ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups -> the same 8 files, plus new skotiq_autosave_* written by the sandbox. python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('.../skotiq_autosave_2026-08-02T10-30-25.892145.nsbackup'))['metadata'])" -> profilesCount 0, i.e. my scratch DB.

**Where:** `the backup service's directory resolution (getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/Skotiq/backups) rather than the SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR-aware resolver used by skotiq.db`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the backup directory through the same data-dir resolver as the main database; stamp an install id into the bundle and show bundle contents (profiles/sequences/targets) in each Recent Backups row.

### Restore silently drops a backed-up equipment profile when its row id is already taken, and still reports 'Restored 123 items'

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The bundle skotiq_backup_2026-08-02T10-43-32.295333.nsbackup contains exactly one equipment profile: {"id": 3, "name": "Backup Test Rig"} (metadata: settingsCount 122, profilesCount 1 -> 123 items). Locally, row id 3 existed but had been renamed to 'Local Work Do Not Lose'. I restored that bundle. The toast said 'Restored 123 items. Restart Skotiq before the next run.' The DB afterwards: 3|Local Work Do Not Lose|1 -- one profile, the local one, unchanged. 'Backup Test Rig' was neither inserted nor applied over row 3; it was dropped on the floor. The same restore into a database where id 3 was free DID bring the profile back (I verified that first, after deleting every row), so the discriminator is the id collision, not the bundle. The count in the message is the bundle's item count, not the number of items actually applied, so it is a claim the app has not checked.

**Expected:** Restore must not key library items on the backup's row ids -- ids are local and get reused as soon as you delete the highest row. Re-key on content (name) or insert with a fresh id, and report what was actually written ('122 settings updated, 1 profile added, 0 skipped'), never the bundle's own count.

**Repro:** Create a profile, Create Backup, rename that profile (or delete it and create another that takes the same rowid), then restore the bundle from Recent Backups. Toast claims all items restored; sqlite3 <data>/skotiq.db 'select id,name from equipment_profiles' shows the backed-up profile is absent.

**Where:** `the restore path in the backup service (row-id-keyed upsert/ignore for equipmentProfiles) called from packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/backup_screen.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Strip ids on restore and insert as new rows (matching existing rows by name to update), and build the success message from the write result rather than from bundle metadata.

### Planetarium view hotkeys swallow typing in the search field - object search by name is unusable from the keyboard

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With the planetarium plan panel open and the caret blinking in the 'Search objects, names...' field, typing 'Vega' deterministically leaves 'Va' in the field: the 'e' toggled the ecliptic layer on and the 'g' toggled the equatorial grid on instead of entering text. Typing the full lowercase alphabet 'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz' left only 'abd' in the field - c/e/f/g/h/m/n/r are all bound to view actions (constellation lines, ecliptic, FOV overlay, grid, reset view, minimap, now) and after 'h' (reset view) the field lost focus entirely so i..z were dropped too. Reproduced at 30ms, 250ms, 400ms and 2000ms inter-key delays: it is a shortcut conflict, not a dropped-keystroke race.

**Expected:** A focused text field must consume ordinary character keys. Planetarium single-letter hotkeys should be suppressed whenever the primary focus is a text input (Flutter: check FocusManager.instance.primaryFocus?.context?.widget is EditableText, or move the shortcuts into a Shortcuts/Actions scope that a TextField's own shortcut scope shadows).

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > click the sidebar toggle (top right) > click the 'Search objects, names...' field > type Vega. Field shows 'Va'; the ecliptic line and the RA/Dec grid have both switched on behind the panel.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/planetarium_screen/actions.dart:551-600`

**Proposed fix:** In _handleKeyEvent, return KeyEventResult.ignored early when the primary focus is an editable text field, before the single-letter branches.

### At a 1-degree imaging FOV the sky renders essentially empty - measured 1 star in 0.6 sq deg at astronomical dark

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Zoomed to the minimum FOV (1.0 deg short axis), time set to tonight's astro dark via the TONIGHT button, view at the zenith (RA 17h44m, Dec +40, in Hercules): an 800x400 px patch of sky - 0.59 sq deg - contained ONE barely-visible dot (2 local maxima above grey level 40, zero above 60, on a background of 12). Star counts measured in the same pixel window while zooming out at the same sky centre: FOV 1.0 -> 1 star, 2.0 -> 6, 4.1 -> 33, 8.1 -> 74, 16.4 -> 328. Sky area scales as FOV^2, so surface density is flat at 1.7-3.5 stars/sq deg at every zoom - i.e. the depth never increases as you zoom in, and 2.9 stars/sq deg is exactly the whole bundled HYG catalog (119,600 stars over 41,253 sq deg), which is complete only to about mag 9.

**Expected:** An imaging-scale field is the whole point of a planetarium inside an astrophotography app - it is what you check framing and guide stars against. At 1 deg it should show tens of stars. The bundled catalog is the binding constraint, not the FOV-adaptive magnitude ramp, which the code intends to add up to +7 mag by 1 deg.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > wheel-zoom to FOV 1.0 deg > press TONIGHT in the time bar > look at the sky.
