**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/providers/planetarium_providers/render_config.dart:434-489`

**Proposed fix:** Ship (or auto-fetch on first run) a Tycho-2-depth tileset, and set the HYG hand-off seam to HYG's real completeness (~9.0), not 11.5.

### Object info popup keeps the old object's name and coordinates but recomputes Alt/Az for the NEW selection - it said Deneb was 'Below Horizon' while Deneb was 11.5 deg up

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicked the star Deneb on the chart; the popup opened correctly (Deneb / HIP102098 / mag 1.3 / Star (A2Ia) / RA 20h 41m 25.9s / Dec +45d 16' 49" / Current Position 12.2 deg, Az 320.1 deg, badge 'Daylight'). Without closing it, I selected M57 from the search results. The view recentred on M57 (Center RA 18h53m35s, Dec +33d 01') and the selection ring moved, but the popup stayed open still headed 'Deneb' with Deneb's identifier, magnitude, spectral type and RA/Dec - while its 'Current Position' block changed to '-10.3 deg, Az 331.2 deg' with a red 'Below Horizon' badge. Independent computation for that instant (2026-08-02 14:51:01 UTC, 40N 105W): Deneb is at alt +11.46 deg, az 320.93 deg; M57 is at alt -10.33 deg, az 331.20 deg. The popup is therefore showing M57's horizon position under Deneb's name, to the 0.1 deg. It is not a one-frame race: 74 seconds later it still read Deneb / -10.4 deg / Az 331.4 deg, which again tracks M57 (-10.4 / 331.4) and not Deneb (+11.4 / 321.0). The popup also carries a 'Slew' button, so the object the user is reading about and the object the app considers selected are not the same thing at the moment they press it.

**Expected:** The popup must either follow the new selection wholesale or close. Half-following - identity and catalogue coordinates from object A, live altitude/azimuth and the 'Below Horizon' warning from object B - is the worst of the three, because the visible result is a true-looking sentence about the wrong star.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium. Click a bright star (Deneb) to open its info popup. Leave it open. In the right panel's Search tab type '6720' and click the M57 result. The popup still says Deneb but its Current Position row now reports M57's altitude and azimuth and flags 'Below Horizon'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/widgets/object_info_popup.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Bind the whole popup to one immutable object snapshot taken when it opens (and dismiss/replace it on selection change), instead of reading identity from a captured object and the live horizon block from selectedObjectProvider.

### The photometry 'Uncertainty' column mixes two units: magnitudes for target/check rows and raw ADU flux noise for every comparison-star row, ~3 million times larger

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > (export hub) > Photometry Measurements > CSV exports 495 rows with columns ... SNR, Uncertainty ... For target and check rows Uncertainty is a magnitude sigma (0.00054-0.00071 across all 198 such rows). For every one of the 297 comparison rows it is instead the flux noise in ADU (1912.7-2211.8). The identity is exact, not approximate: `select max(abs(uncertainty - flux/snr)) from photometry_measurements where role='comparison'` returns 0.0. So one column, in the database and in the CSV a user hands to AAVSO or plots error bars from, carries two physical quantities that differ by a factor of about 3.4 million, with no unit column to tell them apart.

**Expected:** Uncertainty must be a magnitude sigma for every row. A comparison star's uncertainty is 1.0857/SNR (or the same variance-propagated sigma the check star already gets); the ADU flux noise, if it is wanted, belongs in a separate `flux_uncertainty` column with its own header.

**Repro:** 1. Run a science session so photometry_measurements is populated (495 rows here). 2. Analytics > Science > toolbar database icon > Photometry Measurements > CSV. 3. Open the CSV: target/check rows have Uncertainty ~6e-4, comparison rows ~2.1e3. 4. Confirm in the DB: select role,count(*),min(uncertainty),max(uncertainty),max(abs(uncertainty-flux/snr)) from photometry_measurements group by role -> check|99|0.00056|0.00071|2354.4, comparison|297|1912.7|2211.8|0.0, target|99|0.00054|0.00068|2478.3.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/science/science_processing_service/private_helpers.dart:465-471`

**Proposed fix:** Replace the `?? star.flux / star.snr` fallback with a magnitude sigma (1.0857 / snr). Add a schema-level unit note and a test asserting every uncertainty in the table is < 1.0 magnitude.

### Observing Alerts reports a First Light detection's brightness CHANGE as its apparent magnitude, so a self-discovered transient is announced as 'mag 2.4  NAKED EYE'

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > Observing Alerts renders the First Light detection as 'NS J13.50+47.2 / Supernova - Unconfirmed - possible new transient / 13h 29m 52s +47 11' 43" / mag 2.4 [NAKED EYE]'. The detection row has delta_mag = -2.35 (a brightness CHANGE in magnitudes relative to the atlas template) and no photometric zero point anywhere in the pipeline -- there is no apparent magnitude for this object at all. transient_alert_mapper.dart:20 sets `magnitude = detection.deltaMag?.abs()`, and the card at transient_card.dart:581 then buckets `magnitude <= 6.0` as NAKED EYE, <= 10 BINOCULAR, <= 14 SMALL SCOPE. Because a difference-image delta magnitude of more than ~6 is essentially impossible, EVERY First Light self-discovery that has a measured delta is announced as a naked-eye or binocular object. The app is telling the user they have found a magnitude-2.4 supernova -- brighter than Polaris, and the brightest supernova in recorded history.

**Expected:** Do not populate the alert's apparent-magnitude field from a delta magnitude. Either leave magnitude null (the card already handles a null magnitude by omitting the chip) and show the delta separately as 'Δ2.35 mag', or compute a real apparent magnitude from a photometric zero point when one exists. The NAKED EYE / BINOCULAR / SMALL SCOPE bucketing must never run on a delta.

**Repro:** 1. Insert a transient_detections row with kind='newSource', delta_mag=-2.35, snr=18.4, reviewed=1. 2. Restart the app. 3. Analytics > Science > Observing Alerts. 4. Read the chip on the alert card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/transients/transient_alert_mapper.dart:20 (`final magnitude = detection.deltaMag?.abs();`) consumed by packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/transients/widgets/transient_card.dart:553 and :580-588 _getBrightnessInfo`

**Proposed fix:** Add a distinct deltaMag field to TransientAlert and leave `magnitude` null for First Light detections; render 'Δ 2.35 mag brighter' instead of 'mag 2.4', and suppress the brightness bucket entirely when only a delta is known.

### Dashboard SAFETY card shows a green "Safe" badge while it is measuring nothing

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With no weather/safety device connected, the Dashboard (cockpit mode) Weather & Safety tile renders a green checkmark badge reading "Safe" in the card header, while the body of the same card reads Data: Unavailable, "No weather data sources available" and "Weather safety is off - conditions are not being checked". The single most glanceable element on the card asserts a safety state that the card itself says is unknown.

**Expected:** With no data source the badge must read "Not monitored" / "Unknown" in a neutral or amber colour. Green "Safe" must only ever mean a sensor reported safe conditions within the staleness window.

**Repro:** Fresh profile -> skip onboarding -> Equipment -> connect Simulated Camera/Mount/Focuser/Filter Wheel (no weather, no safety monitor) -> Dashboard. Right column, SAFETY tile.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/ - weather/safety dashboard tile`

**Proposed fix:** Make the badge a three-state derived from the data source: safe (fresh reading, safe), unsafe (fresh reading, unsafe), unmonitored (no source or stale). Never fall back to 'Safe' as the default when isSafe is null/absent.

### Registration reports a 10.34px alignment residual for a pure 2.2px translation of the same 40-star field, so even the working path cannot stack; match_radius_px defaults to 50px

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** audit_L_018.fits and audit_L_024.fits contain the SAME 40 stars offset by a rigid ~2.2px translation -- every star in 024 has a counterpart in 018 at distance 2.0-3.16px (median 2.24px), verified independently with a peak-finder and a nearest-neighbour match. The stacker matched 28 pairs and then computed an alignment residual of 10.34px, which exceeded its own 10.00px ceiling, so the frame was rejected. A correct affine fit on that geometry gives a sub-pixel residual. With match_radius_px defaulting to 50.0 and match_flux_tolerance to 0.7 in a 512x512 frame whose mean star separation is ~81px, the matcher is free to pair a star with a neighbour 50px away, and a handful of such wrong pairs is enough to wreck the least-squares fit and inflate the residual.

**Expected:** Registering two frames that differ by a 2.2px shift must produce a residual well under 1px and the frame must be accepted. Separately, max_alignment_residual_px = 10.0 is not a meaningful quality gate -- a 10px misalignment folded into the stack is a visibly smeared image; NINA/DSS-class tolerances are a fraction of a pixel.

**Repro:** 1. Stack & Share on the 8-sub session with 'Apply calibration' off. 2. Log: 'Frame rejected: alignment residual 10.34px exceeds max 10.00px (28 matches)'. 3. Independently measure the true offset: peak-find both FITS (40 peaks each) and nearest-neighbour match -> min 2.00px, median 2.24px, max 3.16px.

**Where:** `native/skotiq_native/imaging/src/stacking.rs:91 match_radius_px: 50.0, :92 match_flux_tolerance: 0.7, :94 max_alignment_residual_px: 10.0; residual computed at :487 compute_alignment_residual and the fit at :485 compute_affine_transform.`

**Proposed fix:** Scale match_radius_px to the frame (e.g. a small multiple of the measured FWHM, or an iterative coarse-then-fine match seeded by a translation estimate) rather than a fixed 50px, and reject pairs whose residual is an outlier before the final fit (RANSAC / sigma-clipped refit). Then tighten max_alignment_residual_px to ~1px so the gate means something.

### A single sub that fails alignment aborts the entire Stack & Share run and deletes the partial result, even though the code counts 'rejected' frames as a normal outcome

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With 'Apply calibration' off the stacker initialised on audit_L_024.fits with 34 stars, then the very first follower frame (audit_L_018.fits) was rejected for alignment residual and the whole run died: the dialog showed 'Stack & Share failed', no stacked_results row was written, and the log shows 'Live stacker stopped and resources released' one millisecond after the rejection -- frames 019-024 were never attempted. The native layer treats this as a per-frame rejection (it increments stats.rejected_alignment_failures and returns Err), and the Dart loop even has a framesRejected counter for exactly this case, but the counter is dead code: _addStackFrame is awaited without a try/catch, so the Err becomes an exception that escapes the for-loop into the run-level catch, which deletes the incomplete stacked_results row and rethrows.

**Expected:** A frame that fails to register must be counted and skipped, and the run must continue with the remaining subs -- exactly what the framesRejected counter was written for. Losing an entire night's stack to one satellite-trailed or wind-shaken sub is the single most expensive failure mode this feature has. The dialog should end with 'Stacked 6 of 8 subs - 2 rejected (alignment)'.

**Repro:** 1. Imaging > Stack > Stack & Share on an 8-sub session, turn 'Apply calibration' off, press Start. 2. Watch the log: 'Adding frame to stack: .../audit_L_018.fits' then 'Frame rejected: alignment residual 10.34px exceeds max 10.00px (28 matches)' then 'Live stacker stopped'. 3. Dialog reads 'Stack & Share failed'. 4. sqlite3 skotiq.db 'select count(*) from stacked_results' -> unchanged (1, the pre-seeded row); no new row for this run.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/stack_and_share_service.dart:328 (`final result = await _addStackFrame(...)` inside the followers loop, no try/catch) and the now-unreachable framesRejected accounting at :335-340; run-level catch that deletes the row at :484.`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the _addStackFrame await in a try/catch that increments framesRejected, records the reason, emits progress and continues; only fail the run if the surviving stacked count is below a floor (e.g. < 2 frames). Surface the rejected count and the most common reason in the completion state.

### Narrowband mixer 'Apply palette' is a dead control: no composite, no file, no error, no log line

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With three finalized single-filter masters for the target (Ha 20 frames, OIII 15, SII 10), the Narrowband mixer renders correctly, SHO/HOO presets populate the weight matrix correctly, and 'Apply palette' is enabled. Pressing it five times (three via click-img, twice via a raw xdotool click on the hovered button) produced nothing at all: narrowband_composites stayed at 0 rows, no FITS was written anywhere on disk, no toast appeared in the accessibility tree on six consecutive polls, and the app log recorded no combine activity. The screen's toast path is demonstrably alive -- the same session surfaced 'Curve is out of date - re-integrate to refresh before culling' from the cull rail -- and the neighbouring 'Run both' A/B button responded immediately with a 'Preview...' state and native STAR_DETECT log lines, so neither the toast machinery nor the click mapping explains the silence.

**Expected:** Apply palette should either produce a narrowband_composites row plus an on-disk composite FITS and render it in the composite card, or fail loudly with the reason ('Need at least two finalized narrowband masters to combine.' is already written for that case). Silently doing nothing on the mixer's only action makes the whole SHO/HOO feature untestable by the user.

**Repro:** 1. Seed three finalized integrated_masters for the target with filter Ha / OIII / SII and real master_fits_path files. 2. Session Review > Workbench > Refresh. 3. Scroll to 'Narrowband mixer', press SHO (matrix fills SII->R, Ha->G, OIII->B correctly). 4. Press 'Apply palette'. 5. sqlite3 skotiq.db 'select count(*) from narrowband_composites' -> 0; no new .fits under the masters output dir; no toast.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/session_review/widgets/narrowband_mixer_panel.dart:195-202 (onApply) -> workbench_view.dart:304 -> session_review_controller.dart:1233 runNarrowband() -> post_session_seam.dart:327 combineChannels()`

**Proposed fix:** Instrument runNarrowband with an entry log and surface every exit path (including the <2-input guard) through the same toast the cull rail uses; then fix whichever leg is swallowing the call. The button should also show a busy state while combiningNarrowband is true, which would have made the no-op visible.

### Dashboard Capture card fires the OLD exposure while displaying the new one (typed value only commits on Enter)

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** In the Capture dashboard widget I clicked the Exp field, selected all, typed 2, pressed Tab, then pressed Capture. The field kept showing 'Exp: 2' but the native log recorded 'Starting 120s exposure with gain=Some(100), offset=Some(50), bin=1x1' and the progress ring read 13% after ~16 s (13% of 120 s = 15.6 s - exact match). Typing the same value and pressing Enter instead did commit (the field renormalised to '3.0' and the capture ran ~3 s). So the card can show one exposure and take a completely different one, indefinitely, with no warning.

**Expected:** Losing focus (Tab, clicking another control, pressing Capture) must commit the edit, or the field must visibly reject/revert the uncommitted text. What the card shows and what it captures must never disagree.

**Repro:** Dashboard -> Edit Dashboard -> Widgets -> Capture Settings -> Done. Click the Exp box, Ctrl+A, type 2, press Tab, press Capture. Watch the progress % and `grep 'Starting.*exposure' /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/app.log`.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/capture_settings_card.dart:536 - _CompactSettingField wires only `onSubmitted: widget.onChanged`; there is no onEditingComplete/onChanged and no FocusNode listener. didUpdateWidget (:478) only rewrites the controller when the provider value changes, so stale text survives forever. Both Exp and Gain use this field.`

**Proposed fix:** Add a FocusNode and commit on focus loss (and on onEditingComplete), or switch to onChanged with debounce. Also re-read the controller text in the Capture handler before dispatching.

### Project-growth panel groups subs by UTC calendar day, so one continuous evening is reported as two nights and every night is dated a day late

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Session Review (narrative) 'This project over time' reports '2.0h integrated - 24 frames across 4 nights' with x-axis labels Jul 26 / Jul 28 / Jul 29 / Jul 30, and 'Best night: Jul 28 - 0.5h'. The database contains exactly THREE observing sessions (Audit Night A on the evening of Jul 25 local, B on Jul 27 local, C on Jul 29 local). Audit Night C is one continuous 50-minute run (19:30-20:20 America/New_York) that straddles 00:00 UTC, and the panel splits it into two separate 'nights' (Jul 29 with 4 frames, Jul 30 with 4 frames). Every other night is also labelled with the following calendar day because 21:00 EDT is 01:00 UTC.

**Expected:** Nights should be grouped by OBSERVING night in the site's local time (the conventional noon-to-noon boundary), so three sessions produce three points labelled Jul 25, Jul 27, Jul 29 and the panel says '3 nights'. Never a UTC calendar day: for any observer west of Greenwich the label is a day late, and for any observer east of Greenwich (all of Europe/Asia) the 00:00 UTC boundary falls in the MIDDLE of the observing night, splitting every single night in two.

**Repro:** 1. Seed three sessions of M51 lights, the third spanning 19:30-20:20 America/New_York on 2026-07-29 (crosses 00:00 UTC). 2. Analytics > History > Audit Night A > sparkles (Review & Integrate). 3. Scroll the narrative view to 'This project over time'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/smart_project_service.dart:679 _dayOf() -> DateTime.utc(dt.year, dt.month, dt.day); used by growthCurve(), bestNight() and reReferenceAdvice()`

**Proposed fix:** Group by observing night, not UTC date: shift the timestamp by the site's UTC offset and then by -12h before truncating to a day (the standard 'noon-to-noon' night key), and label the point with that local date. bestNight() and reReferenceAdvice() share _dayOf and are wrong in the same way -- a European user's 'best night' is currently the better HALF of a night.

### The collaborative mosaic detail screen is a nav trap: no back control, and the whole nav rail stops working

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Plan Tonight -> Discover -> Collaborate -> tapping a collaborative mosaic pushes a full-screen detail page ('M31 Wide Field - Collaborative mosaic - panels split across the club') whose only header controls are the title and a refresh icon. There is no back arrow and no breadcrumb. From there I clicked Dashboard in the left rail (twice), then Imaging, then pressed Escape, Alt+Left, Backspace and the mouse back button. The detail page stayed on screen every time. The rail even repaints the clicked destination as selected - it showed 'Dashboard' highlighted while the mosaic page was still displayed - so the app both refuses to navigate and lies about where you are. The only way out I found was to first go back into the mosaic project screen (via Join mosaic) and use its breadcrumb chevron.

**Expected:** A pushed detail page must have a back control, and clicking a nav destination must pop the pushed route and switch branch.

**Repro:** Connect to a hub, publish a mosaic, Plan Tonight -> Discover -> Collaborate -> Refresh -> tap the mosaic row. Then try every nav item and Escape/Alt+Left.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/collaborative_sky/collab_mosaic_detail_screen.dart (pushed as a bare MaterialPageRoute over the shell, so the StatefulShellRoute branch switch cannot pop it)`

**Proposed fix:** Give the detail page an AppBar with a leading back button, and make the shell's destination handler pop pushed routes (Navigator.popUntil to the branch root) before switching branch. The same fix covers /mosaic/:id, which showed the identical dead-nav behaviour.

### Tonight card reports a concrete twilight time and 9h28m imaging window while saying 'Location needed'

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With no observing location set (app_settings.observer_latitude = 0.0, and the command-bar LST reads '--:--:--'), the Tonight dashboard card shows Twilight 15:22, Moon 90%, Window 9h 28m - and in the same card Target: 'Location needed' plus 'Set an observing location to generate target plans.' The twilight/window numbers are computed at latitude 0 / longitude 0 in the machine's local timezone (15:22 local = 19:22 UTC, which is astronomical twilight on the equator on Aug 1), so they are wrong for every real observer, and there is no marking that they are placeholders.

**Expected:** Twilight and Window must render as '--' (like LST and Exposure already do) until a site is set. A user reading 'Window 9h 28m' will plan a session around a number that is not theirs.

**Repro:** Fresh profile, skip onboarding, do not set an observing location. Dashboard -> Edit Dashboard -> Widgets -> Tonight -> Done. Read the Tonight tile.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/tonight_card.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Gate the twilight/window rows on the same 'has site' predicate the Target row already uses, and render '--' when it is false. Never fall back to lat/lon 0,0.

### Polar alignment ends with a green "Alignment Complete" tick and a green "+0%" chip after a 30-arcminute residual error

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I ran Three-Point Polar Alignment to completion against the simulators (camera+mount connected, ASTAP stub solving). Three points measured, adjust phase entered, then I pressed Done. The result screen shows a large green success check, the headline 'Alignment Complete', 'Final error: 1800.0 arcseconds', and an Alignment Summary reading Before 1800" -> After 1800" with Improvement rendered as a GREEN '+0%' chip. The Alignment History row for the run is likewise stamped with a green checkmark ('Today 10:30 / Final: 1800" / +0%'). 1800 arcsec is 30 arcminutes - half a degree of polar error, 60x the wizard's own Auto-Complete threshold of 30" - and the session improved the alignment by exactly nothing.

**Expected:** The terminal state must be honest about the residual. Success styling (green tick, green chip) belongs only to a run that reached the configured threshold. A run that ends 60x above threshold with 0% improvement should read 'Alignment stopped - still 30' 00" off' in amber/red, and the improvement chip should be neutral or red at <=0%.

**Repro:** Equipment -> connect Simulated Camera + Simulated Mount. Imaging -> Mount tab -> Three-Point Polar Alignment -> Start Alignment. Let the 3 points measure, wait for the adjust phase, press Done.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/polar_alignment/polar_alignment_screen_parts/ (completion panel + _history_panel.dart row styling)`

**Proposed fix:** Drive the completion iconography and the history-row icon off `auto_completed` / (final_total_error <= autoCompleteThreshold), not off 'the task ended'. Colour the improvement chip by sign: green only for a real reduction, neutral at 0, red if the error grew.

### Co-imaging card says "You are pooling light here" after you explicitly decline to share your subs

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I connected to a real hub (a locally run skotiq_hub on 127.0.0.1:8091), pressed Start session and created a session on NGC 7000. The app then opened 'Share your co-imaging subs', whose own text says 'Let this rig contribute my completed subs unattended ... Leave unchecked and your subs will NOT be added to the combined stack.' I pressed Cancel, granting nothing. The session card then rendered a green check with 'You are pooling light here'. Nothing is being pooled: no license was accepted, no contribute consent was given, and FRAMES/COMBINED still read 0.

**Expected:** The caption must reflect contribution consent, not mere membership: 'Joined - not contributing (sharing off)' in a neutral colour, with a 'Turn on sharing' action. Telling a user their photons are being pooled when they declined is the most consequential thing this screen can get wrong.

**Repro:** Run a hub: cd server/skotiq_hub && dart run bin/server.dart --port 8091 --bind 127.0.0.1 --db /tmp/ns-audit/hub/hub.db --atlas-root /tmp/ns-audit/hub/atlas --secret 0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef. App -> Plan Tonight -> Discover -> Collaborate -> Connect to a hub -> http://127.0.0.1:8091 -> Start session (NGC 7000, RA 314.75, Dec 44.31) -> Cancel the sharing dialog.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/collaborative_sky/widgets/coimaging_session_card.dart:372-388 - the caption is gated on `joined == true` only, with no reference to the sharing/consent state.`

**Proposed fix:** Thread the rig's contribute-consent flag into the card and render three states: not joined / joined-not-contributing / contributing.

### Flat Frame Wizard captures at a different camera offset than the Imaging screen (10 vs 50) and never shows which gain/offset it will use

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** At 10:46 the Imaging screen's Camera tab showed 'Gain (0-600) 100 / Offset (0-255) 50', and the app's own light-frame path commands offset 50 (app.log 'Starting 120s exposure with gain=Some(100), offset=Some(50)'). At 10:50 I ran the Flat Frame Wizard on the same camera in the same session, and every one of its seven exposures was commanded as 'gain=Some(100), offset=Some(10)'. The Flat Wizard UI displays no gain or offset anywhere - Quick Capture shows only Filter, Histogram Target, Tolerance and Frame Count - so nothing warns the user. Flats taken at a different offset from the lights carry the wrong bias pedestal and the app's own library matcher refuses them: flat_library_dao.dart:132 states 'gain, offset, and binning must match exactly'.

**Expected:** The flat wizard must capture with the same gain/offset the light frames will use, and it must display them (NINA shows gain/offset/binning on the flat wizard panel and lets you override them). If the two surfaces genuinely have different sources of truth, that is the bug; at minimum the wizard has to show the values it is about to command.

**Repro:** Equipment -> connect Simulated Camera. Imaging -> Camera tab -> read Gain/Offset. Imaging -> Camera tab -> Flat Wizard -> Quick Capture -> Start Capture -> choose a folder -> Continue. Then: grep 'gain=Some' /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/app.log

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/flat_wizard_service.dart:314-323 (offset = profileDefaultOffset ?? currentOffset ?? status.offset) vs the imaging capture path; UI: packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/flat_wizard/ (no gain/offset surface)`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the flat capture config from the same source the light capture uses, and render 'Gain 100 - Offset 50 - Bin 1x1' on the wizard panel with an override affordance.

### An aborted polar alignment is written to Alignment History as a success with a green "+97%" improvement badge

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I started All-Sky polar alignment, let it iterate for ~2 minutes without touching the mount (impossible to touch - it is a simulator), then pressed Stop. The status line correctly read 'Polar alignment stopped'. But a row was still committed to polar_alignment_history and the Alignment History panel now shows 'Today 10:41 / Final: 1376\"' with a green checkmark and a green '+97%' badge. Nothing was adjusted, the user cancelled, and the residual is still 1376\" (23 arcmin). The '97% improvement' is an artefact of the first iteration's noisy 40104\" measurement being taken as 'initial'.

**Expected:** A cancelled run must either not be persisted, or be persisted and displayed as 'Stopped' with a neutral icon and no improvement badge. Improvement must be computed against a stable initial measurement, not the first noisy sample, and must not be reported at all for a run in which the user made no adjustment.

**Repro:** Polar Alignment -> All-Sky -> Start Alignment -> wait ~2 min -> Stop. Scroll the left column to Alignment History. Cross-check: sqlite3 /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/data/skotiq.db 'select id,initial_total_error,final_total_error,auto_completed,completed_at from polar_alignment_history' -> row 2: 40104.58|1376.13|0 (auto_completed=0, i.e. threshold never met) yet completed_at is populated.

**Where:** `the polar alignment history writer (run finalisation) + packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/polar_alignment/polar_alignment_screen_parts/_history_panel.dart row styling`

**Proposed fix:** Record a terminal status (completed / stopped / failed) on the history row and render it; suppress the improvement badge when auto_completed=0 and no adjustment was detected.

### All-Sky polar alignment reticle says "Within acceptance - hold steady" and 0.0" before a single frame has been taken

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On the idle All-Sky screen (Start Alignment button showing, status line 'Ready to start polar alignment', and the Azimuth/Altitude/Total readouts all showing '--'), the target reticle in the right rail draws a green centred dot with the caption '0.0\"' and 'Within acceptance - hold steady'. No image has been captured and no solve has run; the app is asserting a perfect polar alignment purely because its error value is null/zero-initialised.

**Expected:** With no measurement the reticle must show no marker and read '--' / 'Not measured', exactly like the Azimuth/Altitude/Total tiles 12 px below it already do. 'Within acceptance' is the single most consequential sentence on this screen and it must only ever follow a solve.

**Repro:** Imaging -> Mount -> Three-Point Polar Alignment -> select the All-Sky segment -> (if a previous result is showing, press Restart). Read the right rail: green 0.0\" 'Within acceptance - hold steady' above 'Azimuth -- / Altitude -- / Total --'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/polar_alignment/widgets/all_sky_target_reticle.dart (renders from a non-nullable error defaulting to 0) fed by _right_panel.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Make the reticle take a nullable error and render an explicit unmeasured state; gate the acceptance caption on phase != idle && a solve has landed.

### Polar alignment computes and reports azimuth/altitude error at latitude 0, longitude 0 when no observing location is set

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With no observing location configured (app_settings observer_latitude=0.0, observer_longitude=0.0; Equipment's readiness panel is actively flagging 'No observing location set'), I started TPPA and it ran all three points and produced concrete corrections - 'Azimuth Left 427.6"', 'Altitude Up 2287.9"', final 1800.0". The az/alt decomposition of a polar-axis error is a function of site latitude; at lat 0 the pole sits on the horizon, so every number the wizard printed is the correction for an observer on the equator, not for the user. The native layer has an explicit guard for this (polar_align/mod.rs:390-399 returns 'Polar alignment requires observer latitude/longitude...') but it only fires when the location is None, and the app installs a (0,0) location at startup - app.log: 'Setting observer location: lat=0, lon=0, elev=0' - so the guard can never fire.

**Expected:** Starting polar alignment with no site set must be refused with 'Set your observing location first' (the wizard already refuses on a disconnected camera, a disconnected mount and an unavailable solver), or the site must be part of the pre-flight checklist shown on the idle screen. It must never silently substitute 0,0.

**Repro:** Fresh profile, do not set an observing location. Equipment -> connect Simulated Camera + Mount. Imaging -> Mount tab -> Three-Point Polar Alignment -> Start Alignment. Verify with: sqlite3 /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/data/skotiq.db "select key,value from app_settings where key like 'observer_%'" -> observer_latitude|0.0, observer_longitude|0.0.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/polar_alignment/polar_alignment_screen.dart:59-121 (_startAlignment pre-flight has camera/mount/solver checks but no site check); native/skotiq_native/bridge/src/state.rs get_observer_location + the startup path that sets 0,0`

**Proposed fix:** Stop materialising a (0,0) observer location: leave it None until the user sets one, so the existing native guard fires. Add a site check to _startAlignment with the same snackbar treatment as the camera/mount checks, and show it in the idle checklist ('Set your observing location').

### Mobile onboarding decides ASCOM is unavailable from the PHONE's OS, so a phone paired to a Windows rig can never pick ASCOM

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The driver-selection step offers Native / Alpaca / INDI / Sim and prints 'ASCOM COM drivers are Windows-only. Use Alpaca to reach an ASCOM server from this platform.' The available-driver set is computed from Platform.isWindows - the platform of the DEVICE RUNNING THE UI. On Android that is false by construction, so the ASCOM tile can never be offered on the phone, no matter what the host is. Drivers run on the host, not on the phone, and the host already publishes the correct answer: /api/info returns platformCapabilities.drivers[] with backend 'ascom', status and supportedPlatforms.

**Expected:** Drive the driver list from the connected host's platformCapabilities (already served by /api/info), falling back to the local platform only when this device IS the backend. On a phone paired to a Windows imaging PC, ASCOM must be offered and the Windows-only footnote must not appear.

**Repro:** Connection screen -> 'Skip Connection (View UI Only)' -> Next -> Step 2 of 13 'Drivers'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/onboarding_provider.dart:73-84 (consumed by packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/onboarding/steps/driver_step.dart:18,58-72)`

### An unreachable host is reported as 'the server did not report a valid API version - update the server', blaming software that is fine

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** Connect over Tailscale with a MagicDNS name that does not exist. After ~3s the sheet closes and the connection screen error slot reads: 'The Skotiq server did not report a valid API version. Update the server before connecting.' Nothing was ever reached - the name does not resolve. The message names the wrong subsystem, prescribes the wrong fix (go update the observatory PC), and omits the host, the URL and the real cause.

**Expected:** The same class of message the manual Enter-IP path already produces - 'Cannot reach http://<host>:<port>/api/info from this device' plus the tailnet-specific hint (is Tailscale running on this phone and on the rig, is the MagicDNS name spelled correctly). A version-compatibility verdict must only be rendered when /api/info actually answered.

**Repro:** Connection screen -> Connect over Tailscale -> Tailscale address = skotiq-rig.tail1a2b3.ts.net, Port 8080, Transport HTTP -> Connect.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/main_parts/mobile_reconnect_ops.dart:271-284; message at packages/skotiq_remote_protocol/lib/src/server_compatibility.dart:21-29`

**Proposed fix:** In _connectToServer, distinguish 'fetchServerInfo returned null' (unreachable / not a Skotiq endpoint) from 'fetchServerInfo answered but the version is unparseable'. Only run SkotiqServerCompatibility.check on the fetched-and-parsed case; on null, emit the reachability message that already exists on the manual path, parameterised by scheme/host/port.

### Sequencer tab shows a red 'Weather safety: Unsafe' alarm on a phone that has never contacted a host and has no weather data

`defect` · mobile operation - companion dashboard shell, mount/sequencer/log/settings tabs, session picker and session replay, driven disconnected on the ns_test_api35 emulator · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** With no appliance ever paired, the Sequencer tab's sticky header renders 'Weather safety' with a red alert-triangle pill reading 'Unsafe'. The header is COLLAPSED by default, so the red 'Unsafe' verdict is the only thing the user sees. Expanding the card contradicts it outright: 'SAFETY: Unsafe / Data: Unavailable / Connect to an imaging host to evaluate weather safety.' The app is simultaneously asserting a safety verdict and admitting it has no data to base one on. Root cause is that WeatherSafetyStatus has exactly three members - safe, unsafe, snoozed - with no 'unknown', and WeatherSafetyState.initial() picks unsafe with reason 'Weather safety has not been evaluated yet'. Fail-closed is defensible on the desktop, which is the actor that would refuse to open the roof. It is not defensible on a remote companion that cannot park anything: the phone's only job here is to report, and it is reporting a red alarm about weather it has never sampled.

**Expected:** When dataSource is SafetyDataSource.unavailable the pill should read 'Unknown' or 'No data' in a neutral/secondary colour, never the error colour with an alertTriangle. The collapsed header must carry the qualifier that the expanded card already has - a user who never expands the tile must not be left believing conditions were measured and found unsafe.

**Repro:** 1. adb shell setprop wrap.com.skotiq.mobile SKOTIQ_COMPANION_UI=1; drive_android.py start. 2. Skip Connection (View UI Only) - no host is ever contacted. 3. Bottom nav -> Sequencer (tap-xy 540 2253). 4. Read the header at [0,472][1080,619]. 5. Tap it to expand.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/tabs/sequencer_tab.dart:671-683 (label/tint switch); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/weather_safety_provider.dart:92-102, 201-214`

**Proposed fix:** Add WeatherSafetyStatus.unknown (or derive it in the view from dataSource == SafetyDataSource.unavailable) and render it as ('Unknown', colors.textSecondary, LucideIcons.helpCircle). Keep the fail-closed behaviour for the desktop's actuation path - a phone label and a run-gating decision are different questions and should not share one enum value.


## P2

### 'Load Previous Setup' is inert: it restores no equipment, no sequence and writes nothing to the log - it just closes the dialog

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On relaunch I clicked the Continue Session dialog's primary button 'Load Previous Setup' (the only styled action; 'Skip' is the secondary). The dialog closed and NOTHING else changed: the status bar still read Camera Disconnected / Mount Disconnected / Guider Disconnected / Focus ---, the profile chip still read 'My Equipment 0/0', the Sequencer builder still held an empty 'New Sequence' with '0 targets / Sequence 0 steps', and app.log recorded not one line between the click at 10:41:30 and 10:42:40 (no connect attempt, no sequence load, no error). The previous session had 4 connected simulators and a 5-node sequence.

**Expected:** Either it reconnects the previous equipment and loads the previous sequence, or - if it cannot resolve them, which it already admits by printing 'Unknown Profile' and 'No Sequence' - the button is disabled with an explanation. A primary action that silently no-ops is worse than no button: after a crash mid-night this is the control a user reaches for first.

**Repro:** Run a sequence with simulators connected, quit the app, relaunch, click 'Load Previous Setup', then read the status bar, the profile chip and the Sequencer builder.

**Where:** `App launch > Continue Session > Load Previous Setup`

**Proposed fix:** Wire the button to the same restore path the profile auto-connect uses, and gate it on the context actually resolving (same nulls that produce 'Unknown Profile'/'No Sequence' should disable it).

### Sequence recovery checkpoint is stored at a hardcoded shared path, so a brand-new profile was offered another database's interrupted run -- and discarding it deleted that run's recovery data

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On a --fresh profile whose database contains zero rows in sequences, sequence_runs and sequence_checkpoints, the app popped a modal 'Recover Sequence? A previous sequence was interrupted and can be resumed. Sequence: AUDIT SEQ, Saved 0m ago, Completed 16 frames (0m integration)' -- on top of the First Night Walkthrough of a first-run user. The checkpoint is written to ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint, ignoring SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR/SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR, and belongs to a different data directory entirely (its save_path is /tmp/ns-audit/sequencer-run/captures). The modal has only Discard/Resume and Escape does not dismiss it, so to continue I had to press Discard -- which deleted both the checkpoint and its .bak, destroying the other instance's recovery state. Two further problems in the same file: the checkpoint says executor_state='Completed' and is_active=false while the dialog calls it 'interrupted'; and completed_exposures=16 with completed_integration_secs=0.0 for a 3.0 s x 20 node, which the UI renders as '16 frames (0m integration)'.

**Expected:** The recovery checkpoint must live under the active data directory so it can never describe a run that is not in the open database, the dialog must not offer to resume a run whose executor_state is Completed, and a fresh install must never see this modal.

**Repro:** 1. Run instance A against data dir X, start and interrupt a sequence named AUDIT SEQ. 2. Start instance B with SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR=Y (empty DB). 3. B shows 'Recover Sequence? ... AUDIT SEQ'. 4. sqlite3 Y/skotiq.db 'select * from sequence_checkpoints' -> empty. 5. Press Discard in B -> ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint and .bak are both gone.

**Where:** `native checkpoint writer -> ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint (path resolution ignores SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR); recovery prompt raised from app_shell`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the checkpoint path from the same data-dir resolver the database uses, stamp the owning database/profile id into the checkpoint and refuse to offer recovery when it does not match, and gate the prompt on executor_state != Completed.

### The in-app Logs viewer records nothing about the failures the user just watched happen -- five failed catalog downloads and every rejected save are absent from it and from the 18.8 MB log export

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Logs is the surface a user is told to use ('View application logs with filtering and export'). In a session where I triggered the GLADE+ download five times and watched it fail five times, searching the viewer for 'download' gives '0 of 139 entries / No entries match current filters' and searching for 'glade' gives 0 as well. The level filter reports the whole session as one error: Error+ = '1 of 139 entries', the single [ProfileActivationWriteThrough] 'Strict activation aborted: target profile 1 does not exist'. Not logged anywhere: the catalog download failure, the two rejected profile imports, the rejected profile saves, or the two later unhandled 'Cannot activate profile' exceptions that Flutter printed to stderr. I then pressed Export, which produced /home/scdouglas/Documents/skotiq_logs_2026-08-02T11-08-20.txt -- 18,829,614 bytes, 142,921 lines, concatenating the rotated on-disk logs back to 2026-07-27 -- and grepped it: 0 occurrences of 'glade' and 0 of 'annotation catalog download'. The failure genuinely has no persistent record.

**Expected:** Every user-visible failure should leave a log line at WARN/ERROR with the cause (here: the VizieR TAP 503). Otherwise the log viewer gives a user and a support engineer false confidence -- 'only 1 error all night' -- about a session that failed repeatedly.

**Repro:** Settings > Catalogs > Download Standard (~50 MB), which fails. Then Settings > Logs > search 'download' -> 0 of N entries. Set the level filter to Error+ -> 1 of N. Press Export and grep the resulting file for 'glade' -> 0 hits.

**Where:** `the catalog download handler in packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/catalog_settings_screen.dart (catches and snackbars without logging) and the equivalent swallow in the profile save/import paths`

**Proposed fix:** Log every caught user-facing exception at ERROR with the underlying cause before showing the snackbar.

### The desktop app can never tell you a new version exists: the only 'Updates' page in Settings is for a remote appliance, and the built-in update manager is wired to a server URL nothing ever sets

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Searching Settings for 'update' returns exactly two entries: About and 'Appliance Updates'. Appliance Updates says 'Connect to a remote appliance to manage its updates. The local desktop app updates itself through its own installer.' and renders nothing else off-network. There is no local update check anywhere in Settings, and About (which the settings catalog even tags with the keyword 'updates') shows only a version string and three web links. The app does ship an in-app updater -- app.dart:204-206 wraps the desktop shell in UpdateManagerWidget, which fires _checkForUpdates() 3 s after launch -- but that check is gated on state.updateServerUrl being non-empty (update_manager_widget.dart:40-43) and the only caller of UpdateNotifier.configure(serverUrl:) in the whole repo is apps/desktop/lib/headless_api/update_wiring.dart, i.e. the appliance server, never the GUI. So on the shipped desktop build the updater is inert: I ran the app for ~40 minutes with working internet and it made no version request, showed no update UI, and logged nothing about updates. The sentence 'updates itself through its own installer' is also not true of the Linux build, which is distributed as a tarball with no installer.

**Expected:** A 'Check for updates' row in About or a Software Update section: current version + build, channel (stable/beta), last checked, and a manual check. NINA and SGP both check at launch and show release notes; an astrophotographer needs to know a fix landed before the night, not after.

**Repro:** Settings > search 'update' -> About, Appliance Updates. Open Appliance Updates on the desktop build: one explanatory sentence, no controls. Open About: version 6.1.0, three links, System Information. No update control anywhere. drive_linux.py log over the whole session contains no update/version request.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/update_settings.dart (appliance-only page) and the unconfigured desktop path above`

**Proposed fix:** Configure the update service with the public release feed on desktop startup and give Settings > About a 'Software Update' block (version+build, channel, Check now, last checked). Until that exists, drop the 'updates itself through its own installer' claim.

### Planets are computed for the equinox of date but stars and DSOs are drawn at J2000 - the two are mixed on one chart, so planets sit ~22 arcmin off the star background

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicking Jupiter on the chart at 2026-08-02 15:12 UTC from 40N/105W gave RA 8h 39m 30.9s, Dec +18d 53' 53", Alt 38.1, Az 96.9. An independent Keplerian ephemeris puts Jupiter at J2000 RA 8h37m58.3s / +18d59'31"; precessing that to the equinox of date gives 8h39m29.5s / +18d53'50", which matches the app to 1.4 s of RA and 3 arcsec of declination, and its alt/az (38.11 / 96.94) matches the app's to 0.01 deg - whereas the J2000 position gives 38.46 / 97.12, which does not. So the planet path precesses. The star path does not: the app reports Deneb as 20h 41m 25.9s / +45d 16' 49", which is its J2000 position to the tenth of a second, and its Alt/Az reproduces from those unprecessed coordinates. Both sets of coordinates are then fed to the same projection, so every planet is drawn displaced from the true stellar background by the accumulated precession since J2000 - about 1m32s of RA and 5.7 arcmin of declination here, roughly 22 arcmin total, or two-thirds of a Moon diameter. The screenshot shows this in the worst possible place: Jupiter is drawn right next to M44, the Beehive, and the drawn Jupiter-M44 separation is wrong by that amount.

**Expected:** One reference frame for everything on the chart. Either precess the catalog to the epoch of observation before projecting (correct, and it also fixes the alt/az error already filed), or express the planetary ephemeris in J2000. Mixing them is worse than either.

**Repro:** Settings > Location 40N 105W > Plan Tonight > Planetarium > press the home/reset icon so the zenith is centred > click Jupiter and read its Coordinates, then click a bright star and read its Coordinates. The planet is equinox-of-date, the star is J2000.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/astronomy/astronomy_calculations.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Precess catalogue positions to the observation epoch inside the render pipeline (a single rotation applied to the star/DSO arrays per frame or per minute), so planets, stars, the grid and the horizon transform all share one equinox.

### The command bar's 'Search' button (and its Ctrl/Cmd+K shortcut) never focuses the search field, and does not even switch the panel to the Search tab

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With the right-hand plan panel already open on the Info tab, clicking the command bar's 'Search  ⌘K' button only showed a 'Search the sky' tooltip: the panel stayed on the Info tab and the caret was not placed in 'Search objects, names...'. Typing 'Albireo' immediately after produced nothing in the field. Selecting the Search tab by hand left the field still unfocused - typing 'Albireo' again produced nothing, the panel kept showing its 'Search for objects / Try "M42", "Andromeda Galaxy", or "Sirius"' empty state. Ctrl+K and Super+K also did nothing. Source: the handler is _openPlanPanelOnSearch, which on desktop sets _planPanelOpen = true and _layersPanelOpen = false and stops - it never selects the search tab and never requests focus for the text field.

**Expected:** A control labelled Search with a keyboard-shortcut badge should open the panel ON the search tab with the caret in the field, so the user can type immediately. Finding an object by name is the single most common thing anyone does in a planetarium; it currently takes three separate clicks (open panel, Search tab, click into the field) before the first keystroke.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > open the right sidebar and select any tab except Search > click the 'Search  ⌘K' button in the command bar > type 'Albireo'. Nothing is entered. Repeat after clicking the Search tab: still nothing.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/widgets/redesign/planetarium_shell.dart:443`

**Proposed fix:** In _openPlanPanelOnSearch also set the panel's selected tab to Search and call requestFocus() on the search field's FocusNode (autofocus on the field when the tab is entered from this path).

### Confirming a First Light candidate does not update the Observing Alerts tab beside it: the same detection reads 'Confirmed' on one sub-tab and 'New' on the next

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On Analytics > Science > First Light I pressed Confirm on the candidate; the card's badge changed to 'Confirmed', the toast read 'Marked as confirmed.' and the database row updated (reviewed=1). Switching one tab across to Observing Alerts -- the same screen, the same detection, mapped through transientAlertFromDetection -- the card still carries a blue 'New' badge and sits under the 'New' filter. transientAlertFromDetection maps reviewed -> TransientAlertState.acknowledged, so the alert list is being served from a snapshot taken at app launch and is not rebuilt when the detection changes.

**Expected:** Two views of one row, on two tabs of one screen, must not disagree. Confirming in First Light should immediately move the alert out of 'New'.

**Repro:** 1. Seed a confirmed-able detection and restart the app. 2. Analytics > Science > First Light > Confirm (badge becomes 'Confirmed', toast 'Marked as confirmed.'). 3. sqlite3 -> reviewed=1. 4. Click 'Observing Alerts'. 5. The card shows the 'New' badge.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/transient_alert_provider.dart (the alert list is not invalidated when transient_detections changes)`

**Proposed fix:** Have the alert provider watch the detections table (or invalidate it from the confirm/dismiss mutation) so both sub-tabs read one source of truth.

### History and Session disagree about the same session: 46h 2m elapsed vs 0s duration

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Session 3 (no end_time, 3 frames over 20 minutes) reads "46h 2m elapsed" on the History card and "DURATION 0s" on the Session tab, at the same moment, in the same app.

**Expected:** One answer, and neither of these. History uses DateTime.now()-startTime for any session without an end time, so a session the app failed to close (crash, power loss - the exact case this matters) accrues fictional elapsed time forever; the Session tab uses 0.

**Repro:** Seed an imaging_sessions row with end_time NULL. Analytics > History (46h 2m) then Analytics > Session with that session selected (0s).

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/analytics_screen/history_cards.dart:65-67 vs analytics_screen/session_tab.dart:113`

**Proposed fix:** Share one helper: elapsed = (endTime ?? lastFrameCapturedAt ?? startTime) - startTime, and render an explicit unfinished badge when endTime is null rather than a number.

### 'Collapse all' paints a solid grey block over the rest of the sequence tree

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicking the header's 'Collapse' toggle on a 28-node sequence collapses the first two rows correctly and then replaces everything below with one opaque light-grey rectangle filling the remaining tree viewport. The header still reports '28 nodes' and the root still reports '5 steps', so only rendering is broken. It persists across re-screenshots 5s and 20s later. Clicking 'Expand' restores normal rendering; collapsing again reproduces it.

**Expected:** Collapse-all shows one compact row per top-level node.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder with a sequence containing at least one nested container (e.g. a Photometry Run template) > click 'Collapse' in the tree header. Wait 5s and look.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/sequence_tree/support_widgets.dart (_CollapseAllToggle) and the tree's collapsed-row rendering path`

**Proposed fix:** Likely a collapsed row reporting an unbounded/zero intrinsic height that the list then paints as a bare Container. Reproduce under a debug build with the layout explorer on a sequence containing a nested container plus a LiveStacking node.

### Library cards count exposure NODES and call them 'exposures'; the same card's preview then says 'No exposures'

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** A saved sequence containing 20 planned frames (Take Exposures 10 + Smart Exposure 10) shows the chip '2 exposures'. The wizard-built M31 Sequence, which plans 10 frames, shows '1 exposures'. The number is a count of nodes whose specificType is an exposure type, presented with a word that every astrophotographer reads as 'frames'. Expanding the same card's Preview shows 'M31 / No exposures', so one card contradicts itself. 'n targets' is also not pluralised ('1 targets').

**Expected:** '20 frames' (matching the Builder toolbar's own frame counter), and a preview that agrees with the chip.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Sequences tab. Compare each card's 'n exposures' chip with the Builder toolbar's 'n frames' for the same sequence, then click the chevron to expand the card preview.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/tabs/sequence_library_tab/sequence_card.dart:243-250; count query at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/database/daos/sequences_dao.dart:151-153`

**Proposed fix:** Persist a frame total alongside estimated_duration_mins (Sequence.totalExposures already computes it with loop multipliers) and label the chip 'frames'. Pluralise targets. Make the preview walk the same node set as the chips.

### Two settings pages disagree about whether a filter wheel exists; per-filter adaptive-exposure limits become unreachable

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With the Simulated Filter Wheel connected (log: 'Simulator connected: id=sim_filterwheel_1 type=FilterWheel', 15:04:48), Settings > Equipment > Autofocus > 'Autofocus Filter Settings' lists 7 filters (L, R, G, B, Ha, OIII, SII), while Settings > Imaging > Adaptive Exposure > 'Per-filter overrides' shows a warning banner 'No filter wheel on active profile - adaptive applies to every capture (mono camera assumption).' The per-filter min/max/enable editor is replaced by that banner, so per-filter adaptive limits cannot be configured at all.

**Expected:** Both pages read the same source of truth. If a filter wheel is connected with named filters, Adaptive Exposure must offer the per-filter editor for those filters (or at minimum say 'no equipment profile saved' rather than asserting there is no filter wheel).

**Repro:** Fresh profile, Skip onboarding. Equipment > 'I'll do it manually' > Discovery Expand > Filter Wheels > Connect (Simulated Filter Wheel). Settings > Imaging > Adaptive Exposure > enable 'Enable global default' -> banner says no filter wheel. Settings > Equipment > Autofocus, scroll to 'Autofocus Filter Settings' -> 7 filters listed.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/adaptive_exposure_settings.dart:315-317 (reads activeEquipmentProfileProvider?.filterNames) vs packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/autofocus_settings.dart:128,756-760 (reads filterWheelStateProvider.filterNames)`

**Proposed fix:** Make the adaptive-exposure per-filter editor fall back to filterWheelStateProvider.filterNames when the active profile has none (same precedence the Autofocus page uses), and reword the banner to name the real cause ('No filter wheel connected and no filters on the active profile').

### 'Auto dark subtraction' in Dark Library is the same switch as 'Auto-calibrate light frames' - turning it off silently kills flat and bias correction too

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Settings > Equipment > Dark Library > 'Auto dark subtraction' ('Automatically subtract matching darks from light frames') and Settings > Equipment > Calibration > 'Auto-calibrate light frames' ('Apply dark, flat, and bias correction to captured images automatically') are one and the same setting (calibration.auto_calibrate). I switched OFF 'Auto dark subtraction' on the Dark Library page; the DB key calibration.auto_calibrate flipped true->false and the Calibration page's 'Auto-calibrate light frames' was then also OFF.

**Expected:** A control labelled 'Auto dark subtraction' must control dark subtraction only, or be labelled to say it is the master calibration switch. As shipped, a user who wants darks off but flats on has no way to express that and is not told what they just switched off.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment > Calibration: turn 'Auto-calibrate light frames' ON (DB calibration.auto_calibrate=true). Settings > Equipment > Dark Library: turn 'Auto dark subtraction' OFF. sqlite3 .../skotiq.db "select value from app_settings where key='calibration.auto_calibrate'" -> false. Return to Calibration: 'Auto-calibrate light frames' is OFF.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/dark_library_settings.dart:81-91 -> packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/dark_library_provider.dart:322,357-364`

**Proposed fix:** Either rename the Dark Library row to 'Auto-calibrate light frames (dark, flat, bias)' and mirror the Calibration subtitle verbatim, or split it into a real per-frame-type gate. Do not keep one boolean behind two contradictory labels.

### File naming pattern accepts unknown variables and path-traversal segments with no validation; the failure only surfaces at capture time

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Settings > Imaging > 'File naming pattern' accepted and persisted '../../../pwned/$NOPE' and later '$TARGET_$BANANA' with no error, no warning and no preview. DB app_settings.file_naming_pattern held the literal strings. The capture pipeline rejects both: expandNamingPattern throws ValidationException for unknown tokens and buildImageFilePath throws for '..' segments - i.e. every frame of the night fails to be written, and the user is only told when the first exposure completes.

**Expected:** Validate on commit: reject unknown $TOKENs and unsafe path segments in the settings field (the pipeline already has the token set and the segment rules), and show the resolved example filename live like NINA does.

**Repro:** Settings > Imaging > click the 'File naming pattern' field, Ctrl+A, type '$TARGET_$BANANA', Enter. Field shows '$TARGET_$BANANA' in normal (non-error) styling. sqlite3 .../skotiq.db "select value from app_settings where key='file_naming_pattern'" -> $TARGET_$BANANA.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/imaging_settings.dart:63-83 (no validator); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/imaging_service.dart:1051-1067 (unknown-token throw) and :1131-1143 (unsafe-segment throw)`

**Proposed fix:** Reuse ImagingService._patternVariables and the segment rules in a SettingsTextInput validator so the field refuses to commit an invalid pattern, and render a live 'M31/Light/M31_L_120s_0001.fits' preview under the row.

### Captured Images grades dark/calibration frames as "Good", inflating the session quality summary

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Session 1 contains 12 lights (8 good, 2 needs-review, 2 poor) and 4 darks with no HFR/star-count/guiding metrics. The summary chips read Good: 12 / Needs Review: 2 / Poor: 2 / Total: 16. Scrolling the rail to the end shows the 4 darks rendered with a green GOOD badge and "75 score", indistinguishable from light frames except for a "—" filter.

**Expected:** The Captured Images summary on the Session tab should count light frames only, or at minimum not assert a quality grade for a frame that has no quality measurement. "Good: 12" is a false count - only 8 frames were assessed good.

**Repro:** Seed session 1 with 12 lights + 4 darks (darks: sensor_temp only, hfr/star_count/guiding NULL). Restart. Analytics > Session > Night A - M31, scroll to Captured Images; shift+wheel the rail to its right end.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/frame_quality_assessment_service.dart:112 `var advisoryScore = image.qualityScore ?? 75.0;` - a frame with every metric null scores 75 and falls through to FrameQualityLevel.good; packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/image_thumbnail_strip.dart:90 assesses widget.images unfiltered`

**Proposed fix:** assessBatch should return a distinct `unassessed` level when hfr, starCount, guidingRms and qualityScore are all null, and the strip should either exclude non-light frames or badge them by frame type.

### Adaptive Conditions accepts all-zero score weights and calls it a readability preference; it actually switches the feature off

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I set transparency/seeing/cloud/wind weights all to 0 (each field's min is 0, and there is no floor on the total). The page persisted {"transparency":0.0,"seeing":0.0,"cloud":0.0,"wind":0.0} and displayed: 'Weights sum to 0.00. The composer renormalizes available axes at runtime, but keeping the total near 1.00 makes the score easier to reason about.' That message is untrue for a zero total: AdaptiveSwapService.compose returns null when weightTotal <= 0, so no conditions score is ever produced and adaptive target swapping silently degrades to 'ConditionsUnknown' forever.

**Expected:** Either refuse to commit a configuration whose total is 0, or say what actually happens: 'All weights are zero - no conditions score can be computed and adaptive swapping will not run.'

**Repro:** Settings > Automation & Safety > Adaptive Conditions > Score weights: set each of the four fields to 0 (click, Ctrl+A, type 0, Enter).

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/adaptive_conditions_settings.dart:65-68 and :271-273 (min:0, no total floor); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/adaptive_swap_service.dart:174 ('if (weightTotal <= 0) return null;')`

**Proposed fix:** Clamp the commit so at least one weight is > 0, and change the warning copy for the zero case to state that the score cannot be computed.

### Fail-closed is the shipped default and the Weather Safety page never tells a sensorless rig what that means

`default` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** safety_fail_mode defaults to failClosed (verified in the fresh DB and at app_settings.dart:82). 'Enable weather safety' correctly defaults OFF -- but the moment a user turns it on, which reads like the responsible thing to do, a rig with no weather device and no safety monitor is treated as unsafe: the app raises a CRITICAL 'Safe the rig', and per the codebase's own WeatherSafetyNoSourceRule 'the run will park and abort shortly after it starts'. The Weather Safety page itself contains NO fail-mode control, NO row showing which weather sources are connected or what they currently read, and NO hint that zero sources are attached. The fail-mode dropdown lives on a different page (Sequencer). The rule's resolution hint literally says 'switch the safety fail mode to fail open in Settings > Automation & Safety' -- a string the user only meets at run-start, not on the page where they made the choice.

**Expected:** The page that owns the master switch should show live source state and the fail-mode consequence. Concretely: a status row at the top ('Weather sources: none connected -- with Fail Closed, runs will park and abort'), the fail-mode selector moved next to the master switch, and turning the master switch on with zero sources should either default that rig to Warn Only or ask.

**Repro:** Fresh profile, nothing connected. Settings > AUTOMATION & SAFETY > Weather Safety. Read the whole page (3 toggles, 3 thresholds, 2 alert-timing numbers) -- nothing mentions sources or fail mode. Toggle 'Enable weather safety' on. Then go to Sequencer to find 'Safety fail mode: Fail Closed (Park)'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/weather_safety_settings.dart:120-175; default at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/settings/app_settings.dart:82; consequence documented at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/weather_safety_rules.dart:9-70`

**Proposed fix:** Add a source-status SettingRow driven by weatherStateProvider/safetyMonitorStateProvider at the top of WeatherSafetySettings, move the SafetyFailMode dropdown onto that page (mirroring it in Sequencer the way auto-park is already mirrored), and when weatherSafetyEnabled flips true with no live source, present a one-time choice rather than a CRITICAL toast.

### Duplicate reports 'Duplicate profile failed' for a duplicate that succeeded, and leaks an internal invariant message to the user

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > (profile) > Duplicate > Duplicate, accepting the pre-filled name. The app reported: 'Duplicate profile failed: Bad state: Profile duplication completed, but profile 5 was absent from the refreshed profile list.' The duplicate had in fact succeeded -- equipment_profiles gained row 5 and the profile list in the same screen shows it (three rows: 'Local Work Do Not Lose' [Active], 'Local Work Do Not Lose (Copy)', 'Local Work Do Not Lose (Copy)'). So the message is false in both halves: the operation did not fail, and profile 5 is not absent from the list. It reads like a post-write assertion racing the provider refresh, and the assertion text -- which names a row id and an internal list -- is shown verbatim to the user.

**Expected:** Report success, or if the refresh really has not landed, retry/await it rather than declaring failure. A user who believes this message will press Duplicate again and end up with a third copy.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > select a profile > overflow > Duplicate > press Duplicate. (I hit it on the second duplicate of the same source profile, i.e. when the proposed name collided with an existing profile.) sqlite3 <data>/skotiq.db 'select id,name from equipment_profiles' shows the new row; the list in the UI shows it too.

**Where:** `the duplicate handler in packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/equipment_profiles_screen_parts/profile_controls.dart and the profiles notifier's post-duplicate lookup`

**Proposed fix:** Await the refreshed state (or look the row up by id in the DAO result) before asserting; never surface a Bad state message as user copy.

### In the Submit-discovery dialog all three network chips are dead on a default install, and only the currently-selected network's blocker is explained

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Submit discovery opens with Network = TNS and an amber line 'TNS submission needs your bot id/name, reporting group, data source, reporter, and api key -- configure them in Settings > Science.' Clicking the AAVSO chip does nothing (verified twice, once with coordinates measured from a cropped region shot so the hit point was unambiguous); TNS stays selected. Preview and 'Submit to TNS' are equally inert -- no dialog, no snackbar, no log line, no file. All three chips are disabled because _formatEnabled is false for each: AAVSO needs an observer code AND a magnitude zero point, MPC needs an observatory code AND a mover-class detection, TNS needs the five bot fields. None of the chips render a disabled affordance, and the panel prints a reason only for the format that happens to be selected, so a user who wants the AAVSO export -- the cheapest one to enable, it needs a single settings field -- gets a chip that silently ignores them and a message about TNS credentials that has nothing to do with their problem.

**Expected:** A disabled chip must look disabled and must say why on hover/press. Better: list all three networks with their individual status ('AAVSO - needs observer code', 'MPC - not a moving object', 'TNS - needs bot key') so the user can see at a glance which one is reachable and what it costs.

**Repro:** 1. Seed a confirmed newSource detection. 2. Analytics > Science > First Light > Submit. 3. Click AAVSO -> nothing; TNS stays selected (/tmp/ns-audit/shots/rsr2-19.png vs rsr2-20.png are identical). 4. Click Preview -> nothing, no snackbar within 1s, nothing in the log.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/transient_report_panel.dart:127-148 and :296-320 (Preview/Copy), surfaced by packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/first_light/widgets/transient_submit_dialog.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Render disabled chips at reduced opacity with a tooltip carrying that format's own _disabledReason, and show a per-format status list instead of a single reason line.

### First Light's 'Could not load candidates' Retry button does not re-query -- the error survives Retry and a tab round-trip, and only a full app restart clears it

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The First Light candidate list threw while decoding one row and rendered 'Could not load candidates / FormatException: Invalid radix-10 number (at character 1) / tile-042' with a Retry button. I then fixed the offending value in the database (tile_id 'tile-042' -> 261982, confirmed typeof=integer) and pressed Retry twice, and switched to the Science sub-tab and back: the identical error text -- including the stale 'tile-042' fragment -- was still on screen. Only killing and restarting the app made the list load. The same staleness affects the success path: after the list HAS loaded, editing a detection's row (kind) outside the app and switching sub-tabs away and back still rendered the old value, so the candidate list is fetched once per app launch and is never refetched.

**Expected:** Retry must invalidate the provider and re-run the query -- that is the entire contract of a Retry button. And the candidate list must refresh as new detections arrive: the panel's own header promises 'Every solved frame is differenced against your deep sky atlas ... Anything that appeared, moved, or brightened on a deep patch lands here to review', which is false if the list is a one-shot read taken at app launch.

**Repro:** 1. Put a row in transient_detections whose tile_id is not parseable (SQLite type affinity permits a TEXT value in the INTEGER column). 2. Analytics > Science > First Light -> 'Could not load candidates'. 3. Fix the row: update transient_detections set tile_id=261982. 4. Press Retry -> same error. 5. Switch to Science and back to First Light -> same error. 6. Restart the app -> the candidate renders correctly.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/first_light/first_light_view.dart (candidate list provider + the Retry action on its error state)`

**Proposed fix:** Make Retry call ref.invalidate on the candidates provider (and make the provider watch the detections table as a stream, so new candidates appear during a run instead of at next launch). Separately, decode a row defensively so one unparseable row is skipped and reported rather than failing the whole list.

### The 'default startup profile' that Settings > General promises has no UI anywhere -- it is pinned to whichever profile you created first

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > General > Auto-connect equipment reads 'Connects the default startup profile on launch, falling back to the last active profile when no default is set'. Equipment Profiles has no notion of a default: the badge says 'Active', the header action says 'Set Active', and the overflow menu is Edit / Duplicate / Export / Delete. Nothing shows or sets a default. In the database equipment_profiles.is_default is set on whichever profile was created first and never moves: after I created a profile (is_default=1), duplicated it twice (both is_default=0) and pressed Set Active on a duplicate, the rows read 3|...|is_active=0|is_default=1, 4|...|is_active=1|is_default=0. So the rig the app says it will connect on launch is not the rig the operator just made active, and there is no screen where that can be seen, let alone changed.

**Expected:** Either show and let the user set the default (a 'Set as default startup profile' item in the overflow, and a 'Default' badge in the list), or drop the concept and make auto-connect use the active profile -- which is what the operator will assume.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > create a profile, duplicate it, select the copy and press Set Active. sqlite3 <data>/skotiq.db 'select id,name,is_active,is_default from equipment_profiles' -> is_default is still on the original. Search all of Settings for 'default profile': the only hit is the General page's description text.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/equipment_profiles_screen_parts/ (no default affordance) vs the auto-connect description in the general settings section`
