**Proposed fix:** Add a 'Set as default' action and a Default badge, and show which profile auto-connect will use on the General page.

### Session tab reports Duration "0s" for any session that has no end_time

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Reviewing seeded session 3 (Night C - M31, status in_progress, start 2026-07-30 20:30Z, end_time NULL, 3 light frames spanning 20.3 minutes), the summary strip shows DURATION 0s while the charts beside it plot a 20-minute time axis.

**Expected:** A session with no end time should say "in progress" / "--", or compute now-minus-start. 0s is a false statement contradicted by the charts on the same card.

**Repro:** sqlite3 seed an imaging_sessions row with end_time NULL and captured_images spanning 20 min; restart app; Analytics > Session; the review bar auto-selects that session.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/analytics_screen/session_tab.dart:113 formatDuration((reviewSession.endTime ?? reviewSession.startTime).difference(reviewSession.startTime))`

**Proposed fix:** When endTime is null, use DateTime.now() for a session whose status is in_progress, else render Unfinished.

### Builder and Pre-Flight disagree about how long the same sequence takes: '~18m 54s' vs 'Duration 27m'

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The identical 7-step sequence (Cool 15C, Slew, Autofocus, 4x3s lights, Unpark, Park, Warm 20C @2C/min) is estimated at '~18m 54s' by the estimate chip in the Builder header and at 'Duration 27m / Ends 11:20' by the Pre-Flight Simulation panel, both visible in the same screenshot with the dialog open over the builder. That is a 43% disagreement. The header estimate also did not move at all when I changed the Cool Camera setpoint from -10C to +15C (ambient 20C), i.e. after removing ~25C of cooling work the builder still printed exactly ~18m 54s.

**Expected:** One estimate, computed once. Session planning (will this finish before dawn?) depends on it, and the pre-flight's 'Ends 11:20' is what a user schedules around.

**Repro:** Builder: add Cool Camera / Slew / Autofocus / Take Exposures 4x3s / Unpark / Park / Warm Camera. Read the estimate chip next to the sequence header. Press Start and read Simulation > Duration.

**Where:** `Sequencer > Builder header estimate vs Start > Pre-Flight > Simulation`

**Proposed fix:** Have both surfaces call the same estimator; if the pre-flight adds slew/settle/overheads the builder does not, show the same breakdown in both instead of two bare numbers.

### False zeros: guiding peak error reads 0.00" with no guider, stack metrics read 0.00 px with no stack

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Imaging -> Guiding tab with the guider Disconnected: RA RMS, Dec RMS and Total all correctly show '---', but RA Peak and Dec Peak show '0.00"'. A 0.00" peak error is a claim of perfect guiding. The same pattern is in the Stack tab with no stack in progress: 'Avg Alignment Residual 0.00 px' and 'Avg Matched Pairs 0.0' while 'Rejection Rate' correctly shows '--'. Two panels, same bug shape: uninitialised numerics rendered as real measurements.

**Expected:** Every no-data numeric renders as '---', exactly the way RMS and Rejection Rate already do in the same cards.

**Repro:** 1. Leave guider disconnected, no stack running. 2. Imaging -> Guiding tab: read RA Peak / Dec Peak. 3. Imaging -> Stack tab: read Avg Alignment Residual / Avg Matched Pairs.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/guiding_panel.dart and packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/stacking_panel.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Make the peak/residual/pairs fields nullable end-to-end and render null as '---' rather than defaulting them to 0.

### Continue Session dialog dates yesterday's session as 'Today at 14:57'

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Relaunched the app on 2026-08-02 at 10:40 local. The Continue Session dialog header reads 'Today at 14:57'. The session it refers to is sequence_runs id=2, ended_at=1785610650 = Sat Aug 1 14:57:30 EDT - i.e. YESTERDAY. The dashboard card behind the same dialog gets it right ('Completed - 19 hours ago') and the app's own date header says 'Sun Aug 2'. So two surfaces one dialog apart disagree about which day the run happened, and the modal one is wrong.

**Expected:** 'Yesterday at 14:57' (or the date). A handoff dialog exists to tell you whether the state it is about to restore is from the night you are still in; getting the day wrong defeats its only job.

**Repro:** Run a sequence, quit, relaunch the app the next calendar day, read the Continue Session dialog header.

**Where:** `App launch > Continue Session dialog header`

**Proposed fix:** Format the timestamp with a calendar-day comparison (today / yesterday / explicit date) instead of assuming the last session is from the current day.

### The Continue Session handoff dialog says 'Unknown Profile' and 'No Sequence' while naming the sequence in its own header and with a real profile active

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Relaunching the app after a night's session shows the Continue Session dialog titled 'New Sequence', with the rows 'Equipment Profile: Unknown Profile' and 'Sequence: No Sequence', above a primary button 'Load Previous Setup'. The scratch DB has exactly one equipment profile named 'My Equipment' (which the status bar shows as active) and one sequence named 'New Sequence' (which the dialog's own header prints, and which the dashboard card behind it names too). Both fields the dialog needs to restore are unresolved nulls: quick_start_dialog.dart:184-185 falls back to 'Unknown Profile'/'No Sequence' when quickStartContext.profileName/sequenceName are null.

**Expected:** 'Equipment Profile: My Equipment', 'Sequence: New Sequence'. This is the dialog whose whole purpose is telling you what it is about to restore; if it cannot name it, the user cannot trust the button.

**Repro:** Run a sequence, quit the app, relaunch on the same profile. Read the Continue Session dialog.

**Where:** `App launch > Continue Session (quick start / session handoff) dialog`

**Proposed fix:** Populate quickStartContext.profileName/sequenceName from the persisted last-run row (sequence_runs.sequence_name and the active profile) instead of from in-memory state that is empty on a cold start.

### sequence_runs.status stays 'running' while the run is paused, so any non-GUI consumer thinks the rig is still exposing

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P3, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** During a 52-second pause of run 2 the DB row kept status='running'. The GUI knew it was paused, but the persisted state (which the headless API, the web dashboard and the mobile client read) did not distinguish paused from running.

**Expected:** A 'paused' status (or a paused_at column) so remote surfaces can show the true state.

**Repro:** Pause a run, then: sqlite3 <profile>/data/skotiq.db "select status from sequence_runs where id=2;"

**Where:** `sequence_runs table / run state persistence`

**Proposed fix:** Persist the paused state on pause and clear it on resume.

### The top-bar run chip still reads 'Sequence Running' (green) while the sequence is paused

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With run 2 paused at 3/4 exposures (toolbar chip 'Paused 75%', node badge 'PAUSED', Pause button flipped to Resume, and no further frames being captured), the chip in the Sequencer's top tab bar - the app's most prominent run indicator - still showed a green dot and the text 'Sequence Running'. The accessibility tree confirms both strings are present simultaneously: 'panel: Sequence Running' and 'panel: Paused 75%'.

**Expected:** The top-bar chip should read Paused (amber) when the run is paused. Two indicators one row apart must not disagree about whether the rig is exposing.

**Repro:** Start a sequence, wait for exposures, click Pause, read the chip next to the Builder/Templates/Sequences/History tabs and compare with the toolbar chip.

**Where:** `Sequencer > top tab bar run chip`

**Proposed fix:** Drive the chip from the same run-state enum as the toolbar chip instead of a boolean isRunning.

### Campaign Rollup shows "Effective imaging 0.0%" when no session in the campaign has closed

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** meanEffectiveImagingFraction is `simpleMean(efficiencyInputs) ?? 0.0` and efficiencyInputs only accepts sessions with an end_time. A campaign whose only session is still open therefore renders "Effective imaging 0.0%" - a measured-looking claim that no imaging happened - while the neighbouring "Mean seeing" correctly renders "-" for the same missing-data case. REPRODUCED LIVE in this pass: the M13 campaign (whose only session, Night E, is still status='active' with end_time NULL) renders 'Effective imaging 0.0%' beside 'Mean seeing -' - the two tiles handling the same missing-data case in opposite ways, side by side.

**Expected:** "-", as the tile beside it already does for absent data.

**Repro:** Give a target exactly one session and leave it status='active' with end_time NULL. Analytics > Science > click the target's campaign card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/campaign_rollup_service.dart:166 and campaign_rollup_dialog.dart:198-201`

**Proposed fix:** Make meanEffectiveImagingFraction nullable and render - like Mean seeing.

### The Share Card's metadata strip is clipped: it renders 'M51  Integration 00:35:00  Frames 7  Fil…' and the filter is cut off the right edge

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Stack result > Share Card exports a 512x512 RGB PNG whose bottom caption bar reads 'M51   Integration  00:35:00   Frames  7   Fil' and then hits the right edge mid-word. The caption is laid out at a font size derived from the image HEIGHT (ShareCardSpec.fontScale defaults to ShareCardFontScale.scaleToHeight) with no measurement against the available WIDTH, so any card narrower than the caption needs simply truncates. This is the artefact the feature exists to produce -- the thing a user posts publicly -- and it ships with the text running off the edge.

**Expected:** Either measure the caption and shrink the font / wrap to a second line until it fits the card width, or render the card on a fixed-size canvas (e.g. 1200x1200 or 1200x675) with the image letterboxed, so the caption geometry is independent of the stack's pixel dimensions.

**Repro:** 1. Produce any stack result whose image is 512px wide. 2. Stack result screen > 'Share Card' > save to a .png. 3. Open the PNG and read the bottom strip.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/imaging/stack_and_share_models.dart:644-676 ShareCardSpec (layout bottomBar, fontScale scaleToHeight); composition in packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/stack_share_export_service.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Add a width-fitting pass: measure the composed caption at the height-derived size and reduce the size (or split into two rows) until it fits within the card width minus padding. Add a regression test that renders a 512-wide card and asserts the caption's measured width is <= card width.

### Sequence checkpoints and logs are written to the platform app-support directory, ignoring the configured data directory

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR and SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR both pointed at /tmp/ns-audit/settings-imaging/data, the app logged 'Setting checkpoint directory to: /home/scdouglas/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop' and 'Log directory: /home/scdouglas/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/logs'. skotiq_session.checkpoint in that shared directory was being rewritten by my sandboxed instance. The visible consequence: on entering the app I was offered 'Recover Sequence? ... New Sequence, Saved 0m ago, Completed 6 frames (0m integration)' for a sequence that does not exist in this profile's database (select * from sequence_checkpoints returned zero rows).

**Expected:** Checkpoint and log paths must follow the same data-directory resolution as the database, so a second profile/data-dir never reads or overwrites another instance's run state.

**Repro:** Start the app with SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR set to a scratch path; read /tmp/ns-audit/<profile>/app.log for the two 'directory' lines; ls -la ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/ shows skotiq_session.checkpoint mtime tracking the sandboxed run.

**Where:** `source-only beyond the log lines - the checkpoint-directory call site is in the native bridge startup path, not in my area's Dart files`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the checkpoint and log roots from the same SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR-aware helper the database uses.

### Sequence Library card reports 10m for a 20-frame sequence - Smart Exposure integration is dropped from the stored duration

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Built a sequence containing one Take Exposures (10 x 60s) and one Smart Exposure (L, 10 x 60s). The Builder header estimated '~39m 45s' and the toolbar counted '20 frames'. Saving it to the library produced a card reading '2 exposures - 10m', and sequences.estimated_duration_mins in the DB is 10. Bare integration alone is 20 x 60s = 20 minutes. The stored figure equals exactly the ExposureNode contribution (600s); the SmartExposureNode contributes nothing.

**Expected:** The card duration should reflect all imaging nodes. 20 min integration (or the ~40 min run estimate the Builder shows) - anything but half.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > add 'Take Exposures' and 'Smart Exposure' from the palette. Note the Builder header estimate and the toolbar frame count. Sequences tab > Save Current > name it > Save. Read the new card's duration chip, then: sqlite3 /tmp/ns-audit/<profile>/data/skotiq.db 'select name,estimated_duration_mins from sequences;'

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/sequence/sequence_models/sequence_state.dart:315-322 (_estimateNodeIntegration handles ExposureNode only); persisted via packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/sequence_repository.dart:208 and :239`

**Proposed fix:** Add a SmartExposureNode branch to _estimateNodeIntegration using node.totalIntegrationSecs (already defined at smart_exposure_node.dart:84), mirroring what Sequence.totalExposures already does for the frame count. Note the same stored value feeds LongEstimatedDurationRule (settings_rules.dart:158) and the disk-space rule, so both are currently under-estimating.

### A profile saved without optics can never be edited again: Save is rejected because of the 0 the app itself stored, and the rejection is invisible on screen

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** New Profile writes focal_length=0.0 / aperture=0.0. Re-open that profile, press Edit, change ONLY the name, press Save: nothing happens. No banner, no field-level error, no colour change -- the button appears dead. I clicked Save five times over three minutes and the DB never changed (still 'Backup Test Rig' while the field read 'Local Work Do Not Lose'). The failure is real and has a message -- the accessibility tree at that moment contains 'Focal length must be a positive number (leave blank if unknown)' and 'Aperture must be a positive number (leave blank if unknown)' -- but a full-window screenshot taken between the click and that tree read shows no such text anywhere on screen (/tmp/ns-audit/shots/rss-67.png), and neither does a full-resolution capture of the bottom 160px where the snackbar would sit (/tmp/ns-audit/shots/rss-snack-1.png). The snackbar is rendered underneath the persistent bottom status bar. Clearing both optics fields let the save through -- and it stored 0.0 again, so the profile is right back in the unsavable state next time.

**Expected:** Blank optics must round-trip as blank/NULL, not as 0.0 that the validator then rejects; and a rejected save must show its reason where the user is looking (inline under the offending field, plus a visible banner). As shipped, the very first thing a user does after creating a profile -- open it and fill something in -- hits a dead Save button with no explanation.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > New Profile > name 'X' > Create. Overflow menu > Edit > change the name > Save. Nothing happens; sqlite3 <data>/skotiq.db 'select name,focal_length,aperture from equipment_profiles' still shows the old name and 0.0/0.0. Now clear Focal Length and Aperture and press Save: it saves, and stores 0.0/0.0 again.

**Where:** `validator: ProfileValidator.parseOptional... (focal/aperture 'must be a positive number') reached from packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/equipment_profiles_screen_parts/profile_details.dart:175-200; the empty-field -> 0.0 write is in the same save path; the snackbar is shown by the settings scaffold underneath the app's bottom status bar.`

**Proposed fix:** Persist blank optics as NULL (the column is already nullable in the export -- focalLength shows as a number only when set), treat 0 as 'unset' on read, and render validation failures inline on the fields plus a banner above the status bar.

### Planets are typed as 'Star' in the object popup and written to the observation log that way, and the popup shows the internal id 'PLANET_Jupiter'

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicking Jupiter opens a popup headed 'Jupiter' with an identifier chip reading 'PLANET_Jupiter', 'mag -1.8' and a 'Type' field that says 'Star'. Logging an observation from that popup persists the same mistake: the row written to observation_logs is (object_name 'Jupiter', object_type 'star', catalog_id 'PLANET_Jupiter', ra 8.65857 h, dec 18.898, altitude 38.68, azimuth 97.48, rating 3, seeing good, transparency good, latitude 40, longitude -105). So the app's permanent observing record classifies a planet as a star, and the user-visible catalogue identifier is an internal enum token rather than a designation anyone would recognise.

**Expected:** Type should read 'Planet' and the chip should show something meaningful (or nothing) rather than the internal key. object_type in observation_logs should be 'planet' so the log can later be filtered or exported (an observing log is data the user keeps for years).

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > press home to centre the zenith > click Jupiter. Read the Type row and the chip. Then Log Observation > Log Observation, and read the observation_logs table.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/widgets/object_info_popup.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Map the synthetic planet objects to a 'planet' object type at the point they are constructed, and hide/replace the PLANET_* catalog id in the popup chip.

### Deleting your last equipment profile leaves a ghost profile on screen whose 'Set Active' throws an unhandled exception and does nothing

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Equipment Profiles with exactly one profile ('Audit Rig Alpha'): overflow > Delete > Delete. equipment_profiles goes to 0 rows and the list column correctly reads 'No profiles yet' -- but the detail pane on the right keeps rendering the deleted profile in full (name, f/8.0, gain 100, all four filters, focus offsets) with a live 'Set Active' button and a live overflow menu. Two surfaces in the same screen disagree about whether the profile exists. Clicking 'Set Active' produces no toast, no error, no visible change: it throws [ERROR] Unhandled Exception: Bad state: Cannot activate profile 1: no such profile row (activateProfileStrictTransactional) straight into the log. Clicking Edit puts the ghost into an editable form; typing a new name and pressing Save shows 'Failed to save profile: Exception: Profile not found' and the typed work is lost.

**Expected:** Deleting the selected profile must clear the selection when nothing is left -- the pane should fall back to the 'Select a profile / Choose a profile from the list or create a new one' empty state (which the app already has and shows before the first profile is created). No control should remain that acts on a deleted row, and a failed activation must never be an unhandled exception.

**Repro:** Fresh profile > Settings > Equipment Profiles > New Profile 'X' > Create > overflow (three dots) > Delete > Delete. Observe list 'No profiles yet' + full detail pane still shown. Click 'Set Active'. sqlite3 <data>/skotiq.db 'select count(*) from equipment_profiles' -> 0. drive_linux.py log -> Unhandled Exception: Bad state: Cannot activate profile 1: no such profile row.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/equipment_profiles_screen_parts/screen_shell.dart:232 (onSetActive -> _activateProfile, unawaited/uncaught) and packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/profiles_provider.dart:980 -> profile_activation_writethrough.dart:156`

**Proposed fix:** In the profiles notifier, null out the selected profile when the deleted id is the selected one and the list is empty; guard the detail pane on 'selected profile still exists in state.profiles'; wrap _activateProfile in a try/catch that surfaces the failure as a snackbar.

### A rig with no filter wheel cannot create any integration goal, so the autopilot can never be given a target

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With the saved profile 'My First Rig' (sim camera + sim mount, filter wheel deliberately skipped — the normal OSC/DSLR/mono-without-wheel case), opening a queue row's target editor shows the Integration goals section with the message 'Equipment profile has no filters configured; configure a filter wheel first.' and NO 'Add goal' control of any kind. Since the scheduler's own empty state states 'The scheduler needs targets with integration goals', a wheel-less rig can never populate the Target queue and the Unattended Autopilot is unusable. (I could only exercise the editor at all by inserting an integration_goals row directly into the scratch DB.)

**Expected:** A profile with no filter wheel should be able to set a single unfiltered goal. The Smart Night builder already handles exactly this case — packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/smart_night_service.dart:80-98 comments 'An empty filter list is the normal state of an OSC rig with no wheel — Smart Night plans a single unfiltered row for it' — so the scheduler and the sequence builder disagree about whether wheel-less rigs are supported.

**Repro:** 1. Onboarding: skip the Filter wheel step, save the profile. 2. Plan Tonight > Schedule > click a queued target row (needs a target in the active project) -> Integration goals section. 3. Observe the warning and the absence of any add-goal affordance.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/scheduler/widgets/integration_goals_editor.dart (the add-goal control is gated on a non-empty filter list); mirrored in packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/scheduler_provider.dart:551-560 (_availableFilters returns profile.filterNames)`

**Proposed fix:** When the active profile has no filter wheel, offer a single synthetic unfiltered goal slot (label it 'No filter', matching the chip the Recommendation card already renders) instead of blocking the editor, and stop treating an empty filter list as a mis-configuration. Keep the hard error only for the real fault the Smart Night builder already distinguishes: a wheel that IS connected but reports zero slots.

### False "Rises late" warning on a target that is up all night, and the same card gives two different rise times

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Night Outlook hero card for M92 shows the warning '⚠ Rises late at 11:54' while the chart footer of the same card shows 'Rise: 12:01  Transit: 21:34  Set: 07:06' and the chips say 'Peak in dark 84.0°, 5.3h visible'. Two problems on one card: (a) the warning is false — at astronomical dark (22:01) M92 is already above 80°, it is up for the entire imaging window; (b) the warning's rise time (11:54) and the footer's rise time (12:01) disagree by 7 minutes for the same event.

**Expected:** No 'rises late' warning for a target that clears the minimum altitude at the start of astronomical darkness. And one rise time per card.

**Repro:** Site 40/-75, Plan Tonight > Recommendation, hero card = M92 (top-scored on 2026-08-01), scroll to the warning rows under the chips.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/planning/target_scoring.dart:1006-1024 (warning compares visibility.riseTime against nightStart with no check that the rise is in daylight / on the correct day)`

**Proposed fix:** Same root cause as the P0 window bug: visibility.riseTime comes from the noon-anchored calculateObjectVisibility and can name a rise on the wrong day or in broad daylight. Gate the warning on the target's actual altitude at nightStart (if alt >= minAltitude at nightStart, there is nothing to warn about) rather than on a rise timestamp, and source both the warning and the footer from the same visibility object.

### Target-queue empty state misdiagnoses the cause: tells you to add catalog targets and goals when you already have them

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With 3 targets in the catalog, each carrying an integration goal, the Schedule tab's Target queue showed 'No targets to schedule — The scheduler needs targets with integration goals. Add a target to your catalog, then set how many frames you want in each filter.' and the decision panel said 'No candidate targets available'. The real cause was that the active project (planning.active_project_id=1, created earlier through the app's own New Project button) had zero members, and the scheduler candidate query is project-scoped. Adding the same three targets to the project made all three appear immediately with scores and status.

**Expected:** When the catalog is non-empty but the active project is empty, say so: 'This project has no targets — add some, or clear the active project to schedule from the whole catalog', with a link to the project. The current text sends the user to do work they have already done.

**Repro:** 1. Plan Tonight > Projects > New Project (leave it empty; it becomes the active project). 2. Get at least one target with an integration goal into the catalog. 3. Plan Tonight > Schedule > Re-evaluate -> empty queue with the wrong message. 4. Add the target to the project -> rows appear.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planner/widgets/scheduler_tab_content/empty_state.dart:22; scoping at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/scheduler_provider.dart:315-343`

**Proposed fix:** Distinguish the three empty cases in the empty state — (a) catalog empty, (b) catalog non-empty but no goals, (c) goals exist but the active project has no members — and name the active project in case (c). The candidate loader already knows which of these it is.

### Alert Settings presents MPEC and CBAT as enabled alert sources, but no code ever queries them

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > Observing Alerts > settings gear shows 'Alert Sources: AAVSO, TNS, MPEC, CBAT, Manual' with AAVSO, MPEC, CBAT and Manual rendered as selected (TNS off). TransientAlertService has no MPEC or CBAT fetch path at all — `grep -n 'mpec\|MPEC\|cbat\|CBAT' transient_alert_service.dart` returns nothing, and the service's own cache-key helper filters the enabled set down to 'implementedSources' = {aavso, tns}. So two of the four upstreams shown as ON are permanently silent no-ops.

**Expected:** Either implement MPEC/CBAT or do not offer them as toggles. At minimum mark them 'not available in this build' and do not enable them by default.

**Repro:** Analytics > Science > Observing Alerts > gear icon. Observe MPEC and CBAT highlighted as enabled.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/alerts/transient_alert.dart:128-134 (defaults include TransientSource.mpec and .cbat); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/transient_alert_service.dart:843-864 (only aavso and tns are dispatched)`

**Proposed fix:** Drop mpec/cbat from the default enabledSources set and either hide those chips or render them disabled with a 'coming soon' tooltip, so the settings sheet stops asserting that four sources are being monitored when two are inert.

### Observing Alerts refresh is a silent no-op and the screen can never show anything

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Observing Alerts screen says 'Real targets for citizen science' and 'Transient alerts from AAVSO, TNS, and other sources will appear here when available.' Clicking the refresh icon produces no spinner, no last-refreshed timestamp, no result count, no error, and no row — before and after, at +3s and +15s, the screen is byte-identical. transient_detections stayed at 0. Enabling TNS (which requires a bot API key that this sheet has no field for) and refreshing again also produced no error. Nothing is written to the app log. Separately, the only implemented broadband source is called at a URL that cannot return anything useful: `https://www.aavso.org/vsx/index.php?view=api.list&format=json&maxrec=50` 301-redirects to vsx.aavso.org and then returns exactly `{"VSXObjects":[]}` (verified by curl). So the surface is permanently empty and the operator has no way to tell 'no transients tonight' from 'this feature is broken'.

**Expected:** Refresh should show it is working (spinner), report what happened ('AAVSO: 0 alerts, TNS: skipped — no API key, checked 11:16'), and fail loud when an enabled source cannot be reached. TransientAlertFetchException already exists for this; nothing surfaces it.

**Repro:** 1. Analytics > Science > Observing Alerts. 2. Click the refresh icon; screenshot at +3s and +15s. 3. Gear > enable TNS > Done > refresh again. 4. `sqlite3 <profile>/data/skotiq.db 'select count(*) from transient_detections'` -> 0.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/transient_alert_service.dart:80-81 (_aavsoApiUrl) and :843-870 (getAllAlerts); refresh action in packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/transients/transients_screen.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Point AAVSO at https://vsx.aavso.org/ and at a query that actually selects recent alerts (api.list with no selector returns an empty set by design). Add a per-source status strip to the screen header: source name, last fetch time, alert count or error. Surface TransientAlertFetchException in the UI instead of swallowing it, and tell the user when TNS is enabled but unkeyed.

### 'Active Viewers' on the Remote Access page counts co-imaging collaborators, not connected remote clients -- it stays 0 with a live dashboard socket open

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Connection details is introduced as 'Open this section when you need viewer counts, authentication state, or the last runtime error' and shows 'Active Viewers'. With a paired client holding an authenticated WebSocket on /events (verified: HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols, socket ESTAB in ss for the whole window) the panel read 'Active Viewers 0' throughout. The field is only ever set from the collaboration manager's viewer list (desktop_app_bootstrap.dart:346-349 -> collabState.viewers.length), i.e. co-imaging participants, not remote-access clients. The same field drives the status-bar 'N viewers connected' chip, so that indicator is dead for ordinary phone/browser clients too.

**Expected:** On the Remote Access page 'Active Viewers' must mean 'remote clients currently connected to this server'. Count the live event-stream/WS sessions; if the co-imaging number is wanted too, label it separately.

**Repro:** Enable remote access, pair a client, POST /api/ws/ticket, connect ws to /events?ticket=..., hold. Settings > Remote Access > Connection details: Active Viewers 0.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/remote_access_settings.dart:520-524; fed only by apps/desktop/lib/desktop_app_bootstrap.dart:346-349; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/web_server_provider.dart:193 setActiveViewers.`

**Proposed fix:** Publish a connected-client count from the headless API server's WS/SSE registry into webServerStateProvider and bind the row to that.

### Paired devices always say 'Not seen yet / No connection recorded yet' -- last_connected_at is never written

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Every one of the 13 paired devices shows the badge 'Not seen yet' and the line 'No connection recorded yet'. I proved this is untrue for at least one of them: after pairing 'audit-probe' I made three authenticated GET /api/status calls with its bearer (200, 200, 200) and then opened an authenticated WebSocket to /events (HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols, held open 60s). paired_devices.last_connected_at for that device stayed NULL and the UI still says 'Not seen yet'. All 12 pre-existing devices -- including ones that demonstrably drove this app in earlier sessions ('ui-audit-avd', 'Lead Harness') -- are likewise NULL.

**Expected:** last_connected_at should be stamped whenever a device authenticates. Without it the operator cannot tell a phone that is still in use from a stale credential, which makes the revoke list unusable: 7 of the 13 rows are named 'Android companion' with identical subtitles and no last-seen, IP or model to tell them apart.

**Repro:** Pair a device via POST /api/pairing/verify, call GET /api/status with the returned bearer (200), then sqlite3 ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db "select device_name,last_connected_at from paired_devices where device_id='audit-probe-rss'" -> last_connected_at empty. Settings > Remote Access > Manage Pairing > refresh: row still reads 'No connection recorded yet'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_remote_protocol/lib/src/database/pairing_database.dart (no update of last_connected_at on the auth path) / apps/desktop/lib/headless_api_server/http_middleware.dart token resolution.`

**Proposed fix:** Stamp last_connected_at (throttled, e.g. at most once a minute per device) when a bearer resolves, and surface it plus the peer IP in the device row.

### After a device pairs successfully the desktop keeps showing the pairing code counting down, and the device list does not update

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I pressed Start Pairing Mode, read the code ASTRO-NEBULA-1815 off the screen, and completed pairing over HTTP (POST /api/pairing/verify -> 200 with a bearer token). The server immediately invalidated the code (a second verify with the same code returned invalid_pairing_code) and wrote the 13th row into paired_devices. The desktop screen did not react at all: 30s later it still displayed 'Enter this code on your device: ASTRO-NEBULA-1815 / Expires in 04:18 / Cancel Pairing', and Paired Devices still listed 12 entries. Only after manually pressing the small refresh icon did the 13th device appear.

**Expected:** The instant a code is consumed the panel should close and announce which device paired -- that confirmation is the operator's only way to know the phone in their hand is the device that just connected. Showing a live countdown for a code the server has already destroyed is a false statement, and it invites the operator to read the dead code to a second device.

**Repro:** Settings > Remote Access > Manage Pairing > Start Pairing Mode. From a shell: curl -X POST -d '{"code":"<code>","deviceName":"x","deviceId":"x","deviceType":"browser"}' http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/pairing/verify. Watch the desktop: unchanged. sqlite3 ~/Documents/Skotiq/pairing.db 'select count(*) from paired_devices where is_active=1' -> 13 while the UI shows 12.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/pairing_screen.dart:96/215 (loadPairedDevices is only called on init and on the manual refresh button; nothing subscribes to pairing completion).`

**Proposed fix:** Have the pairing session emit a completion event (the server already knows) and drive the panel from it: close the code card, toast '<deviceName> paired', and reload the list.

### After session recovery the imaging Session card says 'Captured 0 frames' while the line under it says 'Integration 40m 0s'

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Resuming an interrupted session (Incomplete Sessions Found > Resume, on a session with 8 successful exposures and 2400s integration) leaves the Imaging screen's Session card reading 'Captured  0 frames' directly above 'Integration  40m 0s', 'Status Active', 'Duration 87:14:28'. Zero frames and forty minutes of integration cannot both be true. The card reads the frame count from sessionImages.length -- the list of frames captured in THIS app run, which is empty after a restart -- while the integration line reads the restored sessionState.totalIntegrationSecs.

**Expected:** Both numbers must come from the same source. After recovery the card should read 'Captured 8 frames / Integration 40m 0s' (SessionStateNotifier.recoverSession already restores completedExposures = 8 from the DB row; the same field is used correctly by the detail panel's 'Images Captured:' line at capture_panel.dart:427).

**Repro:** 1. sqlite3 skotiq.db "update imaging_sessions set status='active', end_time=null where id=3". 2. Restart the app. 3. 'Incomplete Sessions Found' > Resume. 4. Imaging > read the Session card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/capture_panel.dart:201-208 (Captured = sessionImages.length) vs :221-227 (Integration = sessionState.totalIntegrationSecs)`

**Proposed fix:** Render the count from sessionState.completedExposures (falling back to sessionImages.length only when no session row is open), so the two lines cannot disagree.

### Frames captured under a named target are reported as 'Untargeted', and the run's target breakdown is keyed by the SEQUENCE name instead of the target name

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The sequence had exactly one Target node called 'New Target' and every exposure ran underneath it (the FITS OBJECT card and the filenames both say 'New Target'). The Session Report's Targets section shows a single group titled 'Untargeted - 4/4 frames | 12s'. sequence_runs.stats_json targetBreakdown is {"New Sequence": {"Unknown": {...}}} - keyed by the sequence's name, not the target's. captured_images.target_id is NULL for all 4 rows.

**Expected:** 'New Target - 4/4 frames', targetBreakdown keyed 'New Target', and captured_images.target_id pointing at that target.

**Repro:** Build Target 'New Target' with Take Exposures under it, run, open the Session Report and read the Targets section; then read stats_json.targetBreakdown.

**Where:** `Sequencer > Session Report > Targets; sequence_runs.stats_json`

**Proposed fix:** Attribute each frame to the enclosing TargetHeaderNode (the executor already knows it - the filename template resolves the target name) and persist the target id on the captured_images row.

### Multi-select highlights three rows but the batch bar and the delete confirmation both say two

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicked 'Change Filter' (becomes the primary selection, shown in Properties), then Ctrl+clicked 'Live Stacking' and 'Center Target'. All three rows render with an identical cyan selection border at full zoom, but the batch toolbar reads '2 selected' and pressing its Delete opens 'Delete 2 node(s)?'. The primary selection is drawn as selected while being excluded from every batch operation, and there is no visual difference between the two states.

**Expected:** Either the primary selection joins the multi-select set (count 3), or it is drawn differently (e.g. outline vs fill) so the count and the highlight agree.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > click a node row > Ctrl+click two more rows > compare the highlighted row count with the '<n> selected' chip, then click Delete and read the dialog title.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/batch_operations_toolbar.dart; row selection styling in widgets/sequence_tree/node_item.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Make the first Ctrl+click seed the multi-select set with the existing primary selection (the behaviour every file manager has), or give multiSelectedNodeIds a visually distinct row treatment.

### Target header card under-reports planned exposures and integration time by the loop multiplier - including for every sequence the app's own Quick-Start Wizard builds

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** I ran the Quick-Start Wizard end to end (M31, 120s, count 10). It produced a Capture Loop with repeatCount 10 containing a single 'Light 1 x 120s' exposure node, all under the M31 TargetHeader. The toolbar reported '10 frames' and the DB stored estimated_duration_mins = 20 (correct). The M31 target header card on the same screen reported 'Ready / 1 planned exposures - 2m'. Ten-fold understatement of both the frame count and the integration time, on the card the user reads to decide whether the night's plan is right.

**Expected:** '10 planned exposures - 20m', matching the toolbar frame count and the stored estimate.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Templates > Quick-Start Wizard. Step 1: RA 0.712, Dec 41.27, name M31. Next through steps 2-5 with defaults. Create Sequence. Read the M31 target card status line, then scroll down to see 'Capture Loop x10 > Light 1 x 120s', then read the toolbar frame counter.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/target_header_card.dart:679-706 (_calculateTargetPlan visit())`

**Proposed fix:** Give visit() a multiplier argument and multiply by LoopNode.repeatCount for count-type loops, exactly as Sequence._countExposures does at sequence_state.dart:168-188. Better: delete _calculateTargetPlan and reuse the model's counting so the two can never diverge again.

### Session report says 'Autofocus 0' after an autofocus that ran 9 points and completed successfully

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Run 1 executed the Autofocus node: the live tree showed 'Autofocus - Point 5 of 9', the log shows 'Autofocus point 9 sample 1/1 completed' and "Child 'Autofocus' completed with status: Success", and the focuser moved from 25000 to 25074. The Session Report's Mount/Focus block shows Autofocus '0', and sequence_runs.stats_json has "autofocusRuns":0. focus_models is also empty (0 rows).

**Expected:** autofocusRuns == 1, and the report should show the resulting focus position / HFR so the user can tell whether focus was achieved.

**Repro:** Add an Autofocus node to a sequence, run it, open the Session Report and read the Autofocus counter; compare with app.log 'completed with status: Success'.

**Where:** `Sequencer > Session Report > Mount & focus block; sequence_runs.stats_json`

**Proposed fix:** Increment the autofocus counter where the AutofocusNode reports Success, not where the (unused) focus-model persistence path runs.

### Four surfaces disagree about which filter a frame was taken through: filename says 'nofilter', DB says 'R', FITS header has no FILTER card, run stats say 'Unknown'

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** For the same 4 frames of run 1: the files on disk are named 'New Target_nofilter_0001.fits'...'_0004.fits'; captured_images.filter = 'R' for all four; the FITS headers contain no FILTER keyword at all; the Session Report's Targets table shows Filter 'R'; and sequence_runs.stats_json targetBreakdown buckets them under filter key 'Unknown'. The live telemetry strip during the run showed 'Filter: R'.

**Expected:** One answer. If the wheel is on slot R, the filename, the FITS FILTER card, the DB column and the run stats all say R.

**Repro:** Connect the simulated filter wheel, run a Take Exposures node with no explicit filter, then compare ls of the capture folder, the FITS header, captured_images.filter, and stats_json.targetBreakdown.

**Where:** `Sequencer run -> file naming / FITS writer / captured_images / run stats`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the filter name once at exposure start and thread that single value into the filename template, the FITS FILTER card, the DB column and the stats bucket.

### The autofocus result overlay never auto-dismisses and follows the user onto every other screen, covering controls

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** After the autofocus run finished at 14:15 a collapsed pill 'AF: HFR 2.10' appeared bottom-right. It was still on screen 23 minutes later, and it is global: it appeared on the Imaging screen, then on the Guiding screen (where it covered the Calibration card), then on Plan Tonight, then on the Framing screen (where it covered the Rotation controls and the framing-tour buttons). Clicking near its X re-EXPANDED it into a full floating 'Autofocus' window with the HFR curve, overlapping the Framing sidebar; only the window's own X closed it. On the Imaging screen the collapsed pill also sat on top of the Dither Settings 'Settle Threshold' value and the Stack panel's 'Sigma Clipping' row, hiding them.

**Expected:** A completion toast auto-dismisses after a few seconds, or at minimum is scoped to the Imaging screen and never overlaps interactive controls.

**Repro:** 1. Imaging -> Focus tab -> Run Autofocus, wait for completion. 2. Navigate to Guiding, Plan Tonight, Framing. The pill follows and overlaps content on each.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/focus_panel.dart (AF result overlay hosted above the router shell rather than inside the imaging route)`

**Proposed fix:** Auto-dismiss after ~10 s, scope the overlay to the Imaging route, and make the collapsed pill's X actually dismiss instead of expanding.

### Exposure Triggers dialog lets you add and Save a trigger, then silently discards it when the selected node cannot hold triggers

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With the root 'Sequence' InstructionSet selected I clicked the toolbar bell (Exposure Triggers). The dialog opened, 'Add Trigger' created 'Guiding RMS > 2.0" -> Pause & Recalibrate', and Save closed the dialog with no error. Reopening the dialog showed 'No triggers configured'. Repeating the identical steps with a Take Exposures node selected persisted the trigger correctly. The button is enabled regardless of selection and the dialog gives no indication which node it is editing.

**Expected:** Either disable the toolbar button (with a tooltip: 'select an exposure node') when the selection cannot hold triggers, or tell the user on Save that nothing was stored. Silently accepting and dropping a safety trigger is the worst of the three options - a user who set 'abort if guiding RMS > 2"' would believe they are protected.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > click the root 'Sequence' row > toolbar bell icon > Add Trigger > Save > click the bell again: 'No triggers configured'. Now add a Take Exposures node, select it, and repeat: the trigger is there on reopen.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/trigger_configuration_dialog.dart; launched from widgets/sequence_toolbar.dart:502-506`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the target exposure node up front; if none, disable the toolbar action and put the reason in the tooltip. Show the owning node's name in the dialog subtitle so the user knows what they are editing.

### Running an A/B recipe preview leaves a full 'Preview... 100%' progress bar pinned under the header forever, so the screen permanently claims a job is running

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Pressing 'Run both' in the A/B recipe compare panel puts a green progress strip labelled 'Preview... 100%' between the Narrative/Workbench toggle and the content. The two previews completed (the panel filled in with 'Frames kept tie 1 vs 1', 'Residual tie 0.00 vs 0.00 px', 'Integration time tie 00:05:00 vs 00:05:00'), but the strip was still on screen 13 minutes later, survived switching to Narrative and back, and is still present in the AT-SPI tree as 'panel: Preview...'.

**Expected:** The strip must clear when the action that raised it finishes. The controller's own comment says a terminal 1.0 fraction 'is left on screen until the action's own completion handler clears it' -- integrate() does clear it (clearProgress/clearLiveProgress) but reIntegratePreview(), which is what the A/B panel calls, has no such handler, so the strip is orphaned.

**Repro:** 1. Session Review > Workbench > A / B compare > 'Run both'. 2. Wait for both preview cards to fill in. 3. The strip under the header still reads 'Preview... 100%'. 4. Toggle to Narrative and back -- still there. 5. `drive_linux.py tree | grep Preview` still returns 'panel: Preview...'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/session_review/session_review_controller.dart:515 _bindProgress() and :976 reIntegratePreview() (no clearProgress on completion); rendered by session_review_screen.dart:119 _ProgressStrip`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap reIntegratePreview (and the other seam-driven actions: runColorCalibration, runBackgroundExtraction, runDeconvolve, runStarReduction, runNarrowband) in the same try/finally that clears progress on exit, or clear progress centrally whenever `busy` transitions back to false.

### The 'Maximum Quality' integration profile is a placebo -- it produces byte-identical settings to Balanced

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Selecting each of the five integration profiles in turn and diffing the whole ADVANCED block of the accessibility tree: Fast changes the resampler to Bilinear, Few Subs changes rejection to Percentile clip with 0.2/0.1, Long Night / Gradient changes normalization Mode from Global to Local grid -- but Balanced and Maximum Quality produce an IDENTICAL 30-line block (`diff bal.txt maxq.txt` is empty) and an identical summary sentence. The source confirms it: IntegrationSettings.preset(maximumQuality) sets resampler=lanczos3, weighting=snrSquared, generateRejectionMap=true, cosmeticCorrection=true, outputBitDepth=f32 -- every one of which is already the constructor default that balanced inherits. The only field that differs between the two presets is sourcePreset, which is a label.

**Expected:** A profile named 'Maximum Quality' must actually cost something in exchange for quality -- e.g. a tighter RANSAC threshold, more reference stars, local normalization, linear-fit/percentile rejection tuned for the sub count, or drizzle. As shipped, a user who deliberately picks Maximum Quality over Balanced for their best data gets exactly the same stack and is misled about it.

**Repro:** 1. Session Review > Workbench > Integration settings > expand Advanced settings. 2. Click 'Balanced', capture the ALIGNMENT..DRIZZLE block of `drive_linux.py tree`. 3. Click 'Maximum Quality', capture again. 4. diff -> no differences. 5. Repeat for 'Long Night / Gradient' -> exactly one line differs (Global -> Local grid), proving the capture method detects real changes.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/imaging/integration_settings.dart:737 IntegrationSettings.preset(IntegrationPreset.maximumQuality)`

**Proposed fix:** Give maximumQuality settings that actually differ (e.g. ransacThresholdPx 1.5, maxRefStars 120, normalization local, reject linearFit) -- or drop the chip. Also make the panel's one-line summary mention the knobs the chosen profile changed, so three profiles cannot render the same sentence.

### Remote Access claims 'Localhost stays frictionless for this machine' -- localhost is not exempt from auth at all

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With Enable Remote Access on, the page's footer note reads 'Localhost stays frictionless for this machine, while non-local clients must pair before they can control the app', the 'Open on this computer' card says 'Use this link from the same machine to confirm the dashboard is working before sharing it elsewhere', and Connection details reports Authentication = 'Paired devices required remotely'. All three say local is special. It is not: from 127.0.0.1, GET /api/status -> 401 {'error':'Authentication required','message':'Missing Authorization header or session cookie'}; so do /api/images/recent, /api/auth/csrf and POST /api/auth/cookie. The bytes returned to 127.0.0.1 and to 192.168.1.20 are identical 401s. Only the static /dashboard shell is 200, and it can load no data. The auth middleware has no loopback branch at all.

**Expected:** Either implement the documented loopback exemption (peer address is loopback -> full-scope identity), or change the copy so it does not promise a local escape hatch that does not exist. As written, an operator clicking 'Open Local Dashboard' to 'confirm the dashboard is working' sees a dead page and concludes the server is broken.

**Repro:** Settings > Remote Access > Enable Remote Access. Then: curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/status -> 401. curl http://localhost:<port>/api/status -> 401. curl http://192.168.1.20:<port>/api/status -> 401.

**Where:** `copy: packages/skotiq_app/lib/localization/skotiq_localizations/translations.dart:389 and :399; enforcement: apps/desktop/lib/headless_api_server/http_middleware.dart:758-770 (token==null -> 401, no peer-address check anywhere in the chain).`

**Proposed fix:** Add an explicit loopback grant in the auth middleware keyed on the shelf connectionInfo remote address (and NOT on any forwarded header), or drop the 'frictionless' sentence and relabel the card 'Open the dashboard (you will need to pair this browser)'.

### Mosaic Wizard invents a camera field of view and plans the whole mosaic from it, while the framing dialog on the same screen correctly refuses to guess

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With no equipment profile configured, the Mosaic Wizard's Plan summary states 'Panel size: 1.00 deg x 0.67 deg' and derives 'Active panels 9 / Grid 3x3 / Est. time 2.3 h / Total exposures 90' from it. Those degrees are the hardcoded field defaults _panelWidthArcmin = 60.0 / _panelHeightArcmin = 40.0, not anything measured from the user's rig. The 'Frame target' dialog, reachable from the same Builder screen, handles the identical missing data honestly: 'No equipment profile with a focal length - set one in Settings to size the framing box.'

**Expected:** Say the FOV is unknown and require a focal length / sensor size (or an explicit override) before quoting a panel size, panel count and total time as facts.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder (no equipment profile) > toolbar grid icon (Plan Mosaic). Read 'Panel size' in the Plan summary. Then select a Target node and click 'Frame target' and read its warning.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/mosaic_wizard_dialog.dart:64-65`

**Proposed fix:** Initialise the panel size from the active equipment profile's focal length + sensor dimensions. When that is unavailable, show 'unknown' with the same wording the framing dialog uses and disable 'Load into Sequencer' / 'Create mosaic project' until it is supplied.

### Two independent Discord (and two Pushover) configurations on the same page; filling one leaves the other's Test saying it is unconfigured

`defect` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The Notifications page has a 'Discord' section near the top (Webhook URL + Test Discord) and a 'Discord (routing matrix)' section ~2000px lower (Webhook URL + Username override + Save + Test). They are separate stores. I pasted https://discord.com/api/webhooks/111/AUDITMATRIX into the routing-matrix one and saved -- toast 'Discord (routing) saved', field became '******** (stored securely)' with Replace/Clear. I then scrolled back up: the top 'Discord' section's Webhook URL was still the empty placeholder, and its 'Test Discord' answered 'Please enter a Discord webhook URL'. The identical duplication exists for Pushover ('Pushover' with API Key/User Key/Test vs 'Pushover (routing matrix)' with API token/User key/Device/Save/Test).

**Expected:** One Discord configuration per page. A user who configures Discord anywhere on the Notifications page should not be told by another control on that same page that Discord is unconfigured. If the legacy rows must survive for the legacy push path, they need to read from the same secret and be labelled as such.

**Repro:** Settings > NOTIFICATIONS & REMOTE > Notifications. Scroll to 'Discord (routing matrix)', paste any webhook URL, press 'Save Discord config'. Scroll back up to the 'Discord' section and press 'Test Discord'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/notification_settings.dart (legacy Discord/Pushover rows) + packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/notification_routing_settings/discord_transport_section.dart and pushover_transport_section.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Delete the legacy Discord/Pushover rows from notification_settings.dart and migrate discord_webhook / pushover_key / pushover_user into the keyring-backed transport config on first run (the same one-shot migration pattern already used by secrets_store.dart's notification_secrets_migrated_v2). If they must stay, retitle them 'Discord (legacy push)' and have both read the same source.

### Tutorial Tours run as a modal on whatever screen you are on and tell you to click tabs that are not there -- and the modal blocks every one of them

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Help & Tutorials > Tutorial Tours > Equipment Setup > Start opens a centred modal dialog with a full-screen scrim, ON THE SETTINGS SCREEN. Step 1 reads 'Equipment Profiles -- Click the Profiles tab to manage your equipment configurations.' There is no Profiles tab on the Settings screen, and the scrim swallows clicks so the user cannot go find one: with the overlay up I clicked the left-nav 'Imaging' entry (root 240,350) and the app stayed on Settings with the overlay unchanged. All 4 steps behave the same way ('Click the Connections tab', 'Check the status indicators for each device') -- it is a slideshow of instructions about a screen you are not on and cannot navigate to.

**Expected:** Starting a tutorial should route to the screen the tutorial is about (Equipment) and spotlight the real control, or at minimum let the underlying UI stay clickable so the instruction can be followed. NINA/SGP tours anchor to the widget being described.

**Repro:** Fresh profile > skip onboarding > gear icon > Help & Tutorials > scroll to Tutorial Tours > Equipment Setup 'Start'. Observe the modal appears over Settings. Click left-nav Imaging: nothing happens.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/help_tutorials_settings.dart:179 (onStart -> notifier.restartTutorial) and packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/tutorial_provider.dart:640 _restartTutorial -- it only sets state.activeCategory/currentStepIndex, it never navigates.`

**Proposed fix:** Give TutorialStep a target route and have _restartTutorial/resumeTutorial push it before showing step 0; make the overlay a non-blocking anchored coach-mark rather than a modal barrier.

### Moon is always drawn full and always labelled 'MOON 100%' - percent is fed into a 0-1 fraction and clamped

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On 2026-08-02 14:26:36 UTC from 40N/105W the planetarium drew the Moon as a solid, fully-lit white disc with the label 'MOON 100%'. The true illuminated fraction at that instant is 84.6% (independent Meeus computation, elongation 133.7 deg), and the app's own Dashboard Moon card says 'Waning Gibbous 84% illuminated' while Plan Tonight's target card says 'Moon is 79% illuminated'. Three surfaces, three different numbers, and the planetarium's is the one that is impossible. Root cause is a unit mismatch: AstronomyCalculations.moonIllumination() returns a PERCENT (0-100) and its doc comment says so, but SkyRenderer._drawMoon documents its third tuple element as 'illuminated fraction (0 = new, 1 = full)' and does rawIllumination.clamp(0.0, 1.0). Any real phase above 1% therefore clamps to exactly 1.0, so both the terminator geometry and the label are pinned at full moon for 29 of every 29.5 days. Only within a few hours of new moon does the value fall below 1 and render anything else - and then it renders a 1%-lit crescent as if it were 100%-scaled.

**Expected:** The label should read the real illuminated percentage (84% here) and the disc should be drawn with the corresponding terminator, matching the Dashboard's Moon card.

**Repro:** Settings > Location > lat 40, lon -105 > Plan Tonight > Planetarium. The Moon glyph is near the default 0h/0deg view. Read its label; then compare with Dashboard > Moon card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/rendering/sky_renderer/solar_system_and_markers.dart:481`

**Proposed fix:** Divide by 100 at the three call sites that build the moonPosition tuple (interactive_sky_view.dart:1026, planetarium_screen/actions.dart:212, widgets/view_controls.dart:396 - the exported finder chart is affected too), or better, change moonIllumination() to return a fraction and fix the one place that formats it as a percent.

### Pre-flight dark-library check uses the camera default temperature (-10C), not the temperature the sequence's Cool Camera node will actually reach (15C)

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The sequence's Cool Camera node was set to Target Temperature 15C (confirmed on the node chip '15C' and in Properties). Pre-Flight's Dark Library section reported 'No matching darks found for 1 exposure combination: gain=100, offset=50, temp=-10.0C, duration=3s, binning=1x1'. During the run the camera reported Cam: 15.0C. The check therefore matched darks against a temperature the run never uses, and the 'Capture missing darks' action it offers would capture darks at the wrong temperature.

**Expected:** Dark matching should use the setpoint the sequence will actually be at (the Cool Camera node value, 15C), or say which temperature it assumed and why.

**Repro:** Add Cool Camera, set Target Temperature 15. Add Take Exposures 4x3s. Start -> read Pre-Flight > Dark Library.

**Where:** `Sequencer > Builder > Start > Pre-Flight Validation > Dark Library`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the planned sensor temperature from the sequence's cooling instruction chain before querying the dark library; fall back to the camera default only when no Cool Camera node precedes the exposures.

### Equipment readiness panel omits the mount entirely when it is disconnected

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With all four profile devices (camera, mount, focuser, filter wheel) disconnected, the STATUS panel listed only two device items: 'Critical devices - Camera is not connected' and 'Profile devices - Assigned in your profile but not connected: Focuser, Filter Wheel. They will be unavailable this session.' The mount is not mentioned in either item, or anywhere else on the screen. A user scanning the readiness list before heading out would conclude the mount is fine.

**Expected:** Every profile device that is not connected must appear in the readiness list, and a disconnected mount should be a blocking/critical item -- it is the one failure that ends a night outright.

**Repro:** 1. Profile with camera, mount, focuser and filter wheel assigned. 2. Equipment > Disconnect All. 3. Read the STATUS panel: only Camera (critical) and 'Focuser, Filter Wheel' (profile devices) are listed.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/equipment_screen.dart / readiness item builders`

**Proposed fix:** Build the 'Profile devices' list from every assigned-but-unconnected device including the mount, and promote mount to the critical tier alongside camera.

### Pre-flight warns that a step is scheduled 'before target rises' when it is scheduled AFTER the rise time it prints

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Pre-Flight Validation listed three warnings of the form: 'New Target: "Cool Camera" scheduled at 14:45 before target rises at 11:44' (also for Unpark Mount and Slew to Target at 14:55). 14:45 and 14:55 are both AFTER 11:44, and the target card for the same target shows Rise 11:44 / Transit 17:49 / Set 23:47, i.e. the target is up at run time. The warning states the opposite of the data on the adjacent card.

**Expected:** No warning at all for a step scheduled between rise and set. If the intent is 'before the NEXT rise', the message must name that time, not a rise that already happened.

**Repro:** Set site 30N/82E, build Target(RA 00h,Dec +00) with Cool Camera/Unpark/Slew/Autofocus/Take Exposures, press Start at 14:45 local. Read the Simulation section of the Pre-Flight dialog.

**Where:** `Sequencer > Builder > Start > Pre-Flight Validation > Simulation`

**Proposed fix:** Compare the scheduled instant against the rise/set interval that CONTAINS it; only warn when the step really falls outside the visibility window, and print the boundary that was violated.

### Red night theme renders full-saturation blue, cyan and green accent swatches

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Appearance > Theme > 'Red night' correctly re-tints the whole shell, nav rail, cards and status bar in red. The 'Accent color' swatch row directly underneath it, however, stays fully saturated. Sampling the rendered pixels: (91,158,196) blue, (16,185,129) green, (245,158,11) amber, (239,68,68) red, (40,120,168) blue, (236,72,153) pink, (6,182,212) cyan. Cyan and green at those levels are exactly the short-wavelength, high-luminance content red-night mode exists to eliminate.

**Expected:** In red night mode every pixel should be red-channel-dominant. Either desaturate the accent swatches to red-luminance stand-ins (with the name as the differentiator) or hide the accent picker in red night with a note that accent colour does not apply.

**Repro:** 1. Settings > Appearance > Theme = Red night. 2. Look at the Accent color row on the same page. 3. Sample the swatch pixels.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/... appearance accent-colour row (swatches bypass the red-night colour filter)`

**Proposed fix:** Route the accent swatches through the same red-night transform as the rest of the theme, or replace them with labelled red-tinted chips while red night is active.

### Window minimize / maximize / close disappear below ~800px window width, leaving no way to close the app

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The desktop build draws its own title bar (no OS decorations). At 1600x900 and at 900x750 the bar carries remote-status, notifications, account, settings, minimize, maximize and close. Resizing the window to 760x700 switches the shell to the mobile layout (bottom navigation bar: Home/Gear/Imaging/Sequence/Guiding/Plan/More) and the title bar collapses to just the wordmark and a single settings gear -- minimize, maximize and close are gone, along with the notifications and account buttons. With no OS decorations there is then no mouse-reachable way to close or minimize the window.

**Expected:** Window controls belong to the desktop window regardless of width; the responsive breakpoint should never strip them. If the mobile shell is deliberately reused below the breakpoint, it must still render the platform window controls on desktop.

**Repro:** 1. Launch the Linux desktop build. 2. Resize the window to 760x700 (xdotool windowsize <win> 760 700). 3. Inspect the top bar: only the SKOTIQ wordmark and a gear remain.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart + widgets/title_bar_desktop.dart (breakpoint chooses the mobile chrome)`

**Proposed fix:** Keep the desktop title-bar window controls in every layout on desktop platforms; gate only the label density on width.

### The 'Deleted <profile>' snackbar never auto-dismisses and covers the entire status bar

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Deleting an equipment profile raises a full-width bottom snackbar 'Deleted "My First Rig"  [Undo]'. It was still on screen 7 minutes later (delete confirmed at 11:36:45, snackbar still present in the AT-SPI tree at 11:43:29), it survived navigating from Equipment to Settings to Dashboard, it has no close button, and clicking elsewhere does not dismiss it. While it is up, the whole status bar -- profile name, device connection states, focuser position, temperature, clock and LST -- is hidden. Only restarting the app cleared it.

**Expected:** An undo snackbar should auto-dismiss after a few seconds (or on navigation), and should not permanently occlude the status bar.

**Repro:** 1. Equipment > right-click a profile > Delete > Delete. 2. Snackbar appears over the status bar. 3. Navigate to Settings and then Dashboard, wait several minutes -- it is still there.

**Where:** `profile delete handler -> ScaffoldMessenger snackbar (duration/behaviour)`

**Proposed fix:** Give the snackbar a finite duration (e.g. 6 s), make it floating rather than full-width so it does not sit on the status bar, and add a dismiss affordance.

### Campaign Rollup contradicts itself three ways on one card: "Total integration 0.0h" beside "0.17h integration", and "No frames captured for this target yet" beside a session with 2 frames

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > click the M13 campaign card. The Campaign Rollup dialog shows header tiles 'Sessions 1', 'Total integration 0.0h', 'Mean HFR 2.20', 'Effective imaging 0.0%'; then 'Per-filter progress: No frames captured for this target yet.'; then a Sessions list containing 'Night E - M13, Jul 31 2026 23:00 | - | 0.17h integration, HFR 2.20, RMS 0.60'. The session has 2 light frames of 300 s each = 0.167 h, which is exactly what the session row prints - and exactly what the total denies.

**Expected:** One population per card. The header tiles and the per-filter breakdown count only accepted frames (both of this session's frames had just been rejected by the Image grader) while the per-session row counts all frames, so the dialog simultaneously asserts that the target has 0.0h and 0.17h of integration and that it has no frames and 2 frames. A user reading 'Total integration 0.0h / No frames captured for this target yet' for a target they imaged last night would conclude their data was lost.

**Repro:** Give a target one session with N light frames. Analytics > Science > Grade N frames > reject them all. Open the target's campaign card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/campaign_rollup_service.dart (totalIntegration / per-filter aggregation filter on acceptance) vs the per-session row built from the session's own total_integration_secs in campaign_rollup_dialog.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Aggregate the header, the per-filter table and the session rows from the same predicate, and label it ('accepted frames only'). If rejected frames are to be excluded, the session row must show '0.00h accepted (0.17h captured)'.

### Diagnostic dump is described as 'recent log files' but bundles 7 days of logs from every Skotiq instance on the machine

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Help & Tutorials > Generate Diagnostic Dump promises 'Recent log files -- logs/exported_logs.txt -- rotated native logs + in-app entries'. The produced zip contained a 4.78 MB, 38,299-line exported_logs.txt spanning 2026-07-26 through 2026-08-01, and 421 of its lines reference /tmp/ns-audit/sequencer-run -- a completely different profile's capture directory, including that run's target names and FITS filenames. The native log is written to the hardcoded ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/logs/ regardless of SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR, so the dump is not scoped to the session or the data directory the user is running. The save dialog also defaults to the application's own bundle directory (apps/desktop/build/linux/x64/release/bundle), which in a real install is typically read-only.

**Expected:** 'Recent log files' should mean the current session (or a bounded window), scoped to the running data directory; and the save dialog should default to a writable user location such as Downloads or the configured capture folder.

**Repro:** 1. Settings > Help & Tutorials > Generate Diagnostic Dump > Open > Create dump. 2. Note the save dialog opens inside the app bundle directory. 3. Save and inspect: unzip -p dump.zip logs/exported_logs.txt | grep -c . -> 38299 lines, dates 2026-07-26..2026-08-01, and grep for other profiles' paths.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/diagnostics/diagnostic_dump_screen.dart (_createDump) + native log path resolution`

**Proposed fix:** Bound the log export to the current session or last N hours, resolve the log directory from the active data dir, state the actual span in the UI, and default the save location to the user's Downloads/capture folder.

### Dashboard Mount jog pad fires a 500 ms pulse guide instead of a slew - the arrows do nothing a user can see

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Mount Control dashboard widget shows an N/S/E/W arrow pad that looks like a slew/jog pad. Each press calls mountCommandService.pulseGuide(direction), which the native log shows as 'Pulse guiding sim_mount_1 for 500ms in direction east' - about 4 arcseconds at guide rate. RA/Dec on the card stayed at 00:00:00 / +00d00'00" across three presses, no toast, no status change. Press-and-hold for 2 s produced exactly one 500 ms pulse, not a continuous slew.

**Expected:** An arrow pad on a mount control card should slew (moveAxis / directional slew) at a user-selectable rate while held, as it does in NINA, SGP and the ASIAIR, and stop on release. If it really is a guide pulse it must be labelled as one and show the resulting offset.

**Repro:** Dashboard -> Edit Dashboard -> Widgets -> enable Mount Control -> Done -> Unpark -> click the E arrow, then press-and-hold it for 2 s. Watch RA on the card and `tail /tmp/ns-audit/r-wizards/app.log`.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/mount_control_card.dart:173-182 (onDirection -> pulseGuide)`

**Proposed fix:** Bind the pad to a hold-to-slew (start on pointer down, stop on pointer up/cancel) using the mount's directional slew, and add a slew-rate selector next to the existing tracking-rate selector.

### Transient Discovery Report: the AAVSO and MPC network chips are inert and the app never shows why, because the reason is only rendered for the already-selected network

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > export icon > Transient Discovery Report > Open, with a confirmed detection selected ('AT 2026abc / New source / SNR 28.4 / confirmed'). The Network row offers AAVSO, MPC and TNS. TNS is pre-selected and shows a red explanation ('TNS submission needs your bot id/name, reporting group, data source, reporter, and api key'). Clicking AAVSO and clicking MPC does nothing - the selection stays on TNS and no message appears. I clicked each twice and re-read the tree after both.

**Expected:** A user who cannot use TNS should be told, in the panel, why the other two networks are also unavailable. The app already computes exactly that text - _disabledReason() returns 'MPC astrometry is for moving objects - this detection is not a plausible mover' and an AAVSO-specific reason - but it is only rendered for _format, which the user cannot change to a disabled network. The explanation is written and permanently unreachable.

**Repro:** Seed a transient_detections row with reviewed=1 and kind='newSource'; leave the TNS bot credentials and AAVSO observer code unset. Open the panel and click the AAVSO and MPC chips.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/transient_report_panel.dart:129-141 (chip onTap is null when !enabled) and :142-148 (the reason Text is guarded by `if (!_formatEnabled(_format, ...))`, i.e. only for the selected format); the per-format reasons live at :369-402`

**Proposed fix:** Let the user select a disabled network (keep Preview/Submit disabled) so its reason renders, or render a one-line reason under every disabled chip.

### Photometry CSV export writes local wall-clock timestamps with no timezone, silently shifting every measurement by the observer's UTC offset

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Science Data Export > Photometry Measurements > CSV wrote /home/scdouglas/Documents/Skotiq/exports/photometry_2026-08-02T11-18-30.csv. The first data row's Timestamp column reads '2026-08-01T05:00:00.000'. The DB row it came from has timestamp = 1785574800, which is 2026-08-01T09:00:00Z. The CSV is emitting local time (UTC-4 here) in ISO-8601 shape with no offset and no 'Z'.
