**Expected:** Photometry timestamps are the input to period analysis, AAVSO/AID submissions and any cross-observer comparison; they must be UTC (ideally JD or HJD as well) and must carry the offset. Any script that parses this file as ISO-8601 will place every point 4 hours off, and the error changes with the observer's timezone and with DST inside a single observing season.

**Repro:** Analytics > Science > export/database icon > Photometry Measurements > CSV. Compare the Timestamp column with `select timestamp from photometry_measurements order by timestamp limit 1` (unix seconds).

**Where:** `the science CSV export writer used by ScienceExportHub (it calls toIso8601String() on a local DateTime rather than .toUtc())`

**Proposed fix:** Emit `dt.toUtc().toIso8601String()` (which appends Z) and add a JD column; state the convention in the CSV header comment.

### Science Data Export says "No moving object candidates available to report yet" and its MPC Open button is dead, while the page behind it lists two candidates

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science with 2 rows in moving_object_candidates for the selected session: the ANOMALIES section lists 'CAND-1 82% 1.85"/min' and 'CAND-2', and the MPC Report Export card below it shows both with '2 selected'. Clicking the export/database icon at the top right of the Science header opens the 'Science Data Export' dialog, whose 'MPC Astrometry Report' row reads 'No moving object candidates available to report yet.' Clicking its 'Open' button does nothing at all - no dialog, no snackbar, twice.

**Expected:** The hub must see the same candidates the page does. As it is, the user's obvious route to an MPC report ('Export' -> 'MPC Astrometry Report') is a dead end that also tells them, untruthfully, that they have no candidates.

**Repro:** Seed moving_object_candidates rows for the selected session. Analytics > Science, open the export/database icon in the header. Compare the MPC row with the MPC Report Export card behind the dialog. Click Open.

**Where:** `ScienceExportHub takes mpcCandidates and defaults it to const [] (science_export_hub.dart:71-81); the row is enabled only when it is non-empty (:394) and _openMpcPanel returns early otherwise (:660-664). Two of the call sites pass nothing: science_screen.dart:154 `const ScienceExportHub()` and science_analytics_tab.dart:505 `_openExportHub(context, const [])`; only the Moving-objects card's own icon (:842) passes the real list.`

**Proposed fix:** Have ScienceExportHub read the moving-object candidates from the provider itself instead of taking them as a constructor argument, so every entry point is consistent. Failing that, pass `moving` at all call sites and make the disabled row explain 'opened without session context' rather than asserting there are no candidates.

### The whole Settings section navigator is mouse-only: no tab stop, no focus ring, and every entry is exposed as a panel rather than a button

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I clicked the Settings search box and pressed Tab 24 times, grabbing the sidebar region (230x580 at root 245,312) after every press and diffing it against the baseline: all 24 captures were pixel-identical (0 changed pixels, diff bbox None). Focus never lands on any of the 7 group headers or 35 section entries. Tab from the search box instead jumps to the window title bar - one Tab then Enter opened the 'Transient Alerts' popover in the top-right - and further Tabs go into the detail pane (three Tabs then Enter opened the UI scale dropdown). The accessibility tree agrees: every sidebar entry appears as 'panel: General', 'panel: Appearance', 'panel: Connection', while genuine buttons on the same screen appear as 'button: Test Connection', 'button: Save'. Source confirms it: _GroupHeader and _CategoryItem in settings_screen.dart are a bare GestureDetector inside a MouseRegion, with no Focus, no InkWell and no Semantics(button: true).

**Expected:** Every settings section should be a real focusable control: reachable by Tab/Shift-Tab, activatable with Enter/Space, drawing a visible focus indicator, and reported to AT as a button (or a selectable list item) with its selected state. Today a keyboard-only or screen-reader user cannot change settings sections at all.

**Repro:** Settings > click the search box > press Tab repeatedly. The sidebar never shows focus; the first Tab+Enter opens the title-bar Transient Alerts popover. Compare 'drive_linux.py tree': sidebar rows are 'panel:', real buttons are 'button:'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/settings_screen.dart:479 (_GroupHeader), :881 (_CategoryItem)`

**Proposed fix:** Replace both GestureDetectors with InkWell (or Semantics(button:true, selected:isSelected) + Focus + Actions) so they join the traversal order, paint a focus outline, and announce themselves. Keeping MouseRegion for the hover tint is fine - InkWell gives hover, focus and tap in one widget.

### Mouse-wheel zoom discards ~99% of scroll events - 172 notches move the FOV from 60 to 29 degrees

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Each wheel notch is supposed to zoom 1.15-1.2x. Measured live: 5 notches spaced 2.0s apart took FOV 28.9 -> 14.4 deg (exactly 1.15^5, correct). 12 notches spaced 0.3s took 60.0 -> 41.7 deg (the effect of ~2.6 notches). 120 notches spaced 0.05s took 34.7 -> 28.9 deg (the effect of ~1.3 notches out of 120). Spinning the wheel at any natural speed therefore barely zooms; it took 172 notches over two minutes to get from the default 60 deg to 28.9 deg, and there is no other zoom control on the desktop layout (no +/- buttons, no FOV field, and the '=' / '-' hotkeys are dead - see the focus finding).

**Expected:** Successive wheel events must compound onto the pending zoom target, not restart the animation from the current (barely-moved) FOV. A user should reach an imaging FOV in a few flicks of the wheel.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > point at the sky > spin the wheel up 20 notches quickly, watch 'FOV (short axis)' in the bottom bar.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/widgets/interactive_sky_view.dart:475`

**Proposed fix:** Accumulate into _targetFOV (use the in-flight target, not the current FOV, as the base) so N rapid notches equal N steps.

### Framing screen reports the wrong sensor size (1920x1080) for a camera that delivers 4144x2822 - the FOV rectangle is less than half the true width

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Framing sidebar shows 'Sensor 1920 x 1080', 'FOV 0.78 x 0.44 deg', 'Resolution 1.46 arcsec/px', and the OPTICAL CONFIG overlay repeats '1920 x 1080 px (3.76um)'. The same connected camera in the same app session delivers 4144 x 2822 frames: the Imaging toolbar reads '4144 x 2822', the written FITS has NAXIS1=4144/NAXIS2=2822, and each file is 23,394,240 bytes = 4144*2822*2 + header. At 1.4632 arcsec/px the true FOV is 1.68 x 1.15 deg, so the FOV box drawn over the DSS2 imagery is 2.16x too narrow. A user composing a mosaic or checking whether a target fits would get it badly wrong.

**Expected:** The Framing FOV must be computed from the same sensor geometry the camera actually delivers. Two surfaces of one app must not disagree about one connected camera.

**Repro:** 1. Connect the sim camera. 2. Imaging -> Snapshot -> note '4144 x 2822' in the toolbar and NAXIS1/2 in the FITS. 3. Plan Tonight -> Framing (Send to Framing from any target). 4. Read Sensor/FOV in the sidebar.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/framing_provider/support.dart:107-109 (pixelsX = status.sensorWidth from backend.getCameraStatus) vs native/skotiq_native/bridge/src/sim_frame.rs:41-42 (SIM_W=1920, SIM_H=1080) and native/skotiq_native/bridge/src/api/devices/simulation.rs:68-69`

**Proposed fix:** Two parts. (a) Finish the unification sim_frame.rs:33-41 claims to have done - the manual-capture path still produces 4144x2822 while status advertises 1920x1080, so 'the frame a caller receives matches the geometry the camera advertised' is currently false. (b) Independently, have the framing FOV cross-check the advertised geometry against the last delivered frame and warn instead of silently drawing a wrong box - on real ASCOM hardware the same split exists between CameraXSize and an active subframe/ROI.

### The session checkpoint lives outside the database, so the app offers to resume a sequence its own database has never heard of — and the failure dialog contradicts itself

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On launch, a profile whose database contains ZERO rows in sequences, sequence_nodes, sequence_runs, sequence_checkpoints, sequence_versions and captured_images was shown 'Recover Sequence? / A previous sequence was interrupted and can be resumed. / Sequence: New Sequence / Saved: 0m ago / Completed: 4 frames (0m integration)'. The claim of 4 completed frames is untrue for the database this instance is using. Pressing Resume produced, inside the SAME dialog: 'Recovery did not complete — Exception: No valid checkpoint to resume from' immediately followed by 'The checkpoint is still available. Retry resume or discard it to start fresh.' — two contradictory statements, one of them a raw Dart exception string, while the header still asserts '4 frames' were completed.

**Expected:** The recovery prompt must be scoped to the database in use (store the checkpoint in the DB, or key the file to the database identity), the frame count must come from that database, and a failed resume must not simultaneously say the checkpoint is invalid and still available.

**Repro:** Launch with SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR pointed at an empty scratch database while a checkpoint file exists at ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint. The dialog appears; press Resume.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart:398 (Recover Sequence dialog); checkpoint file writer (session checkpoint service, ~/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint)`

**Proposed fix:** Store the checkpoint in the database it belongs to (or stamp it with the database path/id and refuse to offer it to a different one), derive the frame count from that database, and replace the exception text + contradictory follow-up with one honest sentence.

### The cull rail advertises 'Keep best 1 (+0%)' from a curve it already knows is stale; pressing it always refuses, and its advice ('re-integrate to refresh') reproduces the same stale curve

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The improvement curve is derived from the master that was just produced. Because that master contained only 1 registered frame (see the P0 above), the persisted curve has one point and a recommendation of {keepN: 1, predictedSnrGainPct: 0.0, reason: 'Keep all 1 subs: already optimal (+0.0% SNR vs full stack)'}. The cull rail then renders an enabled primary action 'Keep best 1 (+0%)' whose tooltip is 'Drop to the optimizer-recommended best 1 subs (+0% SNR)'. Pressing it three times changed nothing (still 9 accepted in the DB) and toasted 'Curve is out of date - re-integrate to refresh before culling'. So the control knows the curve does not match the 9 accepted subs, yet still renders a specific, quantified, actionable label -- and its remedy is to re-run the same integration that produced the stale curve.

**Expected:** The rail must decide staleness at render time, not at press time. When the curve's population does not cover the currently-accepted subs, or when predictedSnrGainPct <= 0, show a disabled/absent action (or an explicit 'analysis out of date' chip) rather than a confident 'Keep best 1 (+0%)'. A control should not offer an operation it will unconditionally refuse.

**Repro:** 1. Do the Integrate-now repro above (produces a 1-frame master). 2. A new button 'Keep best 1 (+0%)' appears beside Blink mode / Select-lasso. 3. Click it. 4. sqlite3 skotiq.db "select count(*) from captured_images where session_id=1 and is_accepted=1" -> still 9; toast reads 'Curve is out of date - re-integrate to refresh before culling'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/session_review/widgets/sub_cull_rail.dart:473 (tooltip) and :475 (label 'Keep best $keepN (+${gainPct}%)'); guard in session_review_controller.dart:1354 cullToRecommended()`

**Proposed fix:** Lift the population-vs-accepted comparison out of cullToRecommended() into a getter the rail reads while building, and gate the button on it plus predictedSnrGainPct > 0.

### Exported MPC 80-column report puts the CCD observation-type code in column 14 instead of column 15, so every record is malformed

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > ANOMALIES > MPC Report Export > Select All > Export to File produced /home/scdouglas/Documents/Skotiq/exports/mpc_report_2026-08-02T15-14-07-815531Z.txt with two 80-character records:
     NS0001  C 2026 08 01.12847 16 41 41.64 +36 27 40.7           17.4V      W88
Column-by-column that is: 1-5 blank, 6-12 'NS0001 ', 13 ' ', 14 'C', 15 ' ', 16-32 date, 33-44 RA, 45-56 Dec, 57-65 blank, 66-70 ' 17.4', 71 'V', 72-77 blank, 78-80 'W88'. The MPC's 80-column format defines column 14 as Note 1 and column 15 as Note 2, and it is Note 2 that carries the observation type ('C' = CCD). Skotiq writes 'C' into Note 1 and leaves Note 2 blank.

**Expected:** Column 15 = 'C', column 14 blank. As exported, every observation the user submits reaches the MPC with no observation type and a non-standard Note 1 - the exact thing the MPC's submission checker rejects.

**Repro:** Set Settings > Science > MPC observatory code (I set app_settings 'science.mpc.observatory_code' = 'W88'). Analytics > Science > MPC Report Export > Select All > Export to File. Read columns 14 and 15 of the output.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/science/mpc_export_service.dart:180-185`

**Proposed fix:** Swap them: `const note1 = ' '; const note2 = 'C';` and fix the two comments. Add a unit test asserting line[14] == 'C' (0-indexed) for a CCD observation.

### The Smart Night dashboard prompt never appears even with its toggle on and every documented precondition met

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings -> Sequencer -> Smart Night Defaults -> 'Dashboard auto-prompt' ('Show the Smart Night prompt on the dashboard when your equipment is ready') is ON by default and I confirmed the toggle is blue/on. I then satisfied every gate the card checks (smart_night_prompt_card.dart:118-127): profile focal length 550 mm and aperture 100 mm saved (equipmentReady), observing location 40.0 / -105.0 and not 0,0, no sequence running, nothing dismissed on a fresh profile, and I waited more than four minutes - well past the 60 s readiness grace. The bottom-centre prompt never appeared, on the briefing dashboard or the cockpit dashboard.

**Expected:** With the toggle on and equipment ready the prompt should slide in from the bottom centre. Either it shows, or the setting should not promise it.

**Repro:** Fresh profile -> Settings -> Location: 40 / -105 -> Equipment -> Edit Profile -> focal length 550, aperture 100 -> connect the four simulators -> Dashboard -> wait 4 minutes.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/smart_night_prompt_card.dart:118-144 - the two remaining suppressors are the 60 s grace rebuild (_equipmentReadyGraceElapsed / _graceTimer) and 'if (draftAsync.isLoading || draftAsync.valueOrNull != null) return SizedBox.shrink()' on pendingSmartNightDraftProvider; a provider that never leaves loading would hide the card forever with no trace.`

**Proposed fix:** Log and test the two suppressors: give pendingSmartNightDraftProvider a timeout and treat an unresolved lookup as 'no draft', and assert the grace timer schedules a rebuild. Caveat: the equipment on this profile was connected via Discovery rather than assigned to the profile ('0 devices'), which the card does not check but a related provider might.

### Science > Field Quality: HFR chart blames "no star metrics" when the frames have star metrics but were rejected, while the uniformity chart beside it still counts them

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Night E's two frames have hfr = 2.20 and star_count = 3000 in captured_images. Before grading, Analytics > Science > FIELD QUALITY > 'HFR over time' plotted them (axis 2.16-2.24, badge 2.20). After using the Image grader to reject both frames (is_accepted 1 -> 0, metrics untouched), the same card renders the empty state 'HFR appears once star metrics are recorded on captures.' - while the 'Field uniformity (CV)' chart immediately to its right still plots both frames (0.180 -> 0.240, badge 0.240).

**Expected:** Two charts in the same section must use the same frame population, and an empty state must say the real reason. The frames DO have star metrics; they are excluded because they are rejected. 'HFR appears once star metrics are recorded' sends the user to look for a plate-solve/star-detection problem that does not exist.

**Repro:** Analytics > Science on a session with HFR-bearing frames; note HFR over time draws. Grade N frames > lower a threshold > Reject all. Return to FIELD QUALITY: HFR over time is empty with the star-metrics message, Field uniformity (CV) is unchanged.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/science_analytics_tab/science_info_and_series_chart.dart (HFR series filters accepted frames) vs the uniformity series which reads science_frame_quality_metrics unfiltered`

**Proposed fix:** Pick one population for the section and state it in the section header ('accepted light frames only'); change the empty text to 'No accepted frames with star metrics - N frames were rejected.'

### Autofocus row shows an editable 'method' chip that the executor ignores, next to a panel saying the real method is something else

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** An Autofocus node's tree row renders 'Autofocus | [vCurve v] | uses defaults'. The Properties panel for the same node, visible at the same time, shows 'Use Settings Defaults [ON] - Using global AF settings' and then 'Global settings: Method = Star HFR, Curve Fitting = Hyperbolic'. I opened the row chip and changed it to 'quadratic'; the row became 'quadratic | uses defaults' and the panel still said Star HFR / Hyperbolic. The chosen value was persisted to the DB (properties {"method":"quadratic",...,"useSettingsDefaults":true}) but per capture_and_guiding.dart:371-374 and autofocus_controls.dart:87-96 it is discarded at run time whenever useSettingsDefaults is true, which is the default.

**Expected:** When 'Use Settings Defaults' is on, the row should show the method that will actually run (Star HFR) or show nothing, and the chip should be disabled. An editable control that has no effect and displays a value contradicted by the panel beside it is a false statement about what the run will do.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > palette search 'autofocus' > click + . Select the node. Read the tree row chip vs the Properties panel 'Global settings' block. Click the row chip and pick 'quadratic'; re-read both.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/node_summary.dart:290-297; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/sequence/sequence_models/instruction_nodes/capture_and_guiding.dart:371-374`

**Proposed fix:** In node_summary.dart, when useSettingsDefaults is true emit a StaticFragment showing the effective global method rather than an EditableFragment bound to node.method; only render the editable chip when the node is in 'custom' mode. Also relabel: the node's enum values (vCurve/hyperbolic/quadratic) are curve-fit choices, while the global setting distinguishes Method (Star HFR) from Curve Fitting (Hyperbolic) - the node model conflates two different settings under one name.

### Every keyboard shortcut the sequencer advertises is dead in the ordinary state - including the Ctrl+Z that two delete dialogs say is the only recovery

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The sequencer's own Keyboard Shortcuts sheet (5th tab) lists Alt+1..4, Ctrl+T, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Delete, Esc. With an Autofocus node selected (Properties panel showing it) I pressed Delete: nothing. Ctrl+D: nothing. Ctrl+Z after a confirmed node deletion: nothing (the deleted node stayed gone; the toolbar Undo button restored it immediately after). Alt+2, Alt+3 and Ctrl+T never switched tab, even after clicking directly on the tree canvas. The bindings only fired after I clicked a tab-bar button, which happens to move focus into the subtree - Ctrl+D then duplicated correctly (10 -> 11 steps). The delete confirmation dialog states 'This cannot be undone except via Undo (Ctrl+Z)', which is false in exactly the state a user reaches it from (they just typed in the palette search to find the node they are now deleting - that TextField keeps focus and swallows the keys).

**Expected:** Shortcuts listed in the help sheet work whenever the Sequencer screen is showing, regardless of which widget last received a click; and the delete dialog's recovery instruction must be accurate.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > palette search 'delay' > add > click any node row to select it (Properties panel updates) > press Delete, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+Z: nothing happens. Now click 'Sequences' then 'Builder' in the tab bar, click a node, press Ctrl+D: it duplicates. Then press Alt+3 at any time: the tab does not change.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/sequencer_screen.dart:344-440 (CallbackShortcuts); tab list at sequencer_screen_parts/tab_bar.dart:205`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the CallbackShortcuts subtree in a FocusScope with autofocus, or move the bindings to a Shortcuts/Actions pair registered on the route so they do not depend on a descendant holding primary focus. Make the tree rows themselves focusable so clicking one moves focus into the scope. Until Ctrl+Z is reliable, change the delete-dialog copy to point at the toolbar Undo button, which does work.

### Flat wizard's "Flat capture failed - no frames were saved" gives no reason and no next step

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Quick Capture ramped the exposure 0.17s -> 0.42 -> 1.0 -> 2.39 -> 5.75 -> 13.79 -> 30.0 s and the measured ADU crept only from ~1050 to 1361 against a target of 32768. After ~60 s it stopped with the red banner 'Flat capture failed - no frames were saved.' and the status chip 'Failed'. Nothing says what failed: not that the target ADU was unreachable, not that the 30 s exposure ceiling was hit, not that the ADU barely moved as exposure grew 170x (which is the actual diagnosis - the panel is not illuminating the sensor).

**Expected:** Name the failure: 'Could not reach 32768 ADU - at the 30 s maximum the frame only reached 1361 ADU. Increase panel brightness or gain.' And when ADU does not scale with exposure, say so ('brightness did not change when exposure increased 170x - is the flat panel switched on?').

**Repro:** Flat Wizard -> Quick Capture -> Frames 5 -> Start Capture -> pick a folder -> Continue. Wait ~60 s.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/flat_wizard/ (action_buttons / status banner) and the flat capture service that raises the failure`

**Proposed fix:** Carry a typed failure reason (targetUnreachable / exposureCeiling / adduNotResponding / saveFailed) through to the banner and render the measured-vs-target numbers.

### The Alpaca server address can never be set: 'Query Alpaca on startup' points at a 'configured Alpaca server' that no screen in the app configures

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Connection > Discovery has 'INDI Server Address — localhost:7624' with a Configure button, and directly under it 'Query Alpaca on startup / Include the configured Alpaca server in automatic startup discovery'. There is no Alpaca Server Address row anywhere on the page (I scrolled the whole page). app_settings does hold alpaca_server_host=localhost and alpaca_server_port=11111, and skotiq_core exposes setAlpacaServerHost/setAlpacaServerPort — but `grep -rl alpacaServerHost packages/skotiq_app/lib` returns nothing, so no UI ever calls them. The Alpaca discovery address is therefore permanently localhost:11111.

**Expected:** Alpaca is the cross-platform path this very page recommends ('Cross-platform ASCOM devices should use Alpaca'), and the common deployment is an ASCOM Remote / Alpaca device on ANOTHER machine on the LAN. An Alpaca Server Address row with the same Configure + Test affordance as INDI is required, or the toggle's subtitle must stop claiming there is a configured server.

**Repro:** Settings > Connection, scroll the full page. Only INDI has an address row. Toggle 'Query Alpaca on startup' on; there is no way to say where.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/connection_settings.dart:150-190; setters live at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/settings_sections/protocol.dart:42`

**Proposed fix:** Add an 'Alpaca Server Address' row with a Configure dialog mirroring the INDI one (host/port/Test/Save), wired to setAlpacaServerHost/Port.

### Recovery node's properties panel says its trigger is 'Any Error' while pre-flight calls it unconfigured: 'It will never fire'

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Added a Recovery node. Its properties panel shows Trigger Type = 'Any Error', Recovery Action = 'Retry Operation', Max Retries = 3 — reading exactly like a configured node. Pre-flight then blocks the run with an ERROR: 'Recovery Has No Trigger — Recovery node "Recovery" has no trigger configured. It will never fire.' Source confirms the contradiction: recovery_properties.dart:32 renders a null triggerType as the label 'Any Error', while logic_node_rules.dart:45 treats null as unconfigured.

**Expected:** The dropdown should show an explicit unset state (e.g. 'Select trigger…') or 'Any Error' should actually be persisted as a real trigger type.

**Repro:** New sequence > add Recovery > read Properties (Trigger Type: Any Error) > Start. Pre-flight: 'Recovery Has No Trigger'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/logic_node_properties/recovery_properties.dart:32; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/logic_node_rules.dart:45`

**Proposed fix:** Render null as a distinct 'Not set' entry, or persist TriggerType.anyError when the node is created from the palette.

### Sequencer crash-recovery checkpoint is stored outside the profile data dir, so one instance offers to resume another instance's sequence

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On startup this audit instance (SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR/SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR = /tmp/ns-audit/r-analytics/data, with ZERO rows in sequences and sequence_checkpoints) showed 'Recover Sequence? A previous sequence was interrupted and can be resumed - Sequence: New Sequence, Saved 0m ago, Completed 4 frames (0m integration)'. The checkpoint it read is /home/scdouglas/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop/skotiq_session.checkpoint, written by a DIFFERENT concurrently-running instance.

**Expected:** Crash recovery must be scoped to the instance/profile that wrote it. Offering to resume a sequence whose id does not exist in this database is a data-integrity hazard: on a machine that runs both the desktop app and the headless host (a shipped configuration) the two silently clobber each other's recovery state, and Resume would restart the wrong run. Note also the self-contradictory summary line 'Completed 4 frames (0m integration)'.

**Repro:** Run two Skotiq desktop instances with different SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR values. Build a sequence in one. Restart the other: it offers to recover the first one's sequence.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart:347 getApplicationSupportDirectory() is passed to sequencerSetCheckpointDir, ignoring SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR; native/skotiq_native/sequencer/src/checkpoint.rs:516`

**Proposed fix:** Derive the checkpoint dir from the same root as the database (SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR when set), and stamp the checkpoint with the database path/instance id so a mismatched checkpoint is ignored rather than offered.

### Sequence header reports '0 frames / 0m' for a Science Photometry node that says '60 frames', and pre-flight claims 'No exposure nodes found'

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** New sequence containing exactly one Science Photometry node. Its own row reads '(no target) · L · 60 frames' and its properties read Exposure 2.0 s, Frame count 60 (=2 min of integration). The toolbar planning chip reads '0 frames  0m'. Pre-flight adds a warning 'No Exposures — No exposure nodes found. The sequence will run but capture no images.' Earlier, a kitchen-sink sequence with 2 Science Photometry nodes plus 30 normal exposures also reported exactly '30 frames'. So the sequence-level frame count and time estimate ignore SciencePhotometryNode entirely.

**Expected:** A photometry burst is the longest-running thing in a science sequence; the header must count its frames and its time, and the 'No Exposures' rule must not fire when a photometry node is present.

**Repro:** New sequence > add 'Science Photometry' > read the toolbar chip vs the node row.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/sequence_time_estimator.dart (aggregate path); the 'No Exposures' rule in packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/`

**Proposed fix:** Include SciencePhotometryNode.count in the frame aggregate and its count x (exposure + download) in the duration aggregate; exclude it from the No-Exposures rule.

### The NIGHT STORY timeline (and every narrator detail sheet) is hidden whenever the session has no science products, even when narrator events exist

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With session 1 holding 3 narrator_events rows and 14 captured_images rows but zero science products, Analytics > Science rendered the shared empty state ('Turn pretty pictures into real measurements / Show me the 5 steps') and the NIGHT STORY section did not exist anywhere in the widget tree - so the narrator detail sheet was unreachable. Insert any row into transparency_samples for that session and relaunch: NIGHT STORY appears with the same events.

### Diagnostics tab shows loading skeletons forever for a session it cannot analyse

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Diagnostics > select 'Night E - M13' (2 frames, 18 psf_field_tiles rows, 0 plate-solved). All five result cards render as shimmering placeholder rectangles and stay that way. I waited 95 seconds, took two screenshots 80 s apart, and re-read the accessibility tree three times: the tree contains no result text at all - only the header, the session button and the blurb - and no error appears in the app log.

**Expected:** Either produce the analysis or say why it cannot ('needs plate-solved frames'), which is exactly what the same screen does for Night D. An indefinite skeleton is indistinguishable from a hang.
