`ux` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** With 16 frames in the session only 12 thumbnails fit. Pointing at the rail and turning the wheel scrolls the PAGE, not the rail; the remaining 4 frames stay off-screen. Only a horizontal-wheel / shift+wheel gesture moved it. There is no scrollbar and no left/right affordance.

**Expected:** On desktop, a horizontal rail must be reachable with the wheel a user actually has - either map vertical wheel to the rail, or show a Scrollbar and arrow affordances. A 200-frame night is unusable otherwise.

**Repro:** Analytics > Session > Night A - M31; hover the Captured Images rail; wheel down (page scrolls). Shift+wheel to reveal the trailing frames.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/image_thumbnail_strip.dart:178 ListView.builder(scrollDirection: Axis.horizontal) with no Scrollbar and default desktop ScrollBehavior`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap in a Scrollbar(thumbVisibility: true) and add a Listener that maps PointerScrollEvent.dy onto the horizontal controller, as the app already does for the sequencer node rail.

### The five-rung Science guide is a set of paragraphs, not a ladder you can climb -- every rung opens an explanation and a 'Got it' button with no action

`ux` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science shows a 'Science guide' with rungs 1-5 (Measure your sky / Track a star / Build a light curve / Find the period / Contribute it) and a single page-level 'Run calibration' button. Opening rung 2 gives 'Point at a target and let the photometry follow it frame to frame...' plus the gate 'Calibrate your sky first so brightness reads true.' and one button: 'Got it'. Rung 5 gives 'Your measurements are good enough to share...' plus 'Find a period - or build out the curve - before exporting.' and again only 'Got it'. Neither sheet offers the action it is describing, or a link to where that action lives. The only actionable rung is rung 1, whose button is on the page rather than in the sheet.

**Expected:** Each rung sheet should carry the action for that rung -- 'Pick a target' (rung 2), 'Open the light curve' (rung 3), 'Run period search' (rung 4), 'Open the export hub' (rung 5) -- enabled or disabled with the stated prerequisite. As built, a user who reads the ladder has to go find each surface themselves, which is exactly the discoverability problem the ladder exists to solve.

**Repro:** 1. Analytics > Science. 2. Click rung 2 'Track a star' -> sheet with text and 'Got it'. 3. Escape, click rung 5 'Contribute it' -> sheet with text and 'Got it'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/science_rung_sheet.dart, hosted by packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/science/science_screen.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Give ScienceRungSheet a primary action per rung that routes to that rung's surface, disabled with the prerequisite as its tooltip when the gate is unmet.

### PHD2 cannot be configured during onboarding unless PHD2 is already running

`ux` · imaging, framing, guiding · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The onboarding guider step offers native guiders ('Sim (0)' - there is no simulated guider device) plus a PHD2 block with Host=localhost and Port=4400 prefilled and a Test button. Test correctly reports 'No response on localhost:4400. Is PHD2 running with "Enable Server" turned on?'. But there is no way to SELECT PHD2 as the guider - pressing Next with the prefilled host/port silently discards it, and the review step shows 'Guider - not set -'. So a user setting up indoors before their PHD2 install/first launch cannot record their guider choice at all, and the Guiding screen then greets them with a whole panel of dead-looking controls.

**Expected:** Let the user commit host/port without a successful test (Test stays available as a verification), and record the guider as PHD2 in the profile.

**Repro:** 1. Do not run PHD2. 2. Onboarding -> Guider step -> press Test (honest error) -> press Next. 3. Review step shows 'Guider - not set -'.

**Where:** `onboarding guider step + packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/guiding/guiding_screen_parts/desktop_sections.dart:230-245`

**Proposed fix:** Add an explicit 'Use PHD2' selection on the onboarding step that persists host/port regardless of Test result. Separately: discovery reports 'Guider: 1 devices' in the log but the onboarding guider list shows 'Sim (0)' - worth reconciling.

### Equipment Stats: half of the tab is permanently "Not tracked"

`ux` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** With a full session history seeded, Mount shows Total Slews / Total Tracking Time / Meridian Flips = Not tracked, Focuser shows Total Movements = Not tracked, Guider shows Total Guide Time and Star Lost Events = Not tracked. Six of the twelve figures are hardcoded strings.

**Expected:** An "Equipment Stats" tab whose Mount card contains no numbers at all is not worth a tab. Several of these are already derivable from data the app stores: meridian flips are recorded as sequencer decisions/narrator events, guide time and star-lost from guide_rms_history, focuser movements from focus_models/autofocus runs.

**Repro:** Analytics > Equipment Stats with 3 sessions and 27 frames seeded.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/analytics_screen/equipment_stats.dart:137-139,150,156,162`

**Proposed fix:** Either wire the six to the tables that already hold the data, or drop the Mount card and rename the tab to what it measures (camera + focus + guiding quality).

### No single 'safe the rig' action: at 2am with clouds coming in, stopping the run and parking the mount are on two different tabs with no shared shortcut

`ux` · mobile operation - companion dashboard shell, mount/sequencer/log/settings tabs, session picker and session replay, driven disconnected on the ns_test_api35 emulator · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The companion is the surface you hold when you are in bed and the sky turns. The two things you need in that moment - stop the sequence and park the mount - live in two separate bottom-tab destinations with no common entry point. Stopping means: Sequencer tab, then press-and-hold the Stop button. Parking means: switch to the Mount tab, then reach _ControlsRow, which is the FOURTH child of that tab's ListView (after _PositionCard, _SlewRateSelector and the _Dpad), so on a phone it is below the fold and needs a scroll first. That is a minimum of tab-switch + scroll + tap, with a tab switch in the middle, and nothing anywhere in the persistent chrome offers 'stop and park'. The AppBar has room - it currently carries only a battery percentage, a network dot and a 2-item overflow menu whose contents (Saved servers, Session history) are both cold-path.

**Expected:** A single persistent 'Safe the rig' affordance reachable from every tab - press-and-hold, destructive styling, one gesture - that stops the sequence and parks the mount in the correct order. Put it in the AppBar action slot or as a FAB. The press-and-hold pattern the Stop button already uses is the right interaction for this (it survives a pocket tap); it just needs to be reachable without first navigating to the right tab.

**Repro:** 1. Companion UI enabled; Skip Connection. 2. Note the AppBar actions on any tab: battery '100%' at [678,165][828,238] and 'More' at [954,139][1080,265]. 3. Open More: only 'Saved servers' [471,170][1059,296] and 'Session history / replay' [471,296][1059,422]. 4. Sequencer tab: the Stop control is `Button: Press and hold to stop the sequence / Stop [DISABLED] [551,2012][1038,2127]` - present only on this tab. 5. Mount tab with no mount: only the empty state renders.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/mobile_dashboard_screen.dart:129-193 (AppBar actions + overflow); apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/tabs/mount_tab.dart:258-279, 805; apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/tabs/sequencer_tab.dart (Stop)`

**Proposed fix:** Add a third AppBar action - a red shield/park glyph - wired to a press-and-hold that calls sequencer stop then mount park, with a confirmation toast naming both outcomes. Failing that, promote Park into _PositionCard so it is above the fold on the Mount tab and costs one tab-switch plus one tap.

### Default file naming pattern puts every frame of every target and every night in one flat directory, and the field documents 5 of 18 variables

`default` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The shipped default is '$TARGET_$FILTER_$DATE_$SEQ' with no '/' - so lights, and every target and every session, land side by side in the capture root. The row's subtitle advertises only '$TARGET, $FILTER, $DATE, $SEQ, $EXPOSURE'; the pipeline supports 18 tokens including $FRAMETYPE, $FRAMENUM, $EXPTIME, $TIME, $DATETIME, $GAIN, $OFFSET, $TEMP, $BINNING, $CAMERA, $TELESCOPE, $SEQUENCE, $SESSION. Nothing tells the user that '/' creates subdirectories - the single most-used feature of a naming pattern - even though the code's own default constant is '$TARGET/$FRAMETYPE/$TARGET_$FILTER_$EXPTIME_$FRAMENUM'.

**Expected:** Ship '$TARGET/$FRAMETYPE/$TARGET_$FILTER_$EXPTIME_$FRAMENUM' (the constant already in NamingPattern), list all supported variables in a popover, and state that '/' creates folders. NINA and SGP both default to target/date/filter folders.

**Repro:** Fresh profile, Settings > Imaging: pattern reads '$TARGET_$FILTER_$DATE_$SEQ', subtitle lists 5 variables.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/database/database/default_settings.dart:21 and packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/settings_sections/app_settings_state.dart:485 (default); packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/imaging_settings.dart:66-67 (subtitle); packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/imaging/imaging_models/naming_and_autofocus.dart:10,34-51 (the richer default and full variable list)`

**Proposed fix:** Change the seeded default to the subdirectory pattern and replace the 5-variable subtitle with a full variable reference plus a live filename preview.


## P3

### Palette search never ranks the exact node-name match first, so typing a node's name adds a different node

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The palette matches on name OR description and returns results in catalogue order, so a description hit outranks an exact name hit. Three separate searches during this sweep added the wrong node: typing 'Dither' listed 'Smart Exposure' first (its description reads 'handles rotation + dither') and I added a Smart Exposure instead of a Dither; typing 'start guiding' listed 'Photometry Run (template)' first (description 'Center, start guiding, then run AAVSO-grade photometry') and added a 3-child container; typing 'loop' listed 'Instruction Set' first (description 'Group instructions sequentially (no loop)') and added an InstructionSet.

**Expected:** An exact or prefix match on the node NAME ranks above any description match. Typing 'Dither' and clicking the first result must add a Dither node.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > Nodes tab. Type 'Dither' in 'Search nodes...' - Smart Exposure is listed first. Type 'start guiding' - Photometry Run (template) is first. Type 'loop' - Instruction Set is first.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/sequencer_screen_parts/node_palette.dart:35-50 (category-order filter, no scoring)`

**Proposed fix:** Score matches: exact name == 100, name startsWith == 80, name contains == 60, description contains == 20; sort descending and keep category grouping only as a visual header. Optionally bold the matched substring so the user can see why a row matched.

### A single failing Dither node is reported as two identical errors, in the report and in two stacked critical banners

`defect` · sequencer run-time (executing sequences end to end) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The sequence contains one Dither node. With no guider connected it failed once, but the run recorded errorMessages: ['Dither: Dither failed: No active guider configured', 'Dither: Dither failed: No active guider configured'], the Session Report's Errors section printed the line twice, and two identical 'Critical - Sequencer / Dither: Dither failed: No active guider configured' banners stacked on the right edge of the screen.

**Expected:** One error per failure occurrence.

**Repro:** Add one Dither node with no guider connected, run the sequence, read the Session Report Errors list and the on-screen banners.

**Where:** `Sequencer run-time error reporting`

**Proposed fix:** Emit the failure once from the node executor; the second copy looks like the node-level and run-level handlers both recording it.

### All-Sky reticle labels collide: the error marker, the az/alt caption and the "Dn" compass label are drawn on top of each other

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** In All-Sky mode with a result showing, the red error marker sits underneath the caption 'Az -351.4\" Alt -1765.4\"' (the dot bisects the '-351.4' text), and that caption in turn overlaps the 'Dn' axis label of the compass rose. Three separate elements occupy the same ~30 px band.

**Expected:** The marker must draw above/around the labels, and the numeric caption must be placed outside the reticle (or the reticle padded) so the compass labels stay legible.

**Repro:** Run TPPA to completion, then switch the segmented control to All-Sky. Right rail.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/polar_alignment/widgets/all_sky_target_reticle.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Paint the marker last, and lay the numeric caption below the reticle box rather than inside it.

### Automatic backups are ON by default and write to ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups, a location the Files & Storage page never names and offers no way to change

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** 'Enable automatic backups' defaults ON with a 24 h interval and 'Maximum backups 7'. The page shows File Paths for 'Image output' and 'Sequences' and says application data is 'Managed automatically in Skotiq's application data folder' — but it never says where backups go. They go to getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()/Skotiq/backups, i.e. ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups, which ignores SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR entirely: this sandboxed audit instance, whose data dir is /tmp/ns-audit/r-settings-general/data, wrote its backups into the real user's Documents folder. 'Maximum backups' then PRUNES files in that undisclosed directory.

**Expected:** The Status section should show the backup folder path with a 'Reveal' action and let the user change it (backups belong on the same drive the user actually backs up, and an observatory PC's Documents folder is often the smallest volume). Retention that deletes files must name the folder it deletes from. Honouring the configured data directory is the minimum.

**Repro:** Fresh profile with SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ns-audit/r-settings-general/data. Wait 5 min or press Back Up Now. `find /tmp/ns-audit/r-settings-general -iname '*backup*'` -> nothing; `ls ~/Documents/Skotiq/backups` -> skotiq_autosave_2026-08-02T10-27-45.572326.nsbackup and two more from this run.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/backup_service.dart:1025-1032; packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/auto_save_settings.dart (Status section)`

**Proposed fix:** Add a 'Backup folder' path row to Files & Storage (browse + reveal), default it under the app data dir, and show the resolved path in the Status card.

### Importing a detailed .hor horizon silently collapses it to 8 sector maxima — a single tree blocks a whole 45-degree sector

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** I imported a 36-point .hor file (one sample every 10 deg of azimuth, altitudes 10-29 deg). The snackbar said 'Horizon profile imported from test36.hor' and the eight compass fields filled in as N=25, NE=16, E=21, SE=25, S=29, SW=14, W=19, NW=23. Those are exactly the MAXIMUM sample in each 45 deg sector — the other 28 samples were discarded with no mention. Source confirms this is deliberate (location_settings.dart:498-501, 'highest altitude per sector wins'), but nothing in the UI says so.

**Expected:** Real horizon files from Stellarium/HorizonBox have 1-degree resolution and are the reason people generate them. Max-per-sector means one 29-degree tree at azimuth 190 marks everything from 157.5 to 202.5 as blocked, so the planner and Tonight will refuse targets that are actually clear — this costs imaging time on exactly the targets a southern-horizon site cares about. At minimum the import must say 'imported 36 samples, reduced to 8 sectors (worst case per sector)'. Better: store the full profile and interpolate.

**Repro:** Create a .hor with one line per 10 deg azimuth; Settings > Location > Local Horizon Mask > Import .hor / CSV; compare the eight resulting fields with the file.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/location_settings.dart:498-539; LegacyHorizonProfile.parseHorizonText`

**Proposed fix:** Persist the full sampled profile and interpolate at query time; keep the 8 fields as a coarse editor/summary. Failing that, report the sample count and the reduction in the success message.

### Red night mode still paints the accent-colour swatches in full-saturation cyan, green and magenta

`defect` · settings (general / appearance / location / files-storage / connection / settings shell) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Settings > Appearance under Theme = Red night shows the 7 accent swatches at their literal colours: sampled (16,185,129) green, (245,158,11) amber, (6,182,212) cyan, (236,72,153) magenta. On the same page every other pixel is red-shifted.

**Expected:** Either suppress the swatch row in red night (the accent has no visible effect in a monochrome red theme anyway), or dim/annotate it. As shipped, opening Appearance at the telescope to check the theme is the thing that ruins your dark adaptation.

**Repro:** Settings > Appearance > Theme > Red night; the Accent color row is directly under Theme.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/appearance_settings.dart (accent colour swatch row)`

**Proposed fix:** Hide or grey the accent row when theme == redNight, or render each swatch as a red-luminance equivalent with a small label.

### Science tab says 'Science idle - Waiting for the first captured frame' while the session under analysis has 120 frames

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With Night D - V-Test (120 frames, 100% solved, full photometry) selected in the Analysing bar, the banner directly beneath reads 'Science idle / Waiting for the first captured frame', immediately above 'Plate solve health 100% - 120 of 120 solved'.

**Expected:** The banner reports the in-memory pipeline state for the current app process, which is empty after a restart. When the tab is reviewing a historical session it should say so ('Reviewing stored results') rather than claim no frame has been captured.

**Repro:** Restart the app, Analytics > Science, select a past session with frames.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/science_status_banner.dart:120-124 (the no-history else branch)`

**Proposed fix:** When the selected session is not the live one, render a review-mode subtitle keyed off the stored product counts instead of the live pipeline history.

### Dashboard Glance-mode toggle reports itself as DISABLED to assistive tech while working normally

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The accessibility tree reports 'button: Glance mode [off,DISABLED]'. Clicking it works - the same node then reads '[ON,DISABLED]' - so the control is live but permanently advertises itself as unavailable. Its Semantics wrapper sets button: true and toggled: enabled but never sets enabled: true, so the node never carries the ENABLED flag.

**Expected:** A working toggle must announce as enabled. As built a screen-reader user is told the dashboard's Glance-mode control is dimmed and will skip it.

**Repro:** Dashboard -> `drive_linux.py tree --profile r-shared-widgets | grep -i glance` -> click the eye icon in the dashboard header strip -> tree again.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/glance_mode_toggle.dart:60-64 (Semantics(button: true, toggled: enabled, label: 'Glance mode') - no `enabled:`); found while auditing its TouchTargetFloor wrapper at :73.`

**Proposed fix:** Add `enabled: !_saving` to that Semantics node. This is the same omission as the guide-controls finding above; a lint or a shared toggle widget would catch the class.

### 'Save as Template' accepts an empty sequence and says 'This will save 1 nodes'

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With a freshly created (empty) sequence, Templates > Save as Template opens with the info line 'This will save 1 nodes from the current sequence.' - ungrammatical, and the '1' is the invisible root container. Nothing prevents saving a template with no instructions in it.

**Expected:** Pluralise, count real instructions, and disable Save Template when there are none (with a reason).

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > toolbar 'New Sequence' > Templates tab > 'Save as Template'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/tabs/template_tab/_save_template_dialog.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Reuse the same corrected node count as the header fix, pluralise, and gate the primary action.

### Collaborative Sky dialogs render inside an empty full-height slab

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** 'Connect to a hub', 'Start co-imaging session' and 'Share your co-imaging subs' all draw their content card inside a taller, lighter-toned panel that runs from y=20 to y=700 of the 720 px window - roughly 300 px of empty surface above the dialog title and 150 px below its buttons. It reads as a rendering fault rather than a scrim: the slab is a distinct, lighter colour than the dimmed page behind it and has hard vertical edges at the dialog's width.

**Expected:** The dialog surface should wrap its content.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight -> Discover -> Collaborate -> Connect to a hub.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/collaborative_sky/ dialog scaffolding`

**Proposed fix:** Give the dialog container mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min / drop the background from the outer constrained box.

### The Slew split-button is invisible to assistive tech - no node at all in the accessibility tree

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With valid coordinates entered and the mount unparked, the accessibility tree for the GoTo / Sync card contains 'button: Sync' but no node named 'Slew' at all. The control is a PopupMenuButton whose visual child is wrapped in ExcludeSemantics, and the PopupMenuButton itself supplies no label or tooltip, so the primary GoTo action has no accessible name. The menu items ('button: Slew', 'button: Slew & Center') only appear once the menu is already open - which a screen-reader user has no way to discover.

**Expected:** The button should publish a name and enabled state, e.g. Semantics(button: true, label: 'Slew options') on the PopupMenuButton, so it is announced next to Sync.

**Repro:** Imaging > Mount > GoTo/Sync with RA/Dec filled in and the mount unparked -> `drive_linux.py tree --profile r-shared-widgets | grep -i slew`. Only 'ABORT SLEW' and 'Sync' come back.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/slew_dropdown_button.dart:153-181 (ExcludeSemantics around the SkotiqButton child of the PopupMenuButton)`

**Proposed fix:** Give the PopupMenuButton a tooltip/semanticLabel, or replace ExcludeSemantics with a MergeSemantics that keeps the 'Slew' label but suppresses the nested button gesture.

### Weather status card renders an epoch-zero timestamp as 'Updated 20667 days ago' before the first fetch lands

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On first paint of the Weather screen - before the RainViewer/Open-Meteo fetch returns - the card's footer reads 'Updated 20667 days ago'. 20667 days before 2026-08-02 is 1970-01-01, i.e. lastUpdate is DateTime.fromMillisecondsSinceEpoch(0), not null. Roughly 30 seconds later the same slot reads 'Updated 0 sec ago'.

**Expected:** With no data yet the card should say 'Waiting for data' (or hide the footer). Telling the operator their weather data is 56 years old is worse than saying nothing, and 'Updated 20667 days ago' is not a phrase any user can act on.

**Repro:** Launch fresh, click Weather in the sidebar, run `drive_linux.py tree --profile r-shared-widgets` within the first ~20 seconds while the radar is still loading.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/weather/weather_status_card.dart:242 ('Updated ${_formatLastUpdate(widget.lastUpdate!)}') with _formatLastUpdate at :120-137`

**Proposed fix:** Treat a lastUpdate at or near the epoch as absent: in the weather status provider leave lastUpdate null until the first successful fetch, and have _formatLastUpdate return 'never' for anything older than, say, a day.

### Guiding-control buttons are invisible to assistive tech: exposed as generic panels with no button role and no disabled state

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The accessibility tree for the Guiding Controls block reports 'panel: Start', 'panel: Pause', 'panel: Loop Exposures', 'panel: Auto Select', 'panel: Deselect', 'panel: Dither Now' — role 'panel', never 'button', and never [DISABLED] even though all six are logically disabled (onPressed == null) while PHD2 is disconnected. Every other button in the app in the same tree dump reports 'button: <label>' and picks up [DISABLED] correctly (e.g. 'panel: Settle Settings [DISABLED]', 'button: Connect').

**Expected:** These are the controls that start and stop guiding. They should expose a button role and their enabled/disabled state, so a screen-reader user is told 'Start, button, dimmed' rather than a nameless panel.

**Repro:** Guiding screen with PHD2 disconnected -> drive_linux.py tree -> read the Guiding Controls subtree.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/phd2/guide_controls_panel.dart (_actionButton builds a bare Material+InkWell with no Semantics(button: true, enabled: ...))`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the InkWell in Semantics(button: true, enabled: !isDisabled, label: label) or use the design-system button widget that already publishes those flags.

### Dismissing a blocking readiness item hides it while the summary keeps counting it, so the panel claims 3 blockers and lists 2

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicking the X on the blocking 'Critical devices' row (camera not connected) removes the row from the rail, but the summary line above it still reads '3 items are blocking first light.' while only 2 blocking rows remain visible and the hidden one is only reachable via a small 'Show 1 dismissed' link.

**Expected:** Either blocking items should not be dismissable at all (NINA/SGP do not let you hide a hard blocker), or the summary must say '3 blocking (1 hidden)' so the count and the list agree.

**Repro:** Equipment -> right STATUS rail -> click the X on 'Critical devices' -> read the summary line vs the rows shown.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/readiness/readiness_panel.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Exclude dismissed items from the blocking count, or annotate the count with the hidden total.

### 3D Surface Explorer's "Contours" toggle draws vertical drop-lines, not contours

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > 3D Surface Explorer > click the 'Contours' chip. Every tile marker sprouts a straight vertical line down to a flat horizontal baseline at the bottom of the card - a stem/lollipop plot. No iso-value contour is drawn anywhere, and because the baseline is a single horizontal y the drop-lines also break the 3D projection they hang inside.

**Expected:** A control called Contours draws iso-value lines across the surface. If drop-lines are what is wanted, call the control 'Drop lines'.

**Repro:** Seed science_tile_metrics rows (layer_type='uniformity', 5x5 grid) for the selected session's frames. Restart. Analytics > Science, scroll to 3D Surface Explorer, click Contours.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/science_surface_explorer.dart:325-331 `if (showContour) { canvas.drawLine(point, Offset(point.dx, size.height - 10), linePaint); }``

**Proposed fix:** Either rename the chip to 'Drop lines', or implement marching-squares iso-lines over the interpolated tile grid at 4-6 levels.

### Readiness panel reports a green "Profile devices — every device is connected" over an empty profile with nothing connected

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On a fresh profile (My Equipment, 0 devices) with camera/mount/guider all disconnected, the readiness panel shows a GREEN dot on 'Profile devices' with the text 'Every device assigned in the active profile is connected.' The status bar in the same frame reads 'Camera Disconnected  Mount Disconnected  Guider Disconnected'.

**Expected:** With zero devices assigned the row should read 'No devices assigned to this profile' in a neutral/warn state, not a green all-connected pass. A vacuous truth over an empty set is the single most misleading thing a readiness panel can say.

**Repro:** Fresh profile -> Equipment -> 'I'll do it manually' -> right STATUS rail -> 'View all (3 more)' -> read the 'Profile devices' row while the status bar shows every device disconnected.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/readiness/readiness_panel.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Special-case the empty-assignment case in the profile-devices readiness check: emit a distinct 'no devices assigned' item (warn) instead of the all-connected pass.

### The same mosaic is described as "3x2 grid" on one screen and "2x3 grid" on the other

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** A project stored as rows=2, cols=3 is rendered '3x2 grid - 6 panels' by the Mosaic projects list and the project header, and '2x3 grid' by the Collaborative mosaics card for the very same mosaic. Nothing on either screen says whether the first number is columns or rows.

**Expected:** One convention, stated once (e.g. '3 wide x 2 high').

**Repro:** Publish a mosaic, then compare the project header with the Collaborate list entry.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/mosaic/mosaic_project_screen.dart + mosaic_projects_list_screen.dart vs packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/collaborative_sky/`

**Proposed fix:** A single shared formatter, e.g. '${cols} x ${rows} panels (w x h)'.

### "Box Least Squares (Transit Search)" reports negative transit depths, i.e. it presents a brightening as a transit

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** For pure noise the BLS block read 'Depth: -53.6 mmag' and for an injected sinusoid 'Depth: -115.9 mmag'. A negative depth means the best-fitting box is BRIGHTER than the out-of-box baseline - an anti-transit. The card labels it 'Depth' under the heading 'Box Least Squares (Transit Search)' with no sign explanation and no warning. Only when a genuine box transit was injected did the sign flip positive (151.2 mmag for an injected 150 mmag).

**Expected:** A transit search should either restrict the box search to fainter-than-baseline solutions, or clearly flag a negative-depth solution as an anti-transit / brightening event rather than presenting it as the session's transit result.

**Repro:** Seed 80 noisy photometry points with no transit; Analytics > Science > Period Analysis > Run Period Search; read the BLS block.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/science/period_analysis_service.dart:536-545 maximises SR = s^2/(r(1-r)), which is sign-blind, and :597 sets globalBestDepth = inTransitMean - outTransitMean without constraining the sign`

**Proposed fix:** Reject box solutions where s < 0 (in-box brighter) when the mode is transit search, or render negative depths as 'Brightening candidate (anti-transit): 115.9 mmag' so the user is not told a transit was found.

### Glance mode toggle on the Dashboard changes nothing on the Dashboard

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The eye icon in the dashboard command bar is the Glance mode toggle ('bumps the dashboard's secondary-status type up so the run can be read from across the room'). Toggling it on tints the button and the tooltip flips to 'Glance mode: on', but a pixel diff of the whole dashboard content area (1000x700 px) between on and off differs in only a 4x12 px sliver at the panel edge - 0.005% of pixels. No text on the dashboard gets larger.

**Expected:** Either the dashboard's status readouts (target, exposure countdown, HFR, RMS, frame counts) scale up when glance mode is on, or the toggle does not live on the dashboard.

**Repro:** Dashboard -> click the eye icon in the command bar (right of the Local chip) -> click it again. Compare /tmp/ns-audit/shots/glance-on.png and /tmp/ns-audit/shots/glance-off.png.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/glance_mode_toggle.dart (control) vs the two glanceStyle consumers in the sequencer run dashboard`

**Proposed fix:** Apply SkotiqTypography.glanceStyle to the dashboard command-bar metrics, the Now Imaging / target strip and the exposure countdown, or move the toggle to the sequencer run dashboard where its two consumers actually live.

### Snippet palette inserts nothing on tap or on its + button; only drag works, while the panel says 'or tap to insert'

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The Snippets panel advertises '19 snippets available' and its footer reads 'Drag templates to the sequence tree or tap to insert'. With the root 'Sequence' InstructionSet selected (Properties confirmed: InstructionSet, 3 children) I tapped 'Autofocus Routine' and 'HFR-Triggered AF', and separately clicked each card's explicit '+' button. Node count stayed at 26 and the root stayed at '3 steps' every time. No snackbar, no dialog, and the app log stayed silent. Dragging the same card into the tree with a real press-move-release DID insert it (26 -> 27 nodes, root 3 -> 4 steps).

**Expected:** Tapping a snippet card, or clicking its + button, inserts the snippet - or, if insertion is impossible, says why.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > Snippets tab. Click the root 'Sequence' row in the tree so a container is selected. Tap the 'Autofocus Routine' card body; check the node count in the header. Then click that card's '+'; check again. Then press and drag the card onto the tree and release: only this last one works.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/sequencer_screen_parts/snippet_palette.dart:10-33 (onSnippetTap -> withSequenceMutation -> insertSnippet); card affordances in widgets/snippet_palette/actions.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Trace why the tap path never reaches insertSnippet (the drag path shares the same TemplateSnippet payload and works, so the snippet data is fine). Wire the card's + button to the same handler as the drag target, and if withSequenceMutation swallows an exception, surface it - a silent catch on a user-initiated action is what makes this invisible.

### Dashboard Widgets picker lists two different widgets both called "Guiding"

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Dashboard Widgets dialog contains two separate rows named "Guiding": one described 'RMS error and guiding graph' (checked by default) and, ~10 rows later, another described 'RMS and guiding graph' (unchecked). There is also a 'Weather & Safety' and a plain 'Weather', and an 'Equipment Telemetry' and a plain 'Equipment'. A user cannot tell which one they are toggling.

**Expected:** Every row in the picker has a unique, distinguishable name, or the legacy/duplicate entries are removed.

**Repro:** Dashboard -> Edit Dashboard -> Widgets -> scroll the list.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/dashboard/widgets/widget_picker_dialog.dart and the widget registry that feeds it`

**Proposed fix:** De-duplicate the registry (the second set looks like the pre-cockpit widget ids) or rename them so the distinction is explicit.

### Session tab claims "No frames are deleted or auto-rejected" directly above frames the app auto-rejected

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The Captured Images block on Analytics > Session carries the caption 'Quality labels are advisory only. No frames are deleted or auto-rejected.' Immediately after using Analytics > Science > 'Grade 2 frames' to reject both of Night E's frames, the session header correctly flipped to 'EXPOSURES 0/2' and the DB rows carry rejection_reason = 'Auto-grade: Guiding 0.60" > 0.24"' - but the caption still asserts that nothing is auto-rejected.

**Expected:** The caption should be scoped to the advisory Good/Poor badges ('These badges are advisory - they do not reject frames on their own'), because the app ships an auto-grader two tabs away that does reject frames and stamps them 'Auto-grade:'.

**Repro:** Analytics > Science > Grade N frames > lower a threshold > Reject; then Analytics > Session and read the Captured Images caption.

### Pre-flight error title 'No filter wheel for SmartExposure' is untrue — a filter wheel is connected; the body says the real cause

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **CONFIRMED**

The body reads correctly: 'requires a filter wheel, but the active equipment profile has no filters configured.' A user reads the heading, goes to Equipment, sees the wheel connected, and concludes the app is broken.

### Toolbar's 13 icon buttons expose no accessible names at all

`defect` · sequencer · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The AT-SPI tree for the Sequencer screen contains exactly one named toolbar control: 'button: Start'. The other thirteen actions (New Sequence, Quick-Start Wizard, Calibrate Flat Exposures, Plan Mosaic, Plan Tonight, Open Sequence, Import from NINA/SGP, Save Sequence, Polar Alignment, Exposure Triggers, Slew to Target, Undo, Redo) appear nowhere in the tree and carry no visible text label, only a Lucide glyph. They are unreachable by a screen reader and undiscoverable without hovering each one.

**Expected:** Each icon button exposes its tooltip text as its semantics label, as the tab bar buttons already do ('button: Builder (Alt+1)').

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder, then dump the accessibility tree (tools/ui_audit/drive_linux.py tree --profile <p>): only 'button: Start' appears from the toolbar row.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/sequence_toolbar.dart:439-521 (_ToolbarAction / _ToolbarIconButton)`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap each _ToolbarIconButton in Semantics(button: true, label: action.label) - the label strings already exist and are used for tooltips.

### The mosaic project screen's back breadcrumb always says "Mosaic projects" even when it returns somewhere else

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** I reached /mosaic/:id from the Collaborative Sky mosaic detail (via 'Join mosaic'), not from the Mosaic projects list. The header breadcrumb still read '< Mosaic projects'. Pressing it returned me to the Collaborative Sky mosaic detail page, not to the mosaic projects list.

**Expected:** Either label the control generically ('Back') or label it with the actual previous route.

**Repro:** Collaborate -> tap a mosaic -> Join mosaic -> press the '< Mosaic projects' breadcrumb.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/mosaic/mosaic_project_screen.dart (hard-coded breadcrumb label)`

**Proposed fix:** Use a plain back affordance, or pass the origin label through the route extra.

### Guiding chart Y axis draws two labels on top of each other when the series is flat

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** With all three frames at guiding RMS 0.70, the Guiding performance axis renders 0.25 and 0.20 overlapping into an illegible blob at the bottom of the axis.

**Expected:** Axis labels never overlap - which is exactly what NiceAxis was written to guarantee.

**Repro:** Seed frames with identical guiding_rms_total (0.70); Analytics > Session; look at the bottom-left of the Guiding performance chart.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/chart_axis.dart:33-43 (zero-variance branch)`

**Proposed fix:** The zero-variance branch returns min = centre - 2*interval without snapping to a tick multiple (0.70 - 0.50 = 0.20 with interval 0.25), so the boundary label lands 0.05 from the 0.25 tick. Snap: min = (centre/interval).floor()*interval - 2*interval, same for max - the same snapping the varying branch already does.

### Mount jog arrows are silently dead while the mount is parked

`defect` · dashboard, mosaic, polar_alignment, flat_wizard, collaborative_sky · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** While the mount reported Parked, clicking the E arrow produced no motion, no toast and no log entry at all (the first 'Pulse guiding' line in the native log appears only after Unpark). The pad's only disabled affordance is a slightly dimmer icon colour, which is not distinguishable from the enabled state on this dark theme.

**Expected:** Either disable the pad visibly (dim the whole pad / show a 'Mount is parked' tooltip or inline hint), or accept the press and surface 'Mount is parked - unpark first'.

**Repro:** Dashboard with Mount Control widget, mount Parked -> click any arrow. Compare /tmp/ns-audit/shots/r-wizards-28.png (parked) with r-wizards-29.png (unparked): the arrow pad renders identically.

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the pad in an Opacity/overlay when disabled and set the Tooltip message to the reason ('Mount is parked').

### 'Connect All' silently connects a safety monitor that is not in the profile, and did not do so on an identical earlier click

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The 'Connect All' button lives under the profile card in the profile sidebar, so it reads as 'connect this profile's devices'. The first press (15:11:28 UTC) connected exactly the four profile devices. A second, identical press five minutes later (15:16:32) connected the same four plus sim_safety_monitor_1, which is not assigned to the profile -- the header pill changed to '5 connected - 1 unsaved' while the profile card and status bar both still said 4/4. The only thing that changed in between was that I had expanded the Discovery panel, which re-ran discovery. The device card does carry an honest note ('This session only -- not saved to your profile, so it will not reconnect next launch. Use Assign in Discovery to keep it.'), but the same button producing different results on two identical presses is the problem.

**Expected:** Connect All should have one defined scope. If it deliberately picks up safety monitors, do it consistently and label the button accordingly ('Connect profile + safety'); otherwise restrict it to the profile.

**Repro:** 1. Equipment > Connect All (log shows 4 connects). 2. Expand the Discovery panel (discovery re-runs). 3. Disconnect All, then Connect All again -> the log now also shows 'Connecting to Safety Monitor device: sim_safety_monitor_1' and the pill reads '5 connected - 1 unsaved'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/command_handlers.dart (connect-all scope)`

**Proposed fix:** Make the connect-all set deterministic from the profile (plus an explicit, documented safety-device rule) rather than from whatever the last discovery happened to cache.

### Transient alert popover reports a -2.35 delta-mag brightening as 'mag 2.4'

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** A detection with delta_mag = -2.35 (a 2.35-magnitude brightening) renders in the title-bar notification popover as 'NS J0.71+41.3 / Other / mag 2.4 / NEW'. The candidate card itself correctly shows '-2.35 mag' with an up-arrow, so the two surfaces disagree. 'mag 2.4' reads as an apparent magnitude of 2.4 -- a naked-eye star -- not as a delta. The designation 'NS J0.71+41.3' also encodes RA in hours rather than the IAU JHHMMSS.s+DDMMSS form the TNS expects, for a feature whose stated purpose is TNS submission.

**Expected:** Show the signed delta with its symbol, e.g. 'delta-mag -2.35', matching the card. Use an IAU-form designation (this object is J004244.30+411607.3).

**Repro:** 1. Have a transient_detections row with delta_mag = -2.35, ra_deg 10.6847, dec_deg 41.2687. 2. Click the bell in the title bar. 3. Popover reads 'NS J0.71+41.3 ... mag 2.4'. 4. Compare with the card in Analytics > Science > First Light, which reads '-2.35 mag'.

**Where:** `transient alert popover formatter and the designation builder feeding it`

**Proposed fix:** Format delta magnitudes with sign and a delta label everywhere, and build designations from the sexagesimal RA/Dec the card already computes correctly.

### Two profiles can share the same name, making the delete confirmation genuinely ambiguous

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** The New Profile dialog validates an empty name ('Fix 1 problem before saving - Profile name is required') but accepts a name identical to an existing profile. I created a second profile also called 'My First Rig'; the sidebar then showed two entries distinguishable only by their subtitle ('Sky-Watcher Esprit 100ED + Sim...' vs '0 devices'), the screen header, the status bar and the Delete confirmation all just say 'My First Rig'. The delete dialog reading 'Delete "My First Rig"? This cannot be undone.' cannot tell the user which of the two is about to be destroyed. The second profile also saved with no devices and focal_length 0.0 with no warning.

**Expected:** Reject or auto-suffix duplicate profile names, and include a disambiguator (devices/telescope) in the delete confirmation.

**Repro:** 1. Equipment > '+' > name it exactly the same as an existing profile > Save Changes. 2. sqlite3 <db> 'select id,name from equipment_profiles' -> two rows, same name. 3. Right-click either > Delete: the dialog quotes only the shared name.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/profile_editor_dialog.dart (name validation)`

**Proposed fix:** Add a uniqueness check to the same validator that already catches the empty name, or append ' (2)'.

### The delete-profile confirmation says 'This cannot be undone' and then offers a working Undo

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Right-click a profile > Delete raises 'Delete Profile / Delete "My First Rig"? This cannot be undone.' Confirming produces a snackbar with an Undo action, and Undo genuinely restores the profile in full: after undoing the deletion of my only profile the row came back with name, focal_length 550.0, filter_names ["LumTest","R","G","B","Ha","OIII","SII"], default_gain 100, cool_on_connect 1, is_default 1, is_active 1.

**Proposed fix:** Change the copy to 'Delete "<name>"? You can undo this for a few seconds.'

### The onboarding wizard's filter step lets you invent slots the wheel does not have

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** After selecting the Simulated Filter Wheel the step listed its real 7 slots (L, R, G, B, Ha, OIII, SII). Pressing 'Add slot' immediately created an 8th row pre-filled 'Filter 8' with no warning that the connected wheel only reports 7 positions. Deleting a slot is also unconfirmed and renumbers everything below it, which would silently re-map per-filter focus offsets.

**Expected:** Cap the slot count at what the wheel reports (or warn clearly that slot 8 does not exist on this wheel), and warn that deleting a middle slot renumbers the ones below it.

**Repro:** 1. Onboarding step 6, select the Simulated Filter Wheel (7 slots appear). 2. Click 'Add slot' -> row 8 'Filter 8' appears with no warning.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/onboarding/filter_wheel_step.dart (Add slot handler)`

**Proposed fix:** Disable 'Add slot' once the list reaches the wheel's reported position count, with a tooltip naming the count.

### First Light 'Retry' button never re-runs the query

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The First Light candidate list failed to load and rendered 'Could not load candidates / FormatException: Invalid radix-10 number (at character 1) / tile-1 / ^' with a 'Retry' button. I corrected the underlying row in the database, then clicked Retry three times over several minutes: the identical error re-rendered every time, still quoting the old value 'tile-1' that no longer exists in the database. Restarting the app made the list load correctly and render the candidate, proving the data was fine and Retry simply never re-executed the query. Separately, this error state leaks a raw Dart exception (class name and caret diagram) straight into the user-facing UI.

**Expected:** Retry must invalidate and re-run the provider. The error surface should show a human message plus a copyable detail, not a raw FormatException with a caret.

**Repro:** 1. Put a row in transient_detections whose tile_id cannot be parsed as an int. 2. Analytics > Science > First Light -> error state. 3. Fix the row in the DB. 4. Click Retry repeatedly -> same error, same stale value. 5. Restart the app -> loads fine.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/first_light/first_light_view.dart (error/retry branch)`

**Proposed fix:** Have Retry call ref.invalidate on the candidates provider. Wrap the error in a user-facing message and keep the exception behind a 'details' expander.

### 'Connect All result' strip keeps claiming devices are Connected after they have been disconnected

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** After Connect All, a persistent strip sits under the header reading 'Connect All result | camera Connected | mount Connected | focuser Connected | filter wheel Connected | Clear'. I then disconnected the camera: its card vanished, the header pill correctly changed to '3 connected' and the status bar to '3/4 | Camera Disconnected' -- but the strip still showed a green 'camera Connected'. Worse, after Disconnect All the strip still showed all four green 'Connected' chips while the main area said 'No devices connected' and the pill said 0/4. On the next launch the same strip is relabelled 'Startup auto-connect result' and behaves the same way.

**Expected:** Either clear/update the strip when connection state changes, or label it unambiguously as a historical record with a timestamp ('Connect All at 11:16: 4 of 4 succeeded') so a green 'Connected' chip is never read as current state.

**Repro:** 1. Equipment > Connect All (strip appears, 4 green chips). 2. Click the disconnect icon on the camera card > 'Disconnect anyway'. 3. Strip still shows 'camera Connected' while the pill reads '3 connected'. 4. Disconnect All -> strip still shows all four Connected next to 'No devices connected'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/equipment/equipment_screen.dart (connect-all result strip)`

**Proposed fix:** Re-render the strip from live device state, or freeze it as a timestamped result banner that visually cannot be confused with the live status chips.

### Onboarding's 'Regulated cooling' toggle never enables cooling; the camera runs at ambient every session

`defect` · equipment, diagnostics, shell, onboarding, first_light, tutorial · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Onboarding step 9 'Set your capture defaults' has a 'Regulated cooling' switch ('Turn on for a cooled camera; leave off for a DSLR or an uncooled sensor'). I turned it ON and it revealed 'Cooling set-point -10 C'. The saved profile has default_cooling_temp=-10.0 but cool_on_connect=0. On every connect the camera sat at 20.0 C with the cooler at 0%, and the app log shows it polling and doing nothing: '[CameraTemperaturePoller] Camera temp: 20.0C, power: 0%, target: -10.0C'. The connect sequence issues camera_set_gain and camera_set_offset but no cooling command at all. The manual 'Cool to -10C' button works (reaches -10.0 C / 35% within ~50 s), and ticking the hidden 'Cool on connect' checkbox inside Equipment > Edit Profile > Camera Defaults makes the camera cool automatically on the next Connect All -- so the feature works; only the wizard fails to wire it. There is also no cooling control anywhere in Settings: searching Settings for 'cool' returns 'No settings match your search', while app_settings holds cooling_behavior='On Connect' with no UI and no effect.

**Expected:** Turning on 'Regulated cooling' with a set-point in the wizard should set cool_on_connect=1 so the camera actually reaches the set-point when it connects. Otherwise the switch is decorative and a first-run user images all night on a warm sensor.

**Repro:** 1. Fresh profile, walk onboarding, step 9: turn 'Regulated cooling' ON, leave set-point -10. Save profile. 2. sqlite3 <db> 'select cool_on_connect,default_cooling_temp from equipment_profiles' -> 0 | -10.0. 3. Equipment > Connect All. Camera card shows 20.0 C / 0% / Idle indefinitely. 4. Equipment > Edit Profile > Camera Defaults > tick 'Cool on connect' > Save. 5. Disconnect All, Connect All -> camera card shows -10.0 C / 35%.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/onboarding/onboarding_screen.dart (camera-defaults step -> profile mapping); equipment_profiles.cool_on_connect`

**Proposed fix:** Map the wizard's 'Regulated cooling' switch to cool_on_connect (and keep default_cooling_temp for the set-point). Also either surface or delete the orphaned app_settings key cooling_behavior='On Connect', which has no UI and no effect.

Neither narrative_view.dart nor workbench_view.dart references it, and driving the app confirms it: the narrative view renders hero preview, Night Doctor, improvement curve, findings, project growth and the calibration cards, and contains no sub grid at all. sub_cull_rail.dart:24 states 'One controller, two renderings: the narrative screen uses SubGalleryPanel', which is not true of the shipped build.

### Night Outlook hero card freezes its "Alt" and "Airmass" readouts while the list card for the same object keeps updating

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

In between, the candidate-list card for the same object showed 'Alt: 15.1° Airmass: 3.79' at 10:09:24. The labels are present tense, so the hero card is stating a stale altitude as the current one.

**Proposed fix:** Recompute currentAltitude/airmass for the primary card on the same ticker the candidate list uses, or drop the live readout from the hero and keep only the whole-night figures (peak, transit, window hours) that the section header promises.

### The minimum-altitude gate is 25 deg in the scheduler and 30 deg in the planner/sequence builder

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Plan Tonight > Schedule > Scoring weights shows 'Min altitude 25.00°', and the decision panel rejects with 'M31: altitude 24.3° below site minimum 25.0°'. On the same screen, the Recommendation altitude charts draw their threshold line at 30°, the chips read 'hours above minimum altitude' computed at 30°, and the sequence builder errors with '... at min altitude 30°'. A target sitting at 27° is therefore simultaneously eligible for the autopilot and un-sequenceable.

**Expected:** One minimum-altitude value per site, surfaced in one place, used by the scheduler, the outlook scoring and the sequence builder alike.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Schedule > expand 'Scoring weights' (read 25.00°); Plan Tonight > Recommendation > click 'Review in Sequencer' on any non-circumpolar target (error names 30°).

**Where:** `SchedulerWeights.minAltitudeDegrees (packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/scheduler/scheduler_status.dart) vs SmartNightSettings.minAltitudeDeg used at packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/smart_night_service.dart:699-711; note also targets.min_altitude defaults to 30.0 in the DB schema and is not what the reasoning text reports`

**Proposed fix:** Make the scheduler weight the single source of truth and feed it into SmartNightSettings.minAltitudeDeg and into the outlook scoring, or expose a site-level 'minimum altitude' in Settings > Location that all three read. Also decide whether the per-target targets.min_altitude column is live; today it is 30 by default and the reasoning text quotes the global 25.

### "Best night" star lands on the worst night of the week when the project has no targets

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** On a fresh profile, Plan Tonight > Schedule > This Week showed Sat 1 with the star icon and the selected-card highlight, reading '0.0 / 6.2h clear', while Wed 5 in the same strip read '5.0 / 6.4h clear' with no star. NightForecast.score returns 0 whenever bestTargets is empty, so with no project targets every night scores 0, and WeekForecast.bestNight's tie-break keeps the first (earliest) night. The strip therefore recommends a fully-clouded night as the best of the week. Once I added targets to the project the star correctly moved to Wed 5.

**Expected:** With no night scoring above zero, highlight nothing (bestNight == null) — or fall back to ranking by clearDarkHours so the strip is still useful. Never star a 0.0-clear-hours night while a 5.0-clear-hours night is on screen.

**Repro:** Fresh profile with a location set and no project targets: Plan Tonight > Schedule > look at the This Week strip.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/models/planning/night_forecast.dart:205-214 (bestNight) with the score-gate at :132-139`

**Proposed fix:** In bestNight, return null when the maximum score is 0. The widget already renders fine with best == null (WeekForecastStrip passes `isBest: best != null && identical(...)`).

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

The Regions header immediately read '1 region imaged'. Directly above it the Atlas coverage strip correctly reads 0s Integration / 0 Tiles / 0 Frames, and the region card itself reads '0s' and '0 tiles'. Naming a patch of sky is not imaging it, and the screen's own numbers say so.

**Expected:** '1 region' (or '1 region, none imaged yet'). The count should say what it counts; 'imaged' is a claim about captured data that the same screen contradicts twice.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Discover > Your Sky > Name a region > Custom RA/Dec > RA 283.4, Dec 33.03, name anything > Create region. Read the Regions header against the Atlas coverage tiles.

**Proposed fix:** Pluralise on the region count alone, and only say 'imaged' for regions with frames > 0.

### Filter Focus Offsets silently omits the filter you just added -- you cannot set an offset for it without saving and re-opening the editor

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** In Settings > Equipment Profiles > (profile) > Edit, I added a 4th filter 'SII' with the + button and typed its name. Filter Configuration then showed 4 chips (Lum, Ha, OIII, SII) but the Filter Focus Offsets card directly underneath still listed only 3 rows (Lum, Ha, OIII) -- no offset input exists for SII. The section is built from _filterControllers, but nothing calls setState when a filter-name field changes, so the offsets list lags one interaction behind. Pressing Save and re-entering Edit made the SII row appear. Same lag on the very first filter: the offsets section does not appear at all until some other widget forces a rebuild.

**Expected:** Naming a filter should immediately add its focus-offset row; the two lists describe the same filters and must stay in sync within one edit session.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > New Profile > Edit > Filter Configuration '+' > type a filter name > look at Filter Focus Offsets: the new filter is missing. Save, re-open Edit: now present.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/equipment_profiles_screen_parts/profile_details_helpers.dart:154-165 (_buildFilterOffsetRows reads _filterControllers) with no onChanged->setState on the filter-name TextFields in profile_details_rendering.dart:449-500`

**Proposed fix:** Add onChanged: (_) => setState(() {}) to the filter-name fields (or drive both lists off a single ValueNotifier).

### Sunrise and sunset are 3.5-4 minutes wrong (atmospheric refraction counted twice)

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The dashboard night-timeline strip for 40.0N/-75.0W on 2026-08-01 shows Sunset 20:17 and Sunrise 05:55 (and Astro dark 22:01, Astro dawn 04:11). Ground truth (astropy, 15-second grid, sun centre at the standard -0.833 deg upper-limb altitude): sunset 20:13:30, sunrise 05:59:15, astronomical dusk 22:01:30, astronomical dawn 04:11:15. The two twilight numbers are right to under a minute; only sunrise/sunset are off, and symmetrically outward — the app reports 7.5 minutes more daylight than reality. Reverse-solving, the app's sunset time corresponds to a geometric sun altitude of about -1.4 deg, i.e. -0.833 deg plus another ~34' of refraction.

**Expected:** Sunset 20:13 / sunrise 05:59 (matching USNO/NOAA/astropy to the minute).

**Repro:** Set location 40 / -75 / 100m, open Dashboard, read the 'ASTRO DARK IN' timeline strip labels.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/astronomy/astronomy_calculations.dart:940-955 (_apparentAltitudeOf returns trueToApparentAltitude(trueAlt), i.e. geometric + ~34' refraction) used as the search function at :612 and :668 against `_sunRiseSetAltitude = -0.8333` (:483-484), whose own comment says it is "-34' (refraction) - 16' (semi-diameter)"`

**Proposed fix:** Pick one convention. Either search the GEOMETRIC altitude for -0.8333 deg, or keep searching apparent altitude and change the target to -16' (-0.2667 deg, semi-diameter only). The same double-count applies to `_moonRiseSetAltitude = -0.7` at :486-487, so moonrise/moonset are biased the same way. Twilights are unaffected because the Bennett refraction term is suppressed well below the horizon (:139).

Both count toward the '5 warnings' badge, so the summary overstates how many distinct problems exist.

**Expected:** One issue per real problem.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > add a Dither node with no guider connected > Start > scroll the Pre-Flight list.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/sequence/rules/equipment_rules.dart (two rules producing the same finding)`

**Proposed fix:** De-duplicate by (category, equipment kind) before the dialog renders, keeping the more specific message.

### Sequencer crash-recovery checkpoint is shared by every instance on the machine; a second instance is offered another run's live sequence and cannot dismiss the prompt

`defect` · planner, scheduler, tonight, suggestions, weather, transients · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** My sandboxed instance (SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR=SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR=/tmp/ns-audit/planning/data; DB had 0 sequences, 0 sequence_runs, 0 sequence_checkpoints, 0 captured_images) put up a modal "Recover Sequence? A previous sequence was interrupted and can be resumed. Sequence: New Sequence, Saved: 2m ago, Completed: 6 frames (0m integration)". The log line was 'Setting checkpoint directory to: /home/scdouglas/.local/share/com.skotiq.desktop' — the shared user support dir, not the configured data dir. That file (skotiq_session.checkpoint, mtime advancing every few seconds, containing a 20x3s "Take Exposures" node I never created) belongs to a different Skotiq process running concurrently. Clicking "Discard" three times did not dismiss the dialog — the other process rewrote the checkpoint within ~3s each time, so the app re-detected it and re-prompted, leaving the GUI wedged behind a modal barrier (barrierDismissible:false, Escape ignored). I had to relaunch with XDG_DATA_HOME redirected to escape it.

**Expected:** The checkpoint must live under the instance's own data root (SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR / the profile's directory), so two Skotiq processes on one machine — the documented desktop-GUI + headless-appliance pairing, or two profiles — never see each other's run. "Resume" here would have loaded and started a sequence belonging to another rig.

**Repro:** 1. Run two Skotiq desktop instances on one machine with different SKOTIQ_DATABASE_DIR/SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR. 2. Start a sequence in instance A. 3. Launch/relaunch instance B -> "Recover Sequence?" appears in B describing A's run. 4. Click Discard repeatedly while A is still checkpointing -> dialog reappears each time.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart:346-348 (`final supportDir = await getApplicationSupportDirectory(); await backend.sequencerSetCheckpointDir(supportDir.path);`)`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the checkpoint directory the same way the DB path is resolved — honour SKOTIQ_DATA_DIR (and the headless code's `<support>/checkpoints` sub-directory convention from apps/desktop/lib/headless/host_checkpoint_directory.dart:14-33) instead of the bare application-support root. Additionally, after a Discard that is immediately followed by a fresh checkpoint from another writer, do not silently re-prompt in a loop: record the discarded checkpoint id/timestamp and suppress it, and give the dialog a "Not now" escape.

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED**

### FITS omits XPIXSZ/YPIXSZ (and OBJECT) although onboarding collects pixel size and uses it to compute image scale

`defect` · imaging, framing, guiding · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Onboarding's optical-train step asks for 'Camera pixel size' (I entered 3.76 um) and immediately shows 'Image scale 1.46 arcsec/px' computed from it, and the Framing OPTICAL CONFIG card displays '(3.76um)'. Yet every written FITS has FOCALLEN and APTDIA but no XPIXSZ/YPIXSZ and no OBJECT. The Dart header builder hard-codes `pixelSizeX: null, pixelSizeY: null` with the comment 'Pixel size not stored in profile yet', which is no longer true. Without XPIXSZ many downstream tools (ASTAP blind solve hints, PixInsight, AstroBin) cannot derive plate scale from the file alone.

**Expected:** XPIXSZ/YPIXSZ written from the profile pixel size; OBJECT written when a target is known.

**Repro:** 1. Onboard entering pixel size 3.76. 2. Imaging -> Snapshot. 3. Dump the FITS header.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/imaging_service/persistence.dart:73-74 ('pixelSizeX: null, // Pixel size not stored in profile yet')`

**Proposed fix:** Read pixel size from the active profile (or the camera status) and pass it through; delete the stale comment.

### 'Install' on the deep-star tier leads to a form asking the user to build and self-host a tileset - no tileset exists to install

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** The Layers panel row 'Deep stars (Tycho-2 / Gaia tier)' carries a button labelled 'Install'. Pressing it opens Catalog Settings; scrolling to the matching card reveals 'Deep-Star Tier (Tycho-2 / Gaia)' with an empty 'Tileset base URL' text field, a Download button, and the line 'No official tileset is published yet - point this at a tileset you host yourself (see tools/catalog_prep in the Skotiq repository).' So the button named Install cannot install anything: the user is expected to clone the repo, run a catalog-preparation tool, and stand up a web server. Meanwhile the same row is what the app blames for the empty sky at imaging FOVs.

**Expected:** Either publish and host the tileset (the DSS survey layer already proves the app can fetch remote sky data), or stop calling the button 'Install' - label it 'Configure source...' and say up front that no public tileset exists. As shipped this is the single biggest gap between what the planetarium claims and what it can draw.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > Layers > Install (on the Deep stars row) > scroll Catalog Settings to 'Deep-Star Tier (Tycho-2 / Gaia)'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/deep_star_catalog_card.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Ship a hosted Tycho-2 tileset (a catalogs-vN GitHub release already exists for the other catalogs) or relabel the button and the layer subtitle to match reality.

### The app states three different, all-wrong depths for the bundled star catalog: 'mag <= 15.0', '~mag 11.5' and 'below mag 11.5 unavailable'

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Reached from the planetarium's Layers panel > Deep stars > Install, the Catalog Settings sheet describes the installed HYG Star Database as '119.6k objects, 32.4 MB, Version 4.2, Package Complete, Depth mag <= 15.0'. Two cards further down, the Deep-Star Tier card says the same bundled catalog's floor is '~mag 11.5'. The Layers panel itself says 'stars below mag 11.5 are unavailable'. All three are wrong in the same direction: 119,600 stars spread over 41,253 square degrees is 2.9 stars per square degree, and that is exactly the density I measured on screen (about 25 stars in a 3.8 x 2.0 deg field at FOV 2.0). A catalog complete to mag 15 would contain tens of millions of stars; HYG (Hipparcos + Yale BSC + Gliese) is complete to roughly magnitude 9. 'Package: Complete' next to 'Depth: mag <= 15.0' tells the user the empty field they are looking at is the real sky.

**Expected:** State the completeness limit, not the faintest entry: about mag 9 for HYG. And make the three surfaces agree.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > command bar Layers icon > 'Deep stars (Tycho-2 / Gaia tier)' > Install > read the HYG card, then scroll to the Deep-Star Tier card.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/providers/deep_star_providers.dart:25`

**Proposed fix:** One constant for HYG's completeness (~9.0), used by the Layers subtitle, the Catalog Settings depth chip and the deep-star merge seam.

### Duplicate happily creates two profiles with byte-identical names, while Import de-duplicates -- so the profile list can show rows nothing can tell apart

`defect` · settings (remote-access, updates, about, help, replay-debug, backup, equipment-profiles, catalogs, rig-catalogs) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Duplicating the same profile twice and accepting the offered name both times produced ids 4 and 5, both named 'Local Work Do Not Lose (Copy)'. The list renders two identical rows (same title, same 'No description', same icon) with nothing to distinguish them, and the status-bar profile chip shows only a name, so once one of them is active you cannot tell which. The importer, by contrast, does resolve collisions -- importing a profile named 'Audit Rig Alpha' over an existing one produced 'Audit Rig Alpha (1)'. Two code paths, two different rules.

**Expected:** One rule: reject or auto-suffix a colliding name everywhere. Duplicate should offer '(Copy 2)' the second time, exactly as Import offers '(1)'.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > overflow > Duplicate > Duplicate (accept the pre-filled name). Repeat. sqlite3 'select id,name from equipment_profiles' -> two rows with the same name.

**Where:** `the duplicate handler's default name generation vs the importer's collision resolution`

**Proposed fix:** Route both through one uniqueName(base) helper, and add a UNIQUE index on equipment_profiles.name if names are meant to be identifiers.

### The Alt/Az view toggle throws away where you were looking and jumps the chart to the zenith

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With the chart centred on M57 at a 2.0 deg imaging FOV (Center RA 18h53m35s, Center Dec +33d 01'), clicking the command bar's second icon - tooltip 'Alt/Az view - tap for Equatorial' - immediately recentred the chart on RA 4h42m52s / Dec +39d 59'. At the LST shown in the status bar (04:43) that is exactly the zenith: hour angle 0, declination equal to the site latitude of 40 deg. The framing on M57 was gone, at 2 deg FOV, with no undo. Clicking the toggle back to Equatorial restored RA 18h53m35s / +33d 01', and toggling to Alt/Az a second time again landed on the zenith (RA 4h43m52s / +39d 59' at LST 04:44), so it is deterministic, not a transient.

**Expected:** Changing the coordinate frame is a change of grid, not a change of target. The same patch of sky should stay under the cursor, just with the horizon-aligned grid and the up-vector rotated - that is what Stellarium and SkySafari do. If the app genuinely cannot express the current centre in the alt/az frame (e.g. the target is below the horizon) it should say so rather than silently slewing the view.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > centre on any object and zoom to a narrow FOV > click the second command bar icon ('Alt/Az view'). Read Center RA / Center Dec in the bottom bar and compare with the LST in the status bar and the site latitude.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/widgets/redesign/command_bar.dart`

**Proposed fix:** On frame change, convert the current centre RA/Dec to alt/az (and back) instead of resetting the centre to the zenith.

### PHD2 Guiding settings has no way to test the connection -- the one integration you cannot verify

`missing` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P1, corrected by verifier)_

There is no Connect/Test button, no live PHD2 connection state, no PHD2 version readout, no PHD2 profile selector, no 'launch PHD2 if not running', and no 'connect equipment in PHD2 on start'. Every other integration in this app can be verified from its settings page -- SMTP/Discord/Pushover/Telegram/generic-webhook all have 'Send test', cloud sync has 'Test Connection', both plate solvers have 'Verify', and the plate solving page even shows 'Auto-detect -- not found' inline. PHD2 is the most failure-prone dependency in the whole stack and is the only one with no verification affordance.

**Expected:** A 'Test connection' button that opens the PHD2 event socket and reports version + current profile + connected state, and an inline auto-detect status for the executable path matching the Plate Solving page's 'Auto-detect -- not found' pattern. NINA shows PHD2's state and version inline on its guider page.

**Proposed fix:** Add a Test row that calls the existing PHD2 client's connect/get-version RPC against the configured host:port and renders success (version, active profile) or the socket error, plus an auto-detect status subtitle on the executable path row reusing the plate-solver detection pattern.

### Alt/az readouts ignore precession - stated positions are up to 1.4 degrees off

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Clicking Capella at 18:38:16Z from 40N/105W/1650m the popup reported Alt 56.5, Az 295.7. Computing alt/az from the same J2000 RA/Dec with no precession gives 56.462/295.700 - an exact match; precessing to the equinox of date gives 56.804/295.681. Pollux at 18:39:17Z: app 76.0/214.0; J2000-direct 75.964/214.167; precessed 76.080/212.716 (1.45 deg of azimuth). So the horizontal transform uses J2000 coordinates unrotated, with no nutation, aberration or refraction either.

**Expected:** Apply precession (and ideally refraction) before the horizontal transform. 26 years from J2000 is already ~22 arcmin of position, and the azimuth error near the zenith reaches 1.4 deg, which is enough to mis-call a meridian-flip or an obstruction check.

**Repro:** Set the site to 40N 105W, open the planetarium at a known UTC, click Capella and Pollux, read 'Current Position'.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium (horizontal transform)`

**Proposed fix:** Precess catalogue J2000 coordinates to the observation epoch before computing hour angle.

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED**

Capella is V=0.08; the neighbouring Pollux popup correctly shows the proper name 'Pollux', the chip 'HIP37826' and 'mag 1.2' (true 1.14). So for this star the popup contradicts the chart label AND reports a magnitude ~0.9 mag too faint.

**Expected:** The popup heading should use the same proper name the chart uses, and the magnitude should match the catalogue value used to size the star.

**Repro:** Plan Tonight > Planetarium > reset to zenith at ~18:38Z from 40N/105W > click the bright star labelled Capella.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/planetarium/widgets/object_info_popup.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Resolve the proper name (and its magnitude) from the same record the renderer labels from.

Typing 'M31' shows a brief spinner and then nothing: no results list, no message, RA/Dec stay empty. Pressing Enter does nothing. The cause is that the scratch profile's `targets` table has 0 rows (catalogs not downloaded yet) and the widget has no empty-results branch.
