### 'Best Targets Tonight' transit times are computed for the night after tomorrow - every time is ~8 minutes wrong

`defect` · planetarium + your_sky + constellation · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** On 2026-08-02 at 40N/105W the Tonight sidebar listed IC311 with transit 09:24 and NGC6685 with transit 00:48. Selecting each object shows its coordinates in the bottom bar: IC311 = RA 3h16m46s Dec +40d00', NGC6685 = RA 18h39m58s Dec +39d58'. The true meridian crossings for those coordinates at lon -105 are 09:32 / 00:57 tonight (2026-08-02) and 09:28 / 00:53 the next night. The app's numbers match neither - they match 2026-08-04 (09:24 / 00:49), i.e. two sidereal days of drift. Root cause: bestTargetsProvider passes imagingTime = twilight.astronomicalDusk as the 'date', and at this site astronomical dusk falls at 00:00-00:02 LOCAL, so its calendar day is already tomorrow; calculateObjectVisibility then builds its search window as DateTime(date.year, date.month, date.day, 12) + 24h, i.e. noon TOMORROW to noon the day after. Whether the list slips a day therefore depends on whether dusk lands before or after local midnight, which flips with longitude-within-timezone, DST and season - so the same code is right in winter and a day out in summer. The app also contradicts itself on the same screen: with NGC6685 selected, the sidebar's Info tab reports 'Transit 00:52' for the very object whose Best-Targets card two tabs away says 00:48.

**Expected:** The visibility window for 'tonight' should start at the local noon preceding the dusk being planned for (or simply take a night-start date, not a dusk timestamp). Transit for IC311 tonight is 09:32, not 09:24.

**Repro:** Settings > Location > lat 40, lon -105 > Plan Tonight > Planetarium > open the right sidebar > Tonight tab > scroll to 'Best Targets Tonight'. Note the transit time of a row, then click the row and read 'Center RA/Dec' in the bottom bar; compute the meridian crossing for that RA at that longitude.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_planetarium/lib/src/providers/planetarium_providers/mosaic_targets.dart:146`

**Proposed fix:** Pass currentDate (the night's date) rather than the dusk instant, or have calculateObjectVisibility anchor its window on the noon BEFORE the supplied instant (subtract 12h before truncating to the day).

### The stack result's 'Avg HFR' statistic never renders because the service hard-codes avgHfr: null, even though every selected sub carries an HFR

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** stack_result_screen.dart:522 renders an 'Avg HFR ... px' row only `if (result.avgHfr != null)`, and stack_and_share_service.dart:425 persists `avgHfr: null` unconditionally. So the row is dead: two real Stack & Share runs in this session wrote stacked_results rows 2 and 3 with avg_hfr NULL, while every one of their source frames has hfr = 2.4 in captured_images. The only row in the table with an avg_hfr is one I seeded by hand. The result panel therefore shows Integration / Frames stacked / Rejected / Avg residual / Filter / Dimensions and silently omits the one number an imager actually judges a stack by.

**Expected:** Average the HFR of the frames that were integrated (they are already in hand as StackedFrameSelection rows) and persist it, so the panel shows 'Avg HFR 2.40 px'.

**Repro:** 1. Run Stack & Share to completion. 2. The result panel has no Avg HFR row. 3. sqlite3 skotiq.db 'select id,frames_stacked,avg_hfr from stacked_results' -> 2|7|(null), 3|8|(null); 'select distinct hfr from captured_images where session_id=3' -> 2.4.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/stack_and_share_service.dart:425; consumed at packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/stack_result/stack_result_screen.dart:522`

**Proposed fix:** Compute the mean HFR over the frames whose add succeeded and pass it in place of the null.

### The standalone /science route can never build ScienceScreen -- its redirect always fires, so the screen class and its Scaffold are unreachable code

`defect` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** app_router.dart declares the /science GoRoute with BOTH a `redirect` that unconditionally returns '/analytics?tab=science...' and a `pageBuilder` that constructs ScienceScreen. In go_router a non-null redirect short-circuits the route, so the pageBuilder never runs. ScienceScreen is constructed in exactly one place in the whole repo -- that dead pageBuilder -- so the class, its Scaffold and its initialTabQuery plumbing ship but can never appear. Driving the app confirms it: every route into science lands on the Analytics shell with the Science tab selected, never on a standalone science screen.

**Expected:** Delete the dead pageBuilder (and ScienceScreen with it) or drop the redirect if a standalone screen is actually wanted. Leaving both is a trap: a future reader editing ScienceScreen will change nothing that ships.

**Repro:** 1. Reach science by every available path -- Dashboard cockpit narrator, imaging science HUD, sequencer light-curve panel, transients screen. 2. All land on Analytics with the Science tab active (header reads 'Analytics', sub-tabs Science / First Light / Observing Alerts). 3. grep -rn 'ScienceScreen' packages apps --include='*.dart' -> only the definition and app_router.dart:232.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/router/app_router.dart:222-236; packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/science/science_screen.dart:246-258 ScienceScreen`

**Proposed fix:** Remove the pageBuilder from the /science route and delete ScienceScreen, keeping ScienceWorkspaceView as the single implementation.

### Dead/duplicated settings keys persist and are exported while no UI reflects them

`defect` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **CONFIRMED**

**What happens:** Two cases found by diffing the UI against the scratch DB. (1) app_settings.auto_save_sequences = 'true' (its shipped default) coexists with autosave.sequence_enabled = 'false', which is what the Files & Storage toggle showed and what AutoSaveService actually obeys. No UI surfaces auto_save_sequences, but it is written into settings snapshots/exports and served through the app-settings model, so a remote/web surface reading it reports sequence auto-save ON while the desktop has it OFF. (2) app_settings.plate_solver = 'ASTAP' coexists with the Plate Solving page's 'Active solver' radio (Auto-fallback), which is backed by Rust platesolver.json via plateSolverPreferenceProvider. Confusingly the Solve Parameters on that same page DO write sqlite -- I typed 45 into Timeout and plate_solve_timeout went 60 -> 45 -- so one page writes two different stores.

**Expected:** One store per logical setting. Anything that rides export/backup/API should be the value the app actually obeys.

**Repro:** Fresh profile. Settings > Files & Storage > AUTO-SAVE & BACKUPS: 'Enable sequence auto-save' reads OFF. Then `sqlite3 <profile>/data/skotiq.db "select key,value from app_settings where key like '%auto_save%' or key like 'autosave%'"`. Same for plate_solver vs the Active solver radio.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/database/database/default_settings.dart:12 and :23; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/settings_sections/app_settings_stored_snapshot_mapping.dart:515 and :551; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/auto_save_service.dart:819; packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/providers/plate_solver_provider.dart:18-25`

**Proposed fix:** Either delete auto_save_sequences / plate_solver from default_settings.dart and the snapshot mapping, or make the AppSettings getters read through to autosave.sequence_enabled and the Rust platesolver preference so the exported value is the obeyed value.

(It is deleted when a new download starts -- I watched it vanish the moment I pressed Download -- so the space is recovered only by attempting another download, which currently cannot succeed.)

There is no Restart button.

Both are the raw toString() of the thrown exception.

**Expected:** 'That file is not a Skotiq profile export' plus the file name. The exported JSON also carries no format marker or version field at all (I exported one and read it: it is a bare map of profile fields), so the importer has nothing to check against and cannot warn about profiles exported by a newer build.

**Repro:** Settings > Equipment Profiles > import icon > pick any .txt. Then pick a JSON that is not a profile.

**Proposed fix:** Add {"format":"skotiq.equipment-profile","version":1} to the export and validate it on import; map failures to plain-language messages.

### Reference sky brightness range 14-24 mag/arcsec2 admits values no real sky can reach, which pin the feature at its clamp forever

**What happens:** The 'Reference sky brightness' input clamps to [14, 24]. The darkest natural sky is ~22.0 mag/arcsec2. I typed 99 and it silently became 24.00 with no message. At ref = 24 against a typical 21.5 sky the ratio is 10^(2.5/2.5) = 10x, so every exposure is driven to the 'Longest exposure' clamp for the whole session; at ref = 14 every exposure sits on the 'Shortest exposure' clamp.

### Session History's 'Refresh history' toolbar action is enabled while disconnected and produces no perceptible change, unlike every other dead action in the app

`ux` · mobile operation - companion dashboard shell, mount/sequencer/log/settings tabs, session picker and session replay, driven disconnected on the ns_test_api35 emulator · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** With no server paired, Session History correctly shows 'Connect to a server / Session replay requires the phone to be paired with a Skotiq server.' But the AppBar's 'Refresh history' button stays enabled. Tapping it runs _refresh -> _loadMore, which immediately hits `if (backend is! NetworkBackend)` and re-sets the same _LocalBackendNotSupported error, re-rendering an identical screen. No spinner, no SnackBar, no change of any kind. I tapped it and both the a11y tree and the screen were byte-identical before and after. The app disables dead controls correctly everywhere else on this dashboard - the Sequencer's Start and Stop both dump as [DISABLED], and the Log tab's 'Clear log' dumps as [DISABLED] - so this one enabled-but-inert action is inconsistent with the app's own established convention.

**Expected:** Disable the refresh action when the backend is not a NetworkBackend, exactly as Start/Stop/Clear log already do, so `tree` reports [DISABLED] and a gloved 2am tap does not go unanswered.

**Repro:** 1. Companion UI enabled; Skip Connection. 2. AppBar overflow 'More' -> 'Session history / replay'. 3. `drive_android.py tree` and note the screen. 4. `drive_android.py tap 'Refresh history'`. 5. `drive_android.py tree` again.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/screens/replay/session_picker_screen.dart:182-183 (action), 60-71 (_loadMore early return)`

**Proposed fix:** `onPressed: (_loading || ref.watch(backendProvider) is! NetworkBackend) ? null : () => unawaited(_refresh())`.

### An unrecognised transient-kind token is presented to the user as a definite 'Dipole' artefact classification, which also silently blocks submission

`ux` · science / session_review / stack_result / first_light · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** TransientKind.fromWire falls back to TransientKind.dipole for any token it does not recognise. The candidate card then renders a 'Dipole' chip -- a specific, factual claim about the residual's morphology -- and Submit refuses with 'Dipole artefacts are not real transients - they cannot be submitted.' I hit this with a row whose kind was 'new_source' instead of the wire token 'newSource': the card asserted the detection was a dipole artefact and would not let it be reported. The fallback is deliberate and documented ('the least-exciting, artefact-leaning class'), but the UI presents the fallback as a measurement.

**Expected:** Keep the conservative gating, but label it honestly: an unknown token should render as 'Unclassified' and the refusal message should read 'This detection has an unrecognised classification and cannot be submitted', not assert a dipole morphology the differencing code never reported.

**Repro:** 1. Insert a transient_detections row with kind='new_source'. 2. Analytics > Science > First Light. 3. The card shows a 'Dipole' chip; pressing Submit toasts 'Dipole artefacts are not real transients - they cannot be submitted.' 4. Change kind to 'newSource' and restart -> the same row now reads 'New source' and Submit opens the dialog.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/services/transients/transient_candidate.dart:45 fromWire; rendered at packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/first_light/widgets/transient_candidate_card.dart:383-405`

**Proposed fix:** Add an `unknown` member to TransientKind, make fromWire return it, label it 'Unclassified', and gate submission on `kind == newSource || kind == pointBrightening || kind == movingStreak` rather than on `!= dipole`.

### Weather Safety carries three hardware thresholds that are inert without a sensor, and no debounce/hysteresis anywhere

`ux` · settings (sequencer, science, phd2-guiding, plate-solving, weather-safety, notifications + all transports, integrations/plugins, annotations, observation log, auto-save, cloud-sync) · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The Hardware Thresholds section offers Max humidity 90%, Max wind speed 30 km/h and Max cloud cover 80% with subtitles like 'Pause when sensor humidity exceeds this value'. On a rig with no weather device -- the exact rig the fail-closed default punishes -- all three are inert, and the page never says so. Separately, the page has no debounce, no 'N consecutive unsafe readings', and no 'wait N minutes after conditions clear before resuming'; the only timing controls are radar-alert distance (30 km) and lead time (15 min), which are about the cloud-radar API rather than the hardware gate. A single spurious humidity spike therefore trips the whole gate.

**Expected:** Grey out or annotate the threshold rows when no weather device is connected, and add an unsafe-debounce (consecutive readings or seconds) plus a resume-delay. NINA and SGP both expose these; without them the thresholds are all-or-nothing.

**Repro:** Settings > AUTOMATION & SAFETY > Weather Safety, full page.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/weather_safety_settings.dart:170-268`

**Proposed fix:** Add a connected-source guard on the Hardware Thresholds section and add unsafeDebounceSeconds / resumeDelayMinutes to WeatherSettings with rows on this page.

**What happens:** Under 'Broadcast mode' the helper reads 'Broadcast only keeps memory clean; Recor...' and under 'Stack combine method' it reads 'Average is fastest; Median+Rej and Sigma r...'. These sentences exist precisely to help the user choose between the dropdown options, and the half that names the alternative is cut off with no tooltip or expansion.

**Expected:** Wrap to as many lines as needed - the panel scrolls anyway.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > add 'Live Stacking' > read the two helper lines in Properties.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/live_stacking_properties.dart`

**Proposed fix:** Remove maxLines/TextOverflow.ellipsis from the helper Text widgets.

### Validation badges on the sequence header are not clickable, so the only way to read '1 error' is to press Start

`ux` · sequencer · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** A newly created empty sequence immediately displays a red '1' and an amber '1' in the header. Clicking either does nothing (they are plain containers). There is no issues panel in the Builder. The only way to learn what they are is to press Start and read the Pre-Flight dialog - which for the empty case turns out to be 'Empty Sequence' and 'No Exposures', i.e. 'you have not built anything yet', shown as a red error on a screen the user just opened.

**Expected:** Clicking the badge opens the same issue list the Pre-Flight dialog shows. And 'you just created an empty sequence' should be an info/hint, not a red error.

**Repro:** Sequencer > Builder > toolbar 'New Sequence'. Click the red '1' badge: nothing. Press Start to discover what it means.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/sequencer/widgets/sequence_tree/support_widgets.dart:595-676 (_ValidationBadges / _MiniCountBadge have no gesture handler)`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the badges in an InkWell that opens the validation issue list read-only.

### Two onboarding prompts are presented at the same time on the first dashboard

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** After 'Skip onboarding' the dashboard renders with the modal 'Welcome to Skotiq - Step 1 of 7' tour card (Skip - I know what I'm doing / Show me around) AND, simultaneously, a 'Dashboard Tour - Learn about the dashboard controls and status displays' card with its own Maybe Later / Start Tour buttons. Both are live in the accessibility tree at once, so a screen reader reads two competing tour offers, and sighted users see a dimmed second offer behind the first.

**Expected:** One onboarding prompt at a time. The dashboard tour card should be suppressed while the app-level tour overlay is up (and ideally only offered after it is dismissed).

**Repro:** Skip Connection (View UI Only) -> Skip onboarding -> look at the dashboard.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/screens/dashboard/mobile_dashboard_screen.dart + packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/onboarding/onboarding_overlay.dart`

### The onboarding tour announces keyboard shortcuts that do not exist on a phone

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The tour overlay's accessibility label on Android reads 'Skotiq onboarding tour, step 1 of 7, Press Enter for next step, Backspace for previous, Escape to skip'. A phone has no Enter, Backspace or Escape key; a TalkBack user is given three instructions none of which they can follow, and the actual gesture (activate the on-screen buttons) is never mentioned.

**Expected:** Platform-conditional label: keep the key hints on desktop, and on touch platforms announce the buttons ('double-tap Show me around to continue, Skip to dismiss').

**Repro:** Skip Connection (View UI Only) -> Skip onboarding -> dump the accessibility tree.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/onboarding/onboarding_overlay.dart (Semantics label)`

### 'Retry camera' is a dead control once the permission is permanently denied

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** After two denials Android stops showing the permission dialog. 'Retry camera' stays enabled; tapping it produces no dialog, no snackbar and no text change - the screen is identical before and after. The app knows the state, because this is exactly when it adds the 'Open settings' button, yet it keeps offering a button that cannot succeed.

**Expected:** Once 'Open settings' has appeared, either disable 'Retry camera' with a reason or change it to say 'Camera access is blocked in Android settings - use Open settings'. A control that cannot do anything must not look identical to one that can.

**Repro:** Scan QR -> Deny -> Retry camera -> Deny -> ('Open settings' appears) -> tap 'Retry camera'.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/screens/qr_scanner_screen.dart (permission-denied body)`

### Transport segmented control exposes no selected state to accessibility

`defect` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The HTTP / HTTPS transport selector renders HTTP as the selected chip, but in the accessibility tree only the UNSELECTED option ('HTTPS') is a node at all, and it reports selected=false, checked=false. The currently selected value is a plain non-interactive View. A screen-reader user cannot tell which transport is active, and cannot discover that the visible highlighted chip is the current choice.

**Expected:** Both segments should be semantics nodes with inSelectedState / selected true-false, as the design system's other toggle rows do (the sheet's own 'Trust self-signed relay TLS' checkbox correctly reports off).

**Repro:** Connect over Tailscale -> dump the view hierarchy and inspect the Transport row.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/widgets/tailscale_setup_sheet.dart (Transport segmented control)`

### After discovery has already failed, the biggest button on screen re-runs the thing that just failed

`ux` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The connection screen offers seven actions. Six are equally-weighted low-contrast text buttons (Enter IP 209x126px, Scan QR 216x126px, Connect over Tailscale, Connect via Relay, Saved servers, Skip Connection) and exactly one is a filled primary button: 'Search for Server' (996x131px), which repeats the automatic discovery that ran on launch and failed 4 seconds ago. The action with the highest success probability for someone standing next to their rig - Scan QR, because the desktop puts a QR on screen when you start pairing - is a small text button, and the error card that lists 'Scanning the QR code from the desktop' as the first thing to try offers no button to do it.

**Expected:** Once discovery has failed at least once, promote 'Scan QR' to the primary filled button and demote 'Search for Server' to a text action ('Scan again'). Better still, make the three bullets in the failure card tappable so 'Scanning the QR code from the desktop' opens the scanner directly - that removes a full step from the most likely recovery path.

**Repro:** Launch with no appliance, wait for the failure card, count the actions and their weights.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/main.dart (connection screen action list)`

### The Tailscale sheet tells the user to go install an app but will not take them there, and never checks whether it is already installed

`ux` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** Step 1 of the sheet reads 'Install Tailscale on this phone - Get it from the App Store / Play Store and sign in with the same account as your observatory PC.' It is plain text: nothing is tappable, so the user must leave Skotiq, find the store, search for Tailscale, install, sign in, and come back and retype everything (which the sheet then discards on the next failure). The sheet also never checks whether Tailscale is present or up on this phone, even though that is trivially detectable on Android and is the single most common reason the connect will fail.

**Expected:** On a phone this should be a button that deep-links to the Play Store listing (market://details?id=com.tailscale.ipn), and step 1 should collapse to a green tick when the package is already installed. Detecting 'Tailscale is not installed' before the connect attempt turns a mystery timeout into a one-line answer.

**Repro:** Connection screen -> Connect over Tailscale -> read step 1; nothing in it is a control.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/widgets/tailscale_setup_sheet.dart (step list)`

### Manual entry asks for an 'Access Token' that a first-time user cannot have, and never mentions the port it will use

`ux` · mobile first run and connection - main.dart connection screen, mobile_discovery_ops, qr_scanner_screen, saved_servers_screen, tailscale_setup_sheet, first_run_setup_screen, checkpoint_resume_dialog · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** The Enter-IP form has exactly two fields: 'Server address' and 'Access Token' (with a show/hide eye). A first-run user has no access token - the real first-connect credential is the pairing CODE, which the app asks for afterwards in its own dialog. There is no helper text saying the token is optional, and no hint that the address may include a port or that 8080 is assumed; the user only learns the port from the failure message afterwards ('Cannot reach http://192.0.2.77:8080/api/info').

**Expected:** Label it 'Access token (optional - leave blank to pair with a code)', exactly the phrasing the Tailscale sheet already uses for the same field ('Leave blank to pair with a code'), and give the address field the hint '192.168.1.20 or 192.168.1.20:8080 (default 8080)'.

**Repro:** Connection screen -> Enter IP -> inspect the two fields.

**Where:** `apps/mobile/lib/main.dart (manual entry form)`

### Night Narrator ticker produced zero insights across a 65-minute session with 12 frames, an autofocus run and a cooldown

`ux` · imaging, framing, guiding · **not yet verified**

**What happens:** NarratorTicker is mounted unconditionally at the top-right of the preview (live_preview_area.dart:629). Over a 65-minute driven session - 12 captures across two filters, a 9-point autofocus that moved best HFR from 2.46 to 2.10, a cooldown from +20 C to -10 C, a filter change, an aborted exposure - the ticker never rendered a single line in any of the ~40 Imaging screenshots. The Transient Alerts bell was likewise empty ('No active alerts'). Either the detectors need a sequencer run to fire, or the feature is invisible on the surface where it is advertised.

**Expected:** At minimum, something like 'Cooling reached -10.0 C' or 'Autofocus improved HFR 2.46 -> 2.10' should surface, since those are exactly the events the narrator is documented to detect.

**Repro:** 1. Imaging screen. 2. Run autofocus, cool the camera, change filter, take frames. 3. Watch the top-right of the preview.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/narrator_ticker.dart, mounted at packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/imaging/widgets/live_preview_area.dart:629`

**Proposed fix:** Confirm whether the narrator requires an active sequencer session; if so, either wire manual-capture events into it or do not mount the ticker on a surface where it can never speak.

### Sequencer telemetry strip reported 'Mount: Tracking' for 2.5 minutes after the mount had been parked

`defect` · sequencer (the 37 seq-node:* instruction types) · **UNCLEAR** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Park Mount succeeded at 16:31:53 ('Parking mount sim_mount_1', status Success). At 16:34:00 the sequencer's telemetry strip still read 'Mount: Tracking' (green), while the Equipment screen at 16:35:31 read 'Parked' with Dec +90:00:00 and offered an Unpark button. Two surfaces of the same app disagreed about whether the mount was tracking, during a run.

**Expected:** The telemetry strip must reflect the same mount state as the Equipment card within a poll period.

## P4

### Projects header "Active This Month" counts targets, not sessions, and resets on the 1st

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P3, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With three imaging sessions on Jul 25/28/30 and today Aug 1, the Projects header reads "0 Active This Month". Even inside the month it can never exceed the number of targets: the code counts PROJECTS whose lastSessionAt is after the 1st (the variable is even named sessionsThisMonth), so twenty nights on one target still shows 1.

**Expected:** Either a session count over a rolling 30 nights (astronomers do not care about calendar-month boundaries; an observing run straddles them), or a label that says what it counts ("Targets active this month").

**Repro:** Analytics > Projects with sessions dated Jul 25/28/30 and system date Aug 1.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/project_tracking_panel.dart:363-370, 414-418`

**Proposed fix:** Count sessions in the last 30 days: sessions.where((s)=>s.startTime.isAfter(now.subtract(Duration(days:30)))).length, and relabel "Nights (30d)".

### Four widgets are exported from the app barrel but have zero call sites - dead UI shipping in the binary

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P3, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Repo-wide search (packages/ + apps/, excluding each widget's own file) finds no instantiation of ObjectInfoPanel (0 references anywhere, including tests), TourSelectionSheet (0 references to TourSelectionSheet.show), SequenceControls (referenced only by test/widgets/run_controls_paused_skip_test.dart) or VariablePickerField/VariablePickerButton (VariablePickerButton appears only as the suffixIcon of VariablePickerField in the same file; VariablePickerField has no callers outside test/widgets/variable_picker_test.dart). All four are nonetheless exported from packages/skotiq_app/lib/skotiq_app.dart (lines 37, 39, 46, and the sequence barrel).

**Expected:** Either wire them up or delete them. Two of them carry doc comments that assert they are in use and are therefore actively misleading: tour_selection_sheet.dart:7-8 says 'Used from Settings and contextual prompts' (Settings > Help & Tutorials builds its own inline 'Tutorial Tours' list and never calls it) and variable_picker.dart advertises a picker no sequencer field offers - Settings > Sequencer only mentions it in a comment. A sequencer that stores ${TARGET}/${FILTER}-style variables but exposes no picker is a real capability gap, not just dead code.

**Repro:** grep -rn 'ObjectInfoPanel|TourSelectionSheet|SequenceControls|VariablePickerField' --include='*.dart' packages apps, excluding each widget's own file. Also confirmed live: the Sequencer and Settings > Sequencer screens have no variable-insert control, and Settings > Help & Tutorials has no tour bottom sheet (its Tutorial Tours rows are inline list tiles).

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/object_info_panel.dart, widgets/tour_selection_sheet.dart, widgets/sequence_controls.dart, widgets/sequence/variable_picker.dart; barrel exports in packages/skotiq_app/lib/skotiq_app.dart:37,39,46.`

**Proposed fix:** Delete object_info_panel.dart and sequence_controls.dart (both superseded - the planetarium's object_details_panel and the sequencer's own run controls). For variable_picker, wire VariablePickerField into the sequencer's filename/target text fields, which is what it was written for. For tour_selection_sheet, either call it from Settings > Help & Tutorials or drop it and fix the doc comment.

### 'Disconnect All' stays enabled and silently no-ops when nothing is connected

`defect` · shared-widgets · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P3, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** With 0 devices connected the equipment-status popup still offers a fully enabled 'Disconnect All' item. Clicking it closes the menu and does nothing — no toast, no log line, no state change.

**Expected:** Disable (grey) 'Disconnect All' when connectedCount == 0, like every other dead action in this build is greyed.

**Repro:** Fresh profile, nothing connected -> click the equipment chip in the status bar -> 'Disconnect All'. Watch the tree and drive_linux.py log: no change, no message.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/equipment_status_indicator.dart (_buildDropdownItems)`

**Proposed fix:** Gate the Disconnect All PopupMenuItem's enabled flag on connectedCount > 0.

### Image Grading: eccentricity max can be set to 1.00, which is a threshold the pipeline can never exceed - the check becomes inert with no warning

`defect` · settings (imaging / calibration / calibration-library / dark-library / captured-images / image-grading / adaptive-exposure / adaptive-conditions / preflight / autofocus / focus model) · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P3, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** I typed 5 into 'Eccentricity max'; it clamped to 1.00 and persisted image_grading_eccentricity_threshold=1.0. Frame eccentricity is computed as sqrt(1 - lambda2/lambda1), which is strictly < 1.0 for any star with a finite minor axis, and the rule is 'reject if eccentricity exceeds this value' - so 1.00 can never fire. The UI gives no indication that the check is now dead. The same shape applies at the other end of several ranges: HFR absolute min 0.1 px (below any achievable HFR -> rejects every frame), HFR absolute max 50 px (never fires), star-count min max 100000 (rejects every frame on any realistic sensor).

**Expected:** Clamp eccentricity to something the detector can actually exceed (e.g. 0.99), or annotate the field when the value makes the check inert ('at 1.00 this check never fires'). Same for the HFR/star-count extremes.

**Repro:** Settings > Imaging > Image Grading: enable the master toggle, click 'Eccentricity max', Ctrl+A, type 5, Enter -> field shows 1.00, DB shows 1.0.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/settings/widgets/image_grading_settings.dart:196-198 (min 0.0 / max 1.0); native/skotiq_native/imaging/src/difference_image.rs:865-876 (moment_eccentricity returns < 1.0)`

**Proposed fix:** Cap at 0.99 and surface an inline note when a threshold is outside the range the pipeline can produce.

### Image grader's list of frames it is about to reject is clipped behind the dialog footer

`defect` · analytics (Session, History, Projects, Equipment Stats, Science, Diagnostics) + science screen · **CONFIRMED** _(reported P2, corrected by verifier)_

**What happens:** Analytics > Science > Field Quality > 'Grade 2 frames'. After lowering Max guiding RMS to 0.24" the panel correctly reports 'Will reject 2 / Will keep 0' and builds a per-frame list, but only the first row's filename ('m13_0.fits') is visible and it is cut in half by the Cancel / 'Reject 2 frames' action bar. The second frame and both per-frame reasons ('Auto-grade: Guiding 0.60" > 0.24"') exist in the widget tree but cannot be seen or scrolled to.

**Expected:** The confirmation list is the whole point of a destructive-looking action - the user must be able to read which frames will be rejected and why. Either scroll the list inside a bounded region with a visible scrollbar, or size the dialog to the content.

**Repro:** Analytics > Science on a session with frames > 'Grade N frames' > drag Max guiding RMS below the frames' RMS so at least one frame fails > look at the list above the footer.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/analytics/widgets/image_grader_dialog.dart (the failing-frame list is laid out in the dialog body with no Expanded/scroll container above the actions row)`

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the failing-frame list in an Expanded + Scrollbar(thumbVisibility: true) inside the dialog's constrained body so it scrolls independently of the footer.


### Connection stale banner can never render on a desktop remote client — only the mobile app drives its provider

`defect` · remote-appliance · **CONFIRMED** _(found during the paired-appliance sweep, 2026-09-12)_

**What happens:** Paired a desktop client to a headless appliance (`--remote-host`, admin scope) and killed the appliance mid-session to force a WebSocket drop. The connection icon went red and the client cycled reconnects, but `ConnectionStaleBanner` — the "Reconnecting to server… session controls may be stale" strip with a Retry button — never rendered at any point in the 30 s window.

**Expected:** The banner is mounted unconditionally in the desktop shell (`app_shell.dart:460`) and the backend supports `reconnectNow()`, so a desktop remote client inside its reconnect grace window should see it the same way the mobile app does.

**Root cause:** `connectionStaleProvider` (`connection_stale_banner.dart:11`) is written **only** by `apps/mobile/lib/main_parts/mobile_connection_state.dart:278,288`. Nothing in the desktop remote-client path ever sets it, so on desktop the widget is mounted but permanently `false` — dead code — and a desktop remote operator gets no stale-connection warning and no Retry control during a dropout.

**Where:** `packages/skotiq_app/lib/widgets/connection_stale_banner.dart:11` (provider); `apps/mobile/lib/main_parts/mobile_connection_state.dart:278,288` (only writers); `packages/skotiq_app/lib/screens/shell/app_shell.dart:460` (desktop mount point); `packages/skotiq_core/lib/src/backend/network_backend.dart` (`_reconnectAttempt`/`reconnectNow` — the shared state machine the desktop could map).

**Proposed fix:** Either wire the desktop connection-state machine to `connectionStaleProvider` (the desktop NetworkBackend already tracks reconnect attempts and exposes `reconnectNow()` for the Retry button), or scope the mount to the mobile shell so the desktop does not carry a permanently-inert widget. Product call which side is intended — but today the desktop mount is dead either way.
